# miosa command reference

Generated by `miosa __docs`; do not edit by hand.
Regenerate with `make docs`.

## Global flags

- `--api-key string` - API key (overrides MIOSA_API_KEY and config).
- `--api-url string` - API base URL (overrides MIOSA_BASE_URL and config).
- `--bill-to string` - Organization a new machine bills: id, slug or name (env MIOSA_BILL_TO).
- `--json` - Print machine-readable JSON (same as --output json).
- `--no-update` - Skip the automatic update for this run, when it is on (env: MIOSA_NO_UPDATE=1).
- `--no-update-notice` - Silence the new-version notice (env: MIOSA_NO_UPDATE_NOTICE=1).
- `--org string` - Organization to work in: id, slug or name; refused if this key does not act in it (env MIOSA_ORG).
- `-o, --output string` - Output format: text or json. (default `text`)
- `--profile string` - Config profile to use (env: MIOSA_PROFILE).
- `-q, --quiet` - Suppress informational output.
- `--retries int` - Retries on 429 and transient 5xx errors (default 3, env: MIOSA_RETRIES). (default `-1`)
- `--timeout int` - Request timeout in seconds (0 = no timeout); for exec also the command timeout (over 300 or 0 streams the output), for ssh 1-300. (default `60`)

### `miosa admin`

Platform administration (platform operators only).

```
miosa admin
```

Platform administration.

These commands operate on platform state, not on your own sandboxes, and they
require a platform operator API key.

#### `miosa admin slot-revocations`

Revoke a producer bake revision (bad-bake protection).

```
miosa admin slot-revocations
```

Manage the revoked producer bake revisions.

A revocation is the supported replacement for a manual sweep after a bad bake.
Every compute host refuses to acquire or warm-claim a slot whose manifest
names a revoked revision, and the reaper retires the idle slots cut from it.
A revocation reaches running hosts within about a minute, without a restart.

Aliases: `slot-revocation`.

##### `miosa admin slot-revocations list`

List every revoked producer bake revision.

```
miosa admin slot-revocations list
```

List every revoked producer bake revision, newest first.

```
  miosa admin slot-revocations list
  miosa admin slot-revocations list --json | jq -r '.data[].revision'
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa admin slot-revocations revoke`

Revoke a producer bake revision.

```
miosa admin slot-revocations revoke <revision> [flags]
```

Revoke a producer bake revision.

Take the revision from the manifest of the bad bake (producer.vcs_revision), or
from the blocklist of the bake that produced it.
Revoking is immediate and
idempotent: revoking an already-revoked revision reports already_revoked rather
than failing.

```
  miosa admin slot-revocations revoke 1a2b3c4d --reason "mmds token absent"
```

Aliases: `add`.

Flags:

- `--reason string` - Why the revision is revoked (recorded in the audit trail).

### `miosa agent`

Define, version and run agents.

```
miosa agent
```

An agent is a saved, versioned definition: harness, model, instructions, the
machine it runs on, and what it may do.
Runs always use your own model
connections ('miosa connections add models'). <agent> is a name or an id.

```
  miosa agent new reviewer --harness claude-code --instructions-file review.md
  miosa agent publish reviewer --file config.yaml
  miosa agent diff reviewer 2 3
  miosa agent run reviewer "review PR 42" --sandbox my-box
  miosa agent harnesses
```

Aliases: `agents`.

#### `miosa agent approvals`

Approve or deny actions an agent is waiting on.

```
miosa agent approvals
```

An agent that hits a gated action (a deploy, a delete, an external write) pauses until someone approves it.

##### `miosa agent approvals approve`

Approve an action.

```
miosa agent approvals approve <id>
```

##### `miosa agent approvals deny`

Deny an action.

```
miosa agent approvals deny <id>
```

##### `miosa agent approvals list`

List pending approvals.

```
miosa agent approvals list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa agent authority`

What agents are allowed to do.

```
miosa agent authority
```

The catalog of gated actions, the rules for unattended runs, standing grants, and the receipts of what ran.

##### `miosa agent authority catalog`

List gated actions and their scope.

```
miosa agent authority catalog
```

##### `miosa agent authority grants`

List standing grants.

```
miosa agent authority grants
```

##### `miosa agent authority receipts`

List receipts of authorized actions.

```
miosa agent authority receipts [flags]
```

Every action an agent was allowed or refused leaves a receipt.
An approval
policy's automatic decisions have surface agent_policy; the harness gate's have agent_run.

```
  miosa agent authority receipts --workspace-id <id> --surface agent_policy
```

Flags:

- `--limit int` - At most this many, newest first (default 100, up to 500).
- `--surface string` - Only receipts from this surface (agent_policy, agent_run, ...).
- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace's receipts.

##### `miosa agent authority revoke`

Revoke a standing grant.

```
miosa agent authority revoke <grant> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa agent authority rules`

What unattended runs may do alone.

```
miosa agent authority rules
```

A rule set decides what a scheduled or triggered agent run may do on its own.
It applies to the organization, one workspace (--workspace-id) or one agent (--agent).

A rule is class[:resource].
Classes: read, internal_write, external_write,
destructive, publish.
The resource is what the call touches (storage, deployment,
a tool server, a shell program such as npm), or * for any.

```
  miosa agent authority rules create --name drafts --agent drafter \
      --allow internal_write:storage --ask external_write:email --budget-run 50
  miosa agent authority rules list --workspace-id <id>
  miosa agent authority rules rm drafts

```
A deny anywhere wins; otherwise the most specific scope decides, and in it ask beats
allow. destructive and publish are allowed only by a rule naming that class, and
external_write only by a rule naming its resource.
With no rule an unattended run
asks, and an ask nobody answers fails safe.
For time windows give the rules in
--file as JSON: {"rules": [{"effect": "allow", "class": "read", "resource": "*",
"window": {"days": [1,2,3,4,5], "from": "09:00", "to": "17:00", "tz": "UTC"}}]}.

Aliases: `rule`.

###### `miosa agent authority rules create`

Create a rule set.

```
miosa agent authority rules create [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--agent string` - Apply to this agent only (name or id).
- `--allow stringArray` - Rule class[:resource] the agent may do on its own (repeatable).
- `--ask stringArray` - Rule class[:resource] that needs a person's approval (repeatable).
- `--budget-day int` - Most credits the scope's runs may spend in a day.
- `--budget-run int` - Most credits one run may spend.
- `--deny stringArray` - Rule class[:resource] that is always refused (repeatable).
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--name string` - A label.
- `--workspace-id string` - Apply to this workspace (default: the whole organization).

###### `miosa agent authority rules get`

Show a rule set.

```
miosa agent authority rules get <rule-set>
```

Aliases: `show`.

###### `miosa agent authority rules list`

List rule sets.

```
miosa agent authority rules list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--agent string` - Only this agent's rule sets (name or id).
- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace's rule sets.

###### `miosa agent authority rules rm`

Disable a rule set (its receipts stay).

```
miosa agent authority rules rm <rule-set> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`, `disable`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa agent defaults`

Show the workspace's defaults for new agents (harness, model).

```
miosa agent defaults [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--workspace-id string` - Show this workspace's defaults.

#### `miosa agent diff`

Show what changed between two versions.

```
miosa agent diff <agent> <from> <to>
```

Compare two published versions key by key.
Only changed settings are listed.

#### `miosa agent duplicate`

Copy an agent with its current version.

```
miosa agent duplicate <agent> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--name string` - Name of the copy.

#### `miosa agent get`

Show an agent and its current version.

```
miosa agent get <agent>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa agent harness-versions`

Show installed and latest harness CLI versions.

```
miosa agent harness-versions
```

#### `miosa agent harnesses`

Show the available harnesses, models and what each can do.

```
miosa agent harnesses
```

Show every harness (Claude Code, Codex, OSA, custom) with its models, default model
and capability flags such as new-machine runs and computer use.
Needs the
agents:read scope.

##### `miosa agent harnesses default`

Make a harness the default for new agents and prompts.

```
miosa agent harnesses default <harness>
```

##### `miosa agent harnesses set`

Set a harness and the model new agents use with it.

```
miosa agent harnesses set <harness> --model <model> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--model string` - Model for the harness.

#### `miosa agent list`

List agents.

```
miosa agent list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many.
- `--status string` - Only active or archived agents.
- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace.

#### `miosa agent new`

Create an agent with its first version.

```
miosa agent new <name> [flags]
```

Create an agent.
The configuration comes from --file, from flags, or both (flags
win).
Unknown keys are rejected by the server with the offending path.

```
  miosa agent new reviewer --harness claude-code --model sonnet --instructions-file review.md
  miosa agent new builder -f builder.yaml --overdrive
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--description string` - What the agent is for.
- `-f, --file string` - Configuration as YAML or JSON (the version's `configuration` object).
- `--harness string` - Harness: claude-code, codex, osa or custom.
- `--instructions string` - Instructions text.
- `--instructions-file string` - Read the instructions from a file.
- `--model string` - Model.
- `--overdrive` - Run the harness without approval prompts (stored on the version).
- `--project-id string` - Project id.
- `--timeout-sec int` - Run time limit in seconds (execution.timeout_seconds).
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (default: $MIOSA_WORKSPACE, then the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa agent publish`

Publish a new immutable version.

```
miosa agent publish <agent> [flags]
```

Publish a new version of an agent from --file and/or flags.
The full configuration
is validated and fingerprinted; a version never changes after publication, so
a run can always prove what it ran.
Runs started before keep their version.

Flags:

- `-f, --file string` - Configuration as YAML or JSON (the version's `configuration` object).
- `--harness string` - Harness: claude-code, codex, osa or custom.
- `--instructions string` - Instructions text.
- `--instructions-file string` - Read the instructions from a file.
- `--model string` - Model.
- `--overdrive` - Run the harness without approval prompts (stored on the version).
- `--timeout-sec int` - Run time limit in seconds (execution.timeout_seconds).

#### `miosa agent rm`

Archive an agent (runs stop being accepted).

```
miosa agent rm <agent> [flags]
```

Aliases: `archive`, `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa agent run`

Run a saved agent (same as 'prompt --agent').

```
miosa agent run <agent> [text...] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--chat string` - Chat id.
- `--computer string` - Computer to run in.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key.
- `--new-machine` - Provision a machine for this run.
- `--no-follow` - Print the run id and return.
- `-s, --sandbox string` - Sandbox to run in.

#### `miosa agent runs`

List an agent's runs.

```
miosa agent runs <agent> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many.
- `--source string` - Only runs from this source: console, api, workflow, trigger or test.
- `--status string` - Only runs in this status: queued, running, succeeded, failed or canceled.

#### `miosa agent templates`

List starter agent templates.

```
miosa agent templates
```

#### `miosa agent triggers`

Run an agent on a schedule, webhook or event.

```
miosa agent triggers
```

Triggers start runs of an agent without a person: a schedule, a trigger URL, or an event.

Aliases: `trigger`.

##### `miosa agent triggers add`

Create a trigger.

```
miosa agent triggers add <agent> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--input string` - The instruction each run receives.
- `--kind string` - Kind of trigger (schedule|webhook).
- `--name string` - A label.
- `--schedule string` - Cron expression for a schedule trigger.
- `--timeout int` - Seconds a scheduled run may take, 60 to 3600.
- `--tz string` - IANA time zone the schedule is evaluated in (default UTC).

##### `miosa agent triggers fire`

Run a trigger now.

```
miosa agent triggers fire <agent> <trigger>
```

##### `miosa agent triggers list`

List an agent's triggers.

```
miosa agent triggers list <agent>
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa agent triggers rm`

Delete a trigger.

```
miosa agent triggers rm <agent> <trigger> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa agent triggers rotate-secret`

Rotate a webhook trigger's secret (shown once).

```
miosa agent triggers rotate-secret <agent> <trigger>
```

#### `miosa agent unarchive`

Bring an archived agent back.

```
miosa agent unarchive <agent>
```

#### `miosa agent update`

Change an agent's name or description.

```
miosa agent update <agent> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--description string` - New description.
- `--name string` - New name.

#### `miosa agent version`

Show one published version.

```
miosa agent version <agent> <number>
```

#### `miosa agent versions`

List an agent's published versions.

```
miosa agent versions <agent>
```

### `miosa alerts`

Platform alerts for your organization.

```
miosa alerts
```

#### `miosa alerts list`

List alerts.

```

```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa alerts resolve`

Mark an alert resolved.

```

```

### `miosa api`

Make an authenticated API request.

```
miosa api [METHOD] <path> [flags]
```

Send a request to the MIOSA API with your stored credentials and print the
response body.
The path is relative to the API base URL (/api/v1).
Use it for
anything the CLI has no command for yet.

The method defaults to GET, or POST when --data is given. --data takes a JSON
string, @file to read a file, or - to read standard input.
A failing status
exits non-zero (see 'miosa --help' for exit codes) and still prints the body.

```
  miosa api /credits/balance
  miosa api GET /sandboxes/my-box/ports
  miosa api POST /webhooks -d '{"url":"https://example.com/hook","events":["sandbox.ready"]}'
  miosa api PATCH /sandboxes/my-box/tags -d @tags.json
  echo '{"name":"x"}' | miosa api POST /workspaces -d -
```

Flags:

- `-d, --data string` - Request body: JSON, @file, or - for stdin.
- `-H, --header stringArray` - Extra header, 'Name: value' (repeatable).
- `-i, --include` - Print the status line and response headers first.
- `-X, --method string` - HTTP method (default GET, or POST with --data).

### `miosa api-key`

Create and manage API keys.

```
miosa api-key
```

API keys let scripts, CI and other programs call MIOSA.
A key is shown once, at
creation and rotation; store it right away.
A key acts as your organization
with the permissions you give it.

```
  miosa api-key create ci --preset ci --expires-in 90
  miosa api-key create reviewer --scope agents:read --scope agents:run
  miosa api-key list
  miosa api-key rotate <id>
  miosa api-key rm <id>

```
The key goes to standard output on create, so you can capture it:
```
  KEY=$(miosa api-key create ci --preset ci --quiet-key)
```

Aliases: `api-keys`, `keys`.

#### `miosa api-key create`

Create a key.

```
miosa api-key create <name> [flags]
```

Create an API key.
With no --scope or --preset the key gets the default set of
scopes.
Presets: read-only (every :read scope), ci (sandboxes, deployments,
previews), agent (run agents on sandboxes), cli (what the miosa CLI uses day to
day, including connections), automation (server-side scripts: sandboxes with
files, exec, logs and services, deployments, agents), full-access (every scope;
organization owners and admins only). 'miosa api-key presets' lists them.
Scope names look like sandboxes:exec
or agents:run; see 'miosa api-key list --json' for what existing keys hold.

The secret is printed once. --quiet-key prints only the secret.

Flags:

- `--allowed-ip stringArray` - Only accept requests from this IP or CIDR (repeatable).
- `--expires-in int` - Expire after this many days (default: never).
- `--preset string` - Permission preset: read-only, ci, agent, cli, automation or full-access.
- `--quiet-key` - Print only the secret.
- `--rate-limit int` - Requests per minute for this key.
- `--scope stringArray` - A scope to grant, for example sandboxes:exec (repeatable).
- `--ttl duration` - Expire after this long, in whole days: 30d, 720h (same as --expires-in).
- `--workspace-id string` - Bind the key to one workspace.

#### `miosa api-key events`

Show when a key was used and what changed.

```
miosa api-key events <id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--limit int` - How many events.
- `--type string` - Only events of this type.

#### `miosa api-key get`

Show one key (never its secret).

```
miosa api-key get <id>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa api-key list`

List keys (revoked ones hidden unless --all).

```
miosa api-key list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--all` - Include revoked keys.

#### `miosa api-key preset`

Save named scope sets for new keys.

```
miosa api-key preset
```

A preset is a name for a scope list, so a team mints keys by name.
The built-in
presets (read-only, ci, agent, cli) cannot be changed; add your own.

```
  miosa api-key preset new backend --scope sandboxes:read --scope agents:read
  miosa api-key preset rm backend

```
Only organization owners and admins manage presets.

##### `miosa api-key preset new`

Save a named scope set.

```
miosa api-key preset new <name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`, `add`.

Flags:

- `--description string` - What the preset is for.
- `--scope stringArray` - A scope to include, for example sandboxes:read (repeatable).

##### `miosa api-key preset rm`

Remove a custom preset.

```
miosa api-key preset rm <name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`, `remove`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa api-key presets`

List the permission presets for new keys.

```
miosa api-key presets
```

#### `miosa api-key rm`

Revoke a key (it stops working immediately).

```
miosa api-key rm <id> [flags]
```

Aliases: `revoke`, `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa api-key rotate`

Replace a key's secret (the old one stops working).

```
miosa api-key rotate <id>
```

Issue a new secret for the key.
The old secret stops working at once, so update whatever uses it first or have both ready.
The new secret is shown once.

#### `miosa api-key scoped`

Mint a restricted key for one end user of your product.

```
miosa api-key scoped <label> [flags]
```

Create a key bound to a workspace and an external user id, for products that act
for their own customers.
Needs an owner or admin.

Flags:

- `--expires-at string` - RFC 3339 expiry.
- `--external-user-id string` - Your id for the end user.
- `--scope stringArray` - A scope to grant (repeatable).
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the key is bound to.

#### `miosa api-key update`

Rename a key or change its limits.

```
miosa api-key update <id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--name string` - New name.
- `--rate-limit int` - Requests per minute.

#### `miosa api-key usage`

How much a key is used: requests, last use and from where.

```
miosa api-key usage <id> [flags]
```

Shows how many requests the key has made, when it was last used and from which
address, how old it is and when it expires. --days N also lists its requests per
day (UTC) for the last N days, up to 90; today trails by up to a minute.
'miosa api-key events <id>' lists what it did.

```
  miosa api-key usage k1
  miosa api-key usage k1 --days 30
```

Flags:

- `--days int` - Also list requests per day for the last N days (1-90).

### `miosa audit`

Show the audit log of API activity.

```
miosa audit [flags]
```

Who did what, when, from where.
Newest first.

Aliases: `activity`.

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many entries. (default `50`)
- `--type string` - Only this event type.
- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace.

#### `miosa audit export`

Write the audit log as JSONL (90 days by default).

```
miosa audit export [flags]
```

Write the organization's audit log to stdout as JSONL, one event per line,
exactly as the API returns it.

```
  miosa audit export > audit.jsonl
  miosa audit export --since 7d --resource-type sandbox > sandboxes.jsonl
  miosa audit export --since 2026-09-01 --until 2026-09-30 --type api_key.created

```
--since takes a duration (30m, 24h, 7d, 2w) or an RFC 3339 date; the default
is 90 days, which is how long the audit stream keeps events.
Pages are read
newest first and the export stops as soon as an event is older than --since,
so a window costs only the pages it needs.

Flags:

- `--resource-id string` - Only events about this resource id.
- `--resource-type string` - Only events about this resource type.
- `--since string` - How far back to read: a duration (7d) or an RFC 3339 date. (default `90d`)
- `--type string` - Only this event type.
- `--until string` - Only events at or before this RFC 3339 date.
- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace.

### `miosa batch`

Create many sandboxes in one request.

```
miosa batch
```

A batch asks for N identical sandboxes and creates them with bounded
concurrency.
The call returns at once with a batch id; 'status' and 'watch'
show how many are up, and 'items' lists the sandboxes.
Quota, credit and
placement apply to every sandbox as for a single 'miosa create'.

```
  miosa batch new --count 50 --size small --name-prefix ci --wait
  miosa batch status <batch>
  miosa batch items <batch>
  miosa batch cancel <batch>
```

Aliases: `batches`.

#### `miosa batch cancel`

Stop creating the sandboxes still pending.

```
miosa batch cancel <batch>
```

Sandboxes already created are left alone; destroy them with 'miosa destroy'.
A batch that has finished cannot be canceled.

#### `miosa batch items`

List the sandboxes of a batch.

```
miosa batch items <batch> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--limit int` - How many to show.

#### `miosa batch new`

Create a batch of identical sandboxes.

```
miosa batch new [flags]
```

Creates --count sandboxes (1 or more; the server states the maximum for your
plan) with at most --concurrency creating at once.
Prints the batch id.
With --wait the command blocks until the batch has settled and exits non-zero
if any sandbox failed.

```
  miosa batch new --count 20 --template nextjs --name-prefix demo --ttl 2h --wait
```

Flags:

- `--concurrency int` - How many to create at once (default: the server's).
- `--count int` - How many sandboxes to create.
- `--idle-timeout string` - Idle timeout of each sandbox, for example 15m.
- `--name-prefix string` - Prefix for the sandbox names, numbered from 1.
- `--region string` - Region.
- `--size string` - Sandbox size, as for 'miosa create'.
- `--tag stringArray` - Tag KEY=VALUE for every sandbox (repeatable).
- `--template string` - Sandbox template (default miosa-sandbox).
- `--ttl string` - Time to live of each sandbox, for example 2h.
- `--wait` - Wait until the batch has settled.
- `--wait-timeout duration` - How long --wait may take. (default `15m0s`)
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug (default: the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa batch status`

Show how a batch is going.

```
miosa batch status <batch>
```

Aliases: `get`.

#### `miosa batch watch`

Follow a batch until it settles.

```
miosa batch watch <batch> [flags]
```

Prints a progress line each time the batch changes and exits when it has
settled: 0 when every sandbox came up, non-zero when any failed or the batch was
canceled.

Flags:

- `--timeout duration` - Give up after this long (seconds, or a duration like 3m). (default `15m0s`)

### `miosa billing`

Credits, spend, invoices and plan.

```
miosa billing
```

What you have and what you spent.
Credits are cents. 'billing add-credits' adds credits
(a Stripe checkout in your browser) and waits for the payment; 'billing portal'
opens the Stripe portal for cards and invoices; plans change in the console
('miosa open billing').

#### `miosa billing add-credits`

Add credits: opens a Stripe checkout and waits for the payment.

```
miosa billing add-credits <dollars> [flags]
```

Add credits to the organization.
A Stripe checkout opens in your browser (or
its link is printed with --no-open); the command then waits, up to 15 minutes,
until the payment lands and says the new balance.
Ctrl-C stops waiting, not the
payment.
Free organizations add from $5.

```
  miosa billing add-credits 20
  miosa billing add-credits 50 --no-open      # print the link, e.g. on a server
```

Flags:

- `--no-open` - Print the checkout link instead of opening a browser.
- `--no-wait` - Return once the checkout exists, without waiting for the payment.

#### `miosa billing auto-reload`

Add credits when the balance is low.

```
miosa billing auto-reload
```

Auto-reload adds credits when the balance falls below a threshold.

```
  miosa billing auto-reload
  miosa billing auto-reload set --threshold 500 --amount 5000 --monthly-cap 20000
  miosa billing auto-reload off

```
Numbers are credits (cents). 'off' keeps the amounts and turns the request off;
the API reports 'enabled' as true only when the request is on, a card is ready
and the amounts make sense, so read the answer rather than the flag.

##### `miosa billing auto-reload get`

Show the current settings.

```
miosa billing auto-reload get
```

##### `miosa billing auto-reload off`

Turn auto-reload off.

```
miosa billing auto-reload off
```

##### `miosa billing auto-reload set`

Turn auto-reload on with these amounts.

```
miosa billing auto-reload set [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--amount int` - Credits to add each time.
- `--monthly-cap int` - Most credits to add in a month.
- `--threshold int` - Balance that triggers an add, in credits.

#### `miosa billing balance`

Show the credit balance.

```
miosa billing balance
```

#### `miosa billing breakdown`

Spend by resource.

```
miosa billing breakdown
```

#### `miosa billing cap`

Monthly spending caps for the organization and its workspaces.

```
miosa billing cap
```

A cap is a monthly spending limit.
Reaching it either pauses (new spend is refused and
running workloads are stopped, never destroyed, and nothing resumes by itself) or only alerts.
Alerts go out at the organization's thresholds (50, 80, 90 and 100 percent by default) by email and
the billing.spend_threshold_reached webhook.

##### `miosa billing cap set`

Set or clear a monthly cap.

```
miosa billing cap set [flags]
```

Set the organization cap or one workspace's cap, in US dollars a month.

```
  miosa billing cap set --org --monthly 500 --action pause
  miosa billing cap set --workspace web --monthly 100 --action alert
  miosa billing cap set --workspace web --scope sandboxes --monthly 40
  miosa billing cap set --workspace web --clear

```
The organization cap and a workspace's default scope ("all") count every product.
A workspace
action applies to both of its caps.
Only owners and admins can change caps.

Flags:

- `--action string` - At the cap: pause (refuse new spend, stop running work) or alert.
- `--clear` - Remove the cap.
- `--monthly float64` - Cap in US dollars a month.
- `--org` - Set the organization cap.
- `--scope string` - For a workspace: all products, or sandboxes only. (default `all`)
- `--workspace string` - Set this workspace's cap (slug, name or id).

##### `miosa billing cap show`

Show the organization cap and every workspace cap.

```
miosa billing cap show
```

#### `miosa billing invoices`

List invoices.

```
miosa billing invoices
```

#### `miosa billing payment-methods`

List the cards on file, or choose the default.

```
miosa billing payment-methods
```

miosa billing payment-methods list
```
  miosa billing payment-methods default <id>

```
Adding or removing a card happens in the console: card details never reach this
CLI, which only ever sees the brand, last four digits and expiry.

##### `miosa billing payment-methods default`

Charge this card by default.

```
miosa billing payment-methods default <id>
```

##### `miosa billing payment-methods list`

List the cards on file.

```
miosa billing payment-methods list
```

#### `miosa billing plan`

Show your plan and current usage against its limits.

```
miosa billing plan
```

#### `miosa billing portal`

Open the Stripe billing portal (cards, invoices, receipts).

```
miosa billing portal [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--no-open` - Print the portal link instead of opening a browser.

#### `miosa billing spend`

Compute spend per workspace.

```
miosa billing spend [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--period string` - Window to read (default 30d) (7d|30d|mtd).

#### `miosa billing transactions`

List credit transactions.

```
miosa billing transactions [flags]
```

Aliases: `tx`.

Flags:

- `--page int` - Page number.
- `-n, --per-page int` - How many.

#### `miosa billing upcoming`

Show the upcoming invoice.

```
miosa billing upcoming
```

#### `miosa billing usage`

Model usage by day.

```
miosa billing usage
```

### `miosa browser`

Drive a sandbox's live browser over the DevTools Protocol.

```
miosa browser
```

The browser runs inside the sandbox. 'start' launches it lazily and 'open'
shows its live view; 'forward' and 'devtools' open it on your machine over the
DevTools Protocol, which is what Selenium and chromedp expect, without exposing
the plaintext debugging port.
Actions (goto, click, type, key) drive it directly.

Every subcommand targets the current sandbox unless --sandbox names another.

Flags:


#### `miosa browser cdp`

Mint a CDP grant for the live browser.

```
miosa browser cdp [flags]
```

Mint a scoped, expiring CDP grant and print its WebSocket URL and the cdp host
forms, plus Playwright and Puppeteer one-liners.
Needs the sandboxes:write
scope.
The cdp host carries the CDP token: do not log or share it.

Flags:

- `--ttl int` - Grant lifetime in seconds (0 = server default).

#### `miosa browser click`

Click at a point in the live browser.

```
miosa browser click <x> <y>
```

#### `miosa browser devtools`

Forward CDP and open chrome://inspect.

```
miosa browser devtools [flags]
```

Forward the sandbox's CDP endpoint to a local port (default 9222, the port
chrome://inspect auto-discovers), open chrome://inspect/#devices, and block
until Ctrl-C.
Needs the sandboxes:write scope.

Flags:

- `--bind string` - Local address to listen on. (default `127.0.0.1`)
- `--port int` - Local port for the forwarded CDP endpoint. (default `9222`)

#### `miosa browser forward`

Forward a sandbox's CDP endpoint to a local port.

```
miosa browser forward <sandbox_id> [flags]
```

Mint a scoped, expiring CDP grant for the sandbox's live browser and serve it
on 127.0.0.1:<port>.
It proxies CDP discovery (/json/*) to the cdp host and the
/devtools/* WebSocket the client then dials, rewriting webSocketDebuggerUrl to
the local listener.
The grant stays in the host label, so no plaintext
debugging port is ever exposed.

Then point a tool at the local port:

```
  Selenium:  options.add_experimental_option("debuggerAddress", "127.0.0.1:9222")
  chromedp:  chromedp.NewRemoteAllocator(ctx, "http://127.0.0.1:9222")

  miosa browser forward my-box
  miosa browser forward my-box --port 9333
```

Flags:

- `--bind string` - Local address to listen on. (default `127.0.0.1`)
- `--port int` - Local port to listen on. (default `9222`)

#### `miosa browser goto`

Navigate the live browser to a URL.

```
miosa browser goto <url>
```

#### `miosa browser key`

Press a key in the live browser.

```
miosa browser key <key>
```

Press a key or key combination, for example "Enter" or "ctrl+a".
Needs the
sandboxes:write scope.

#### `miosa browser open`

Start the live browser and open its view URL.

```
miosa browser open [flags]
```

Start the sandbox's browser lazily and open its live view URL in your browser.
Use --print to only print the URL.
Needs the sandboxes:write scope.

Flags:

- `--print` - Print the live URL instead of opening it.
- `--ttl int` - Session lifetime in seconds (0 = server default).

#### `miosa browser revoke`

Revoke share links for the live browser.

```
miosa browser revoke [flags]
```

Revoke either every share and CDP link (--all) or one share (--share-id ID).
Exactly one of the two is required.
Needs the sandboxes:write scope.

Flags:

- `--all` - Revoke every share and CDP link.
- `--share-id string` - Revoke one share by id.

#### `miosa browser screenshot`

Capture a screenshot of the live browser.

```
miosa browser screenshot [flags]
```

Capture a screenshot of the sandbox's live browser.
With -O the image is
written to a file (the path is required) and the path and size are printed;
without -O the raw image bytes go to stdout.
The global -o selects the output
format, so this uses -O/--output-file.
Needs the sandboxes:read scope.

Flags:

- `--format string` - Image format: png or jpeg (default: server default).
- `-O, --output-file string` - Write the image to this file path.
- `--tab string` - Target tab id (default: the active tab).

#### `miosa browser share`

Create a view-only share link for the live browser.

```
miosa browser share [flags]
```

Create a view-only share link for the sandbox's live browser and print its
view URL, share id, and expiry.
The server shares view-only mode; --view-only is
accepted because it is the only mode.
Needs the sandboxes:write scope.

Flags:

- `--ttl int` - Share lifetime in seconds (0 = server default).
- `--view-only` - View-only mode (the only mode the server shares).

#### `miosa browser start`

Start the sandbox's live browser.

```
miosa browser start [flags]
```

Start the sandbox's browser lazily and print its state and live view URL.
A
stopped sandbox answers SANDBOX_NOT_RUNNING; resume it first.
Needs the
sandboxes:write scope.

Flags:

- `--ttl int` - Session lifetime in seconds (0 = server default).

#### `miosa browser status`

Show the live browser state without starting it.

```
miosa browser status
```

Read the live browser state without starting anything.
Prints the state, the
epoch, the viewport, and every external Chrome attached to the browser.
Needs
the sandboxes:read scope.

#### `miosa browser stop`

Stop the sandbox's live browser.

```
miosa browser stop
```

Stop the sandbox's browser and print its state.
Needs the sandboxes:write
scope.

#### `miosa browser type`

Type text into the live browser.

```
miosa browser type <text>
```

### `miosa catalog`

Show canonical compute products, templates, sizes, and readiness.

```
miosa catalog [flags]
```

Show the canonical MIOSA compute catalog.

Use this before creating resources so you can choose the right product lane
(sandbox, computer, docker_deploy_host), template, size, and fast-readiness
state.
Readiness states are fast_ready, cold_boot_only, and missing.

Flags:

- `--product string` - Filter by product id: sandbox, computer, docker_deploy_host.
- `--size string` - Filter by size: xs, small, medium, large, xl.
- `--state string` - Filter by readiness: fast_ready, cold_boot_only, missing.
- `--template string` - Filter by template id, e.g. miosa-sandbox, nextjs, or static-html.

### `miosa chat`

Chats with an agent that remembers.

```
miosa chat
```

A chat is a series of runs that share a chat id: each follow-up resumes the
harness's own session on the same machine, so the agent keeps its context.

```
  miosa chat start --sandbox my-box      # interactive; prints the chat id
  miosa chat list
  miosa chat show 3f0c...
  miosa prompt --chat 3f0c... "and add tests"
```

#### `miosa chat compact`

Compact the agent's context so the chat can go on.

```
miosa chat compact <chat-id> [flags]
```

Starts a run in the same chat, on the same machine, that compacts the harness's
context.
It needs the latest run to be finished and the harness to support
compaction (Claude Code does).
Follow it with 'miosa run follow <run-id>'.

Flags:

- `--focus string` - What the summary should keep.

#### `miosa chat context`

Show how full the agent's context is.

```
miosa chat context <chat-id>
```

Shows how many tokens the chat has used of the harness window, how many
compactions it has had, and whether it can be compacted now.
When it cannot, the
reason is one of: run_in_progress, no_session, harness_unsupported.

#### `miosa chat list`

List recent chats.

```
miosa chat list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many recent runs to group, 1-100. (default `100`)
- `-s, --sandbox string` - Only chats on this sandbox.

#### `miosa chat show`

Print a chat, oldest first.

```
miosa chat show <chat-id>
```

#### `miosa chat start`

Chat with an agent interactively.

```
miosa chat start [chat-id] [flags]
```

Read prompts line by line and run each as a follow-up in one chat.
The
agent keeps its memory between lines.
Give a chat id to resume an earlier chat.
End with /exit or Ctrl-D.

```
  miosa chat start --sandbox my-box
  miosa chat resume 3f0c...
```

Aliases: `resume`.

Flags:

- `--agent string` - Saved agent (name or id).
- `--chat string` - Chat id.
- `--computer string` - Computer.
- `--harness string` - Harness: claude-code, codex or osa.
- `--model string` - Model.
- `-s, --sandbox string` - Sandbox (default: the current sandbox).

### `miosa checkpoint`

Manage sandbox checkpoints.

```
miosa checkpoint
```

Create, list, inspect, and delete checkpoints for a sandbox.

#### `miosa checkpoint create`

Create a checkpoint.

```
miosa checkpoint create [name|id] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--comment string` - Optional description for the checkpoint.

#### `miosa checkpoint delete`

Delete a checkpoint.

```
miosa checkpoint delete <checkpoint-id>
```

#### `miosa checkpoint info`

Show details for a checkpoint.

```
miosa checkpoint info <checkpoint-id>
```

#### `miosa checkpoint list`

List checkpoints for a sandbox.

```
miosa checkpoint list [name|id]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

### `miosa cloud`

Your own cloud accounts, regions and host pools (bring your own cloud).

```
miosa cloud
```

Shows the cloud accounts connected to your organization, the regions and host
pools built on them, and the hosts in each pool, and sets them up.
Bring your
own cloud is in private preview: connecting an account and creating regions and
pools are accepted when your organization is enrolled, but starting hosts
('pool provision') and creating pools need host provisioning to be activated
for you, exactly as in the console. 'miosa cloud capabilities' shows what is.

Cloud changes need an owner or admin and a key with the cloud:write scope.

```
  miosa cloud account new prod --provider aws
  miosa cloud account attach-aws <account> --role-arn arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/MiosaAccess --region us-east-1
  miosa cloud preflight run --account <account>
  miosa cloud pool new --region <region> --instance-type m7i.8xlarge --target-nodes 1 --max-nodes 4
  miosa cloud pool provision <pool> --count 1
```

Aliases: `byoc`.

#### `miosa cloud account`

Connect a cloud account.

```
miosa cloud account
```

An account is the AWS or Google Cloud account MIOSA starts hosts in.
Creating one is the first step; attach the access role or identity next.

##### `miosa cloud account attach-aws`

Attach the AWS role MIOSA assumes.

```
miosa cloud account attach-aws <account> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--region string` - Default AWS region.
- `--role-arn string` - ARN of the role in your AWS account.

##### `miosa cloud account attach-gcp`

Attach the Google Cloud identity MIOSA uses.

```
miosa cloud account attach-gcp <account> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--actuator-service-account string` - Email of the service account that launches hosts.
- `--identity-provider string` - Workload identity provider resource name.
- `--project-id string` - Google Cloud project id.
- `--project-number string` - Google Cloud project number.
- `--region string` - Default region.
- `--worker-service-account string` - Email of the service account hosts run as.

##### `miosa cloud account new`

Create a cloud account record.

```
miosa cloud account new <name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--provider string` - Cloud provider (aws|gcp).

#### `miosa cloud accounts`

List connected cloud accounts.

```
miosa cloud accounts
```

#### `miosa cloud capabilities`

Show what each provider can do for your organization.

```
miosa cloud capabilities
```

#### `miosa cloud node`

Hosts in a pool.

```
miosa cloud node
```

##### `miosa cloud node certify`

Check that a host can run workloads.

```
miosa cloud node certify <node> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--workload string` - Workload to certify. (default `sandbox`)

#### `miosa cloud nodes`

List the hosts in your pools.

```
miosa cloud nodes
```

#### `miosa cloud pool`

Pools of hosts in a region.

```
miosa cloud pool
```

A pool says how many hosts of one type to keep in a region.
Creating and starting them needs host provisioning activated for your organization.

##### `miosa cloud pool new`

Create a host pool.

```
miosa cloud pool new [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--instance-type string` - Instance type, for example m7i.8xlarge.
- `--max-hourly-cents int` - Spending cap per hour, in cents.
- `--max-nodes int` - Most hosts the pool may reach.
- `--region string` - Region id.
- `--scope stringArray` - Workload the pool may serve: sandbox, computer or deployment (repeatable).
- `--target-nodes int` - How many hosts to keep.
- `--ttl-seconds int` - Retire hosts after this many seconds.

##### `miosa cloud pool provision`

Start hosts in a pool.

```
miosa cloud pool provision <pool> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--count int` - How many hosts to start. (default `1`)
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa cloud pool update`

Change a pool's size or limits.

```
miosa cloud pool update <pool> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--max-hourly-cents int` - Spending cap per hour, in cents.
- `--max-nodes int` - Most hosts the pool may reach.
- `--scope stringArray` - Workload the pool may serve (repeatable).
- `--target-nodes int` - How many hosts to keep.
- `--ttl-seconds int` - Retire hosts after this many seconds.

#### `miosa cloud pools`

List host pools.

```
miosa cloud pools
```

#### `miosa cloud preflight`

Check that an account or region is ready.

```
miosa cloud preflight
```

A preflight tests access and configuration without starting any host.

##### `miosa cloud preflight run`

Run a preflight check.

```
miosa cloud preflight run [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--account string` - Account id to check.
- `--region string` - Region id to check.

#### `miosa cloud preflights`

List preflight checks run against your accounts.

```
miosa cloud preflights
```

#### `miosa cloud region`

Regions built on a cloud account.

```
miosa cloud region
```

A region ties an account to a network (subnet, security groups) and the host image MIOSA starts.

##### `miosa cloud region new`

Create a region on an account.

```
miosa cloud region new [flags]
```

The core fields are flags.
For anything else (Google Cloud networks, host image details) put a JSON object in --file; flags override its fields.

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--account string` - Account id.
- `--artifact-manifest-uri string` - Location of the host artifact manifest.
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--instance-profile string` - Instance profile reference.
- `--meta stringArray` - Region metadata KEY=VALUE, for example host_image_id=ami-123 (repeatable).
- `--name string` - Region name.
- `--provider-region string` - Provider region, for example us-east-1.
- `--security-group stringArray` - Security group id (repeatable).
- `--subnet string` - Subnet id.
- `--zone string` - Provider zone.

#### `miosa cloud regions`

List the regions built on your accounts.

```
miosa cloud regions
```

### `miosa computer`

Manage computers.

```
miosa computer
```

#### `miosa computer auto-stop`

Show when an idle computer stops itself.

```
miosa computer auto-stop <computer>
```

#### `miosa computer create`

Create a computer.

```
miosa computer create <name> [flags]
```

Provision a MIOSA computer (a persistent desktop machine).

--template picks the computer template; it defaults to miosa-desktop, the
desktop machine image.
The API requires a template on this route (it answers
400 template_type is required without one), so the default is sent, not left
to the server.

--env picks the environment (repositories, secrets, connections) the computer
starts from; without it your default environment is used. --no-env passes
nothing of yours. --setup-file runs a script (UTF-8, up to 64KB) in the
background once the computer is ready.

Examples:
```
  miosa computer create my-desktop
  miosa computer create my-desktop --env staging --setup-file ./setup.sh
```

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment the computer starts from (default: your default environment).
- `--no-env` - Pass nothing of yours to the computer (safe to hand to third parties).
- `--setup-file string` - Shell script (UTF-8, max 64KB) run in the background once the computer is ready.
- `--size string` - Computer size: xs, small, medium, large, or xl. (default `small`)
- `--template string` - Computer template type (miosa-desktop by default). (default `miosa-desktop`)
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug, name or id (default: $MIOSA_WORKSPACE, then the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa computer get`

Show a computer.

```
miosa computer get <computer>
```

Aliases: `info`, `show`.

#### `miosa computer list`

List computers.

```
miosa computer list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa computer logs`

Show a computer's log.

```
miosa computer logs <computer>
```

#### `miosa computer ports`

List ports exposed by a computer.

```
miosa computer ports <computer>
```

#### `miosa computer restart`

Restart a computer.

```
miosa computer restart <computer>
```

#### `miosa computer rm`

Permanently delete a computer.

```
miosa computer rm <computer> [flags]
```

Aliases: `destroy`, `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa computer start`

Start a stopped computer.

```
miosa computer start <computer>
```

#### `miosa computer stop`

Stop a computer (it keeps its disk).

```
miosa computer stop <computer>
```

#### `miosa computer urls`

Show the computer's desktop and service URLs.

```
miosa computer urls <computer>
```

Print the URLs for the computer's desktop stream and exposed services.
They can carry a key: treat the output like a password.

### `miosa config`

Read and change CLI settings.

```
miosa config
```

Settings live in ~/.miosa/config.toml.
Keys: api_url, auto_update, current_sandbox, default_workspace.
The API key is never printed here; sign in with 'miosa login'.

#### `miosa config get`

Print one setting.

```
miosa config get <key>
```

#### `miosa config list`

Show the effective settings of the current profile.

```
miosa config list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa config path`

Print the config file path.

```
miosa config path
```

#### `miosa config set`

Change one setting in the current profile.

```
miosa config set <key> <value>
```

### `miosa connections`

Model providers, apps and tool servers your agents use.

```
miosa connections
```

Connections are what your agents and machines use to reach the outside world:
model providers (Claude, ChatGPT, API keys), apps (GitHub, Slack, Linear,
Discord, Google Workspace, Notion) and tool servers.
MIOSA's own model keys never power your agents;
every run uses a connection of yours.

```
  miosa connections list
  miosa connections add models anthropic --key-stdin <<< "$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"
  miosa connections add models claude --sandbox my-box     # sign in with a subscription
  miosa connections add apps github
  miosa connections add tools --name docs --url https://mcp.example.com/sse
  miosa connections rm models anthropic

```
For a platform builder: connect accounts for your end users, without sending
anyone to the console.
Every command takes --external-user (your own id for the
user) and --workspace-id:

```
  miosa connections add models claude --external-user cus_123 --no-wait   # link + code to show them
  miosa connections add models anthropic --external-user cus_123 --key-stdin <<< "$KEY"
  miosa connections test models anthropic --external-user cus_123
  miosa connections tool-keys set APIFY_TOKEN --external-user cus_123 --key-stdin <<< "$TOKEN"
  miosa connections harnesses --external-user cus_123

```
An API key needs the connections:read scope to list and connections:write to
change them: 'miosa api-key create NAME --preset cli' has both.
A list the key
may not read is reported as unavailable instead of failing the whole command.
Signing in with a subscription or an app prints a link or code to finish in a
browser.

Aliases: `connection`, `conn`.

#### `miosa connections add`

Connect a model provider, an app or a tool server.

```
miosa connections add
```

##### `miosa connections add apps`

Connect an app through your browser.

```
miosa connections add apps <github|slack|linear|discord|google|notion> [flags]
```

Starts the OAuth flow for an app, prints the consent URL, and - once the
browser finishes - prints the connection id.

A Google or Notion account is one connection: connect four Gmail accounts and
you get four connections, each with its own label.
Google scopes follow the
products you name, never more:

```
  miosa connections add apps google --label work-gmail --products gmail,calendar,drive
  miosa connections add apps notion --label team-wiki
  miosa connections add apps google --label qa-gmail --products gmail --no-wait

```
Use --no-wait when the browser is on another device; check the result with
'miosa connections list --kind apps'.

Flags:

- `--label string` - Name this connection (for example work-gmail).
- `--no-wait` - Print the consent URL and exit without waiting.
- `--products string` - Google products to request (gmail, gmail-send, calendar, drive, docs, sheets).
- `--workspace-id string` - Bind the connection to one workspace.

##### `miosa connections add models`

Connect a model provider.

```
miosa connections add models <provider> [flags]
```

Connect a model provider for your agents.

With an API key (anthropic, openai, google, groq, mistral, openrouter, together,
...): the key is read from standard input (--key-stdin) or typed hidden.
It is
never accepted as an argument, so it stays out of your shell history.

With a subscription plan (claude, chatgpt, kimi, vibe): MIOSA signs in on the
control plane, prints a URL (and a code), waits for you to approve it in the
browser and, for Claude, to paste the code back here.

For one of your end users, add --external-user ID: the account is theirs alone.
Add --no-wait to print the link and code and return, so your own UI can show them
(then follow up with 'miosa connections signin ...').

```
  miosa connections add models anthropic --key-stdin <<< "$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"
  miosa connections add models chatgpt
  miosa connections add models claude --external-user cus_123 --no-wait
  miosa connections add models openrouter --external-user cus_123 --key-stdin <<< "$KEY"
```

Flags:

- `--computer string` - Computer the sign-in runs in (subscriptions); it is saved for the org, workspace or end user, not this computer.
- `--external-user string` - Connect it for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--key-stdin` - Read the API key from standard input.
- `--label string` - A label for the connection.
- `--no-wait` - Subscriptions: print the link and code and return; your UI shows them.
- `--replace` - Replace the organization's existing sign-in for this provider.
- `-s, --sandbox string` - Sandbox the sign-in runs in (subscriptions); it is saved for the org, workspace or end user, not this sandbox.
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.

##### `miosa connections add tools`

Connect an MCP tool server.

```
miosa connections add tools [flags]
```

Register a remote tool server (MCP) so agents can use its tools.
Header values
that are secrets are read from the environment, not the command line:

```
  miosa connections add tools --name docs --url https://mcp.example.com/sse
  DOCS_TOKEN=... miosa connections add tools --name docs --url https://mcp.example.com/sse \
      --header-env Authorization=DOCS_TOKEN
```

Flags:

- `--header-env stringArray` - Header whose value is read from an environment variable, HEADER=ENV_VAR (repeatable).
- `--name string` - Name agents refer to it by.
- `--url string` - The server's MCP endpoint.

#### `miosa connections harnesses`

Which harnesses can run, and with which connections.

```
miosa connections harnesses [harness] [flags]
```

Shows each harness (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, ...), whether a run would
have a connection to think with, and which of the connections could power it.
One plan can power several harnesses.

With a harness name: its models, and whether each is available with what is
connected.

```
  miosa connections harnesses --external-user cus_123
  miosa connections harnesses opencode
```

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.

#### `miosa connections list`

List connections.

```
miosa connections list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--kind string` - Only models, apps or tools.
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.

#### `miosa connections rm`

Disconnect a model provider, an app or a tool server.

```
miosa connections rm <models|apps|tools> <provider-or-id> [flags]
```

Disconnect a model provider, an app or a tool server.

For a workspace or an end user (--workspace-id, --external-user) a model provider
is removed at exactly that scope by name; a wider scope's connection is never
touched, and the scope falls back to it:

```
  miosa connections rm models claude --external-user cus_123
  miosa connections rm models anthropic --external-user cus_123
```

Aliases: `remove`, `delete`.

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa connections share`

Share a model connection with other workspaces.

```
miosa connections share <connection> [flags]
```

Share a model connection (an API key or subscription) with other workspaces.

A share is a grant, never a copy: the secret stays in the workspace that holds
it and a run in a shared workspace uses it when it runs.
Revoke it with
'miosa connections unshare'; it takes effect on the next run.

<connection> is the connection id from 'miosa connections list --json' (for
example openrouter, or agent_credential:openrouter).
The connection must be held
by a workspace itself; one held by the organization already reaches every
workspace.
The holding workspace is found for you, or name it with --from.

```
  miosa connections share openrouter --workspace research,ops
  miosa connections share claude_subscription --all-workspaces
  miosa connections share openrouter --workspace ops --from models

```
--all-workspaces skips workspaces flagged private unless you name them with
--workspace.

Flags:

- `--all-workspaces` - Every workspace of the organization except private ones.
- `--from string` - The workspace that holds the connection (default: the one that does).
- `--workspace stringSlice` - Workspace(s) to share with (slug or id, comma separated).

#### `miosa connections signin`

Follow a subscription sign-in started with --no-wait.

```
miosa connections signin
```

A subscription sign-in started with 'miosa connections add models claude --no-wait'
prints a link (and a code).
Show them to the user in your own UI, then drive
the sign-in from your side:

```
  miosa connections signin status SIGNIN_ID
  miosa connections signin code SIGNIN_ID --code-stdin <<< "$PASTED_CODE"   # Claude
  miosa connections signin wait SIGNIN_ID                                     # ChatGPT, Kimi
  miosa connections signin cancel SIGNIN_ID
```

##### `miosa connections signin cancel`

Cancel a sign-in.

```
miosa connections signin cancel <signin-id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).

##### `miosa connections signin code`

Hand over the code the user pasted (Claude).

```
miosa connections signin code <signin-id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--code string` - The pasted code.
- `--code-stdin` - Read the pasted code from standard input.
- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).

##### `miosa connections signin status`

Where a sign-in stands.

```
miosa connections signin status <signin-id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).

##### `miosa connections signin wait`

Wait until the user has approved the sign-in.

```
miosa connections signin wait <signin-id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--timeout duration` - Give up after this long (seconds, or a duration like 3m). (default `15m0s`)

#### `miosa connections test`

Check that a tool server answers, or that a model connection works.

```
miosa connections test <tool-server-id> | models <provider> [flags]
```

With a tool server id: check that it answers and list its tools.

With 'models <provider>': does the connection work?
One cheap authenticated
request to the provider (a subscription is checked by its stored state).
The key
and the provider's response are never shown.
Add --external-user to test an end
user's own connection.

```
  miosa connections test m1
  miosa connections test models anthropic --external-user cus_123
```

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.

#### `miosa connections tool-keys`

Keys for your tool servers, per workspace or end user.

```
miosa connections tool-keys
```

A tool server (Apify, Refero, your own) is defined once with its key as a
{{secret:NAME}} placeholder.
Each end user then supplies their own value for NAME, and an agent
run for that end user reaches the tool server with their key, falling back to
the workspace's.

```
  miosa connections tool-keys list --external-user cus_123
  miosa connections tool-keys set APIFY_TOKEN --key-stdin --external-user cus_123 <<< "$APIFY_TOKEN"
  miosa connections tool-keys rm APIFY_TOKEN --external-user cus_123
```

Aliases: `tool-key`.

##### `miosa connections tool-keys list`

Keys the tool servers need, and whether the scope has them.

```
miosa connections tool-keys list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.

##### `miosa connections tool-keys rm`

Remove the key held at exactly this scope.

```
miosa connections tool-keys rm <NAME> [flags]
```

Aliases: `remove`, `delete`.

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa connections tool-keys set`

Set or rotate a tool-server key.

```
miosa connections tool-keys set <NAME> [flags]
```

Reads the value from standard input (--key-stdin) or hidden at the prompt; it is
never accepted as an argument.
Fails with UNKNOWN_TOOL_KEY when no tool server
references NAME.

Flags:

- `--external-user string` - Act for one of your end users (your own id for them).
- `--key-stdin` - Read the value from standard input.
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace the connection belongs to.

#### `miosa connections unshare`

Stop sharing a model connection with workspaces.

```
miosa connections unshare <connection> [flags]
```

Revoke a share made with 'miosa connections share'.
Runs in the workspace use
their own connection, or none, from their next run.

```
  miosa connections unshare openrouter --workspace research
  miosa connections unshare openrouter --all-workspaces
```

Flags:

- `--all-workspaces` - Every workspace of the organization except private ones.
- `--from string` - The workspace that holds the connection (default: the one that does).
- `--workspace stringSlice` - Workspace(s) to stop sharing with (slug or id, comma separated).

#### `miosa connections use`

Choose which connections a harness runs on.

```
miosa connections use <harness> [connection...] [flags]
```

A connection is connected once and powers every harness that accepts it.
This
chooses which ones a harness uses, for the organization or one workspace.

A harness that takes one method at a time (claude-code, codex) takes exactly one
connection.
A harness that takes several (opencode, pi, prime, kimi, mistral,
hermes) takes a list, or --all for every connected one. --model and --effort set
the harness's defaults.

```
  miosa connections use claude-code claude            # the Claude plan
  miosa connections use claude-code anthropic         # the Anthropic key
  miosa connections use opencode chatgpt openrouter   # the ChatGPT plan and the OpenRouter key
  miosa connections use opencode --all
  miosa connections use codex --model gpt-6.1-sol --effort high

```
Connections are named claude, chatgpt, kimi, vibe (plans) or anthropic, openai,
openrouter, deepseek, llmgateway, moonshot, mistral, bedrock (keys).
The harness
settings belong to you, the builder: they are the same for all of your end users,
who bring only their connections.

Flags:

- `--all` - Every connected provider (harnesses that take several).
- `--effort string` - Default reasoning effort: max, xhigh, high, medium, low, minimal or none.
- `--model string` - Default model of the harness.
- `--workspace-id string` - Set it for one workspace instead of the organization.

### `miosa console`

Open an interactive shell in a sandbox.

```
miosa console [name|id] [-- <cmd> [args...]] [flags]
```

Open a full-duplex interactive terminal (a PTY) in a sandbox over the
miosa-terminal-v1 WebSocket.
Your terminal goes into raw mode for the session;
resizing the window resizes the remote PTY and Ctrl-C reaches the remote process.

With a command after -- the terminal runs that program instead of a login shell.
The exit status is the program's.
Use 'miosa exec' or 'miosa ssh -- cmd' for
non-interactive commands.

```
  miosa console my-box
  miosa console              # the current sandbox
  miosa console my-box --cwd /workspace
  miosa console my-box -- htop
```

Aliases: `shell`.

Flags:

- `--cwd string` - Absolute directory to start in.
- `--shell string` - Shell to start (a single executable, default: the sandbox's login shell).

### `miosa conversations`

List agent chats: last activity, prompts, running, harness/model, last prompt.

```
miosa conversations [sandbox] [flags]
```

Lists agent chats, newest first: when each was last active, how many prompts
it has, whether a turn is running, its harness and model, and its last prompt.
Without a sandbox every recent chat is listed.
Continue one with
'miosa prompt --chat <id> "..."' and stop its turn with 'miosa interrupt --convo <id>'.

Aliases: `convos`.

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many recent runs to group, 1-100. (default `100`)

### `miosa create`

Create a new sandbox.

```
miosa create [name] [flags]
```

Provision a new MIOSA sandbox.

If no name is provided, one is generated automatically.

Examples:
```
  miosa create my-box
  miosa create my-box --size medium --template miosa-sandbox
  miosa create my-box --runtime soma
  miosa create --size large
  miosa create my-box --env staging
  miosa create my-box --no-env
  miosa create my-box --setup-file ./setup.sh
  miosa create my-box --wait --ttl 2h --meta team=infra
  miosa create my-box -e API_URL=https://api.example.com -e DEBUG=1
  miosa create ci --count 20 --wait
  miosa create --from web-stack --wait     # from a named snapshot

```
Creation returns while the sandbox boots. --wait blocks until it accepts
commands, so 'miosa create x --wait && miosa exec x -- ...' never races.

--env picks the environment (repositories, secrets, connections) the sandbox
starts from; without it your default environment is used. --no-env passes
nothing of yours. --setup-file runs a script (UTF-8, up to 64KB) in the
background once the sandbox is ready; watch it with 'miosa info'.

-e KEY=VALUE sets a variable in this sandbox only, on top of the environment's.

--count N creates N identical sandboxes in one batch, their names starting with
<name> (--concurrency caps how many start at once); with --wait it blocks until all
have settled and exits non-zero if any failed. 'miosa batch' manages the batch.
--env, --no-env, --runtime, --meta and --expires apply to one sandbox only.

Flags:

- `--always-on` - Keep the sandbox running: no idle pause, and the platform resumes it after a host restart.
- `--concurrency int` - With --count: how many to create at once (default: the server's).
- `--count int` - Create this many identical sandboxes in one batch.
- `--env string` - Environment the sandbox starts from (default: your default environment).
- `--expires string` - Governance deadline: 24h, 7d or an RFC 3339 time (purpose=test defaults to 24h).
- `--fail-fast` - Start only if a machine is ready now; otherwise fail at once and start, bill and count nothing.
- `--from string` - Start from a snapshot: its id, or the name of a named snapshot.
- `--idle-timeout string` - Pause after this much inactivity: seconds, 15m, 1h.
- `--meta stringArray` - Metadata, KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--no-env` - Pass nothing of yours to the sandbox (safe to hand to third parties).
- `--runtime string` - Sandbox runtime: firecracker, soma or auto.
- `--setup-file string` - Shell script (UTF-8, max 64KB) run in the background once the sandbox is ready.
- `--size string` - Sandbox size: micro, tiny, xs, small, quad, medium, large, xl. (default `small`)
- `--tag stringArray` - Governance tag, KEY=VALUE (repeatable): owner, entity, purpose, project.
- `--template string` - Sandbox template, for example miosa-sandbox, nextjs, static-html, or fastapi-auth. (default `miosa-sandbox`)
- `--ttl string` - Time to live: seconds, 90m, 2h or 2d (default: the plan's).
- `-e, --var stringArray` - Variable for this sandbox only, KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--visibility string` - Who in the organization may use it: shared or private (default: the organization's, see 'miosa org default-visibility').
- `--wait` - Wait until the sandbox is ready for commands.
- `--wait-timeout duration` - How long --wait may take. (default `3m0s`)
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug to assign the sandbox to (default: the profile's default workspace).

### `miosa daemon`

Run named long-lived processes (daemons) in a sandbox.

```
miosa daemon
```

A daemon is a named background process in a sandbox that outlives your session: its
status and logs stay available after the CLI disconnects.
The sandbox is optional
everywhere (the current one is used).

```
  miosa daemon create my-box --name web --command "python -m http.server 8080"
  miosa daemon list
  miosa daemon logs web --tail 50
  miosa daemon restart web
```

#### `miosa daemon create`

Start a daemon.

```
miosa daemon create [sandbox] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--command string` - Command to run.
- `--cwd string` - Working directory (default /workspace).
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--name string` - Daemon name.
- `--sudo` - Run as root.

#### `miosa daemon get`

Show one daemon.

```
miosa daemon get [sandbox] <daemon>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa daemon list`

List daemons.

```
miosa daemon list [sandbox]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa daemon logs`

Show a daemon's output.

```
miosa daemon logs [sandbox] <daemon> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-n, --tail int` - Number of recent lines. (default `100`)

#### `miosa daemon restart`

Restart a daemon (also starts a stopped one).

```
miosa daemon restart [sandbox] <daemon>
```

Aliases: `start`.

#### `miosa daemon stop`

Stop a daemon.

```
miosa daemon stop [sandbox] <daemon>
```

Stop a daemon by killing its process.
Start it again with 'daemon restart'.

Aliases: `delete`, `rm`.

### `miosa dashboard`

Open the dashboard, already signed in.

```
miosa dashboard [flags]
```

Open the MIOSA dashboard in your browser, signed in as you, with a one-time
link that works once within 5 minutes.
Your second factor is still asked for
if you have one.

It needs a 'miosa login' session.
With any other key (MIOSA_API_KEY, --api-key)
it opens the dashboard's sign-in page instead.

```
  miosa dashboard
  miosa dashboard --print   # print the one-time link instead of opening it
```

Flags:

- `--print` - Print the link instead of opening it.

### `miosa data-retention`

Keep or delete a sandbox's data when it stops.

```
miosa data-retention
```

An organization keeps a stopped sandbox's disk (mode "keep", the default), so it can be
resumed later.
With zero data retention (mode "delete_on_stop") nothing is kept once a
sandbox stops: a stop destroys the sandbox and its disk, a sandbox stopped for idleness
is destroyed shortly after, and pauses and snapshots are refused.
Only organization
owners and administrators can change it.

```
  miosa data-retention status
  miosa data-retention enable --yes
  miosa data-retention disable
```

#### `miosa data-retention disable`

Keep a stopped sandbox's disk again (the default).

```
miosa data-retention disable
```

#### `miosa data-retention enable`

Delete a sandbox's data when it stops (zero retention).

```
miosa data-retention enable [flags]
```

Turn on zero data retention.
From then on a stop destroys the sandbox and its disk,
and pauses and snapshots are refused.
Sandboxes already stopped are not touched.
--yes is required.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Confirm that stops will destroy sandboxes.

#### `miosa data-retention status`

Show the organization's data retention.

```
miosa data-retention status
```

### `miosa db`

Managed databases.

```
miosa db
```

Databases run on MIOSA and bill while running. <database> is a name or id.
'connection' prints the connection secret: treat the output like a password.

Aliases: `database`, `databases`.

#### `miosa db backup`

Take a backup now.

```
miosa db backup <database>
```

#### `miosa db backups`

List backups.

```
miosa db backups <database>
```

#### `miosa db connection`

Show how to connect (a secret).

```
miosa db connection <database>
```

Aliases: `url`.

#### `miosa db create`

Create a database.

```
miosa db create <name> [flags]
```

Create a managed database.

```
  miosa db create app-db --engine postgres --version 16 --size small
  miosa db create app-db --extensions vector,pg_trgm,pgcrypto --workspace tz
  miosa db create app-db --project shop

```
--extensions applies to PostgreSQL only; allowed: vector, pg_trgm, pgcrypto, uuid-ossp,
pg_stat_statements, citext, hstore, btree_gin, btree_gist. --version is a version with an
image (postgres 16, mysql 8, redis 7, mongodb 7). --size is one of tiny, xs, small, medium, large.

--project adds the database to a project, where its services can reference it; it is
created in the project's workspace unless --workspace says otherwise.

--engine optimal creates an Optimal Engine; 'miosa engine create' is the same call with
the Engine-specific flags and an idempotent name.

Flags:

- `--class string` - Data classification: public, internal, confidential or restricted (public|internal|confidential|restricted).
- `--engine string` - Engine: postgres, mysql, redis, mongodb, qdrant or optimal (postgres|postgresql|mysql|redis|mongodb|qdrant|optimal). (default `postgres`)
- `--extensions stringArray` - PostgreSQL extensions to enable at create, comma separated.
- `--project string` - Add it to this project (name, slug or id), in the project's workspace.
- `--region string` - Pin to a region.
- `--size string` - Size: tiny, xs, small, medium or large (tiny|xs|small|medium|large).
- `--version string` - Engine version (default: the engine's current one).
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug, name or id (default: $MIOSA_WORKSPACE, then the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa db get`

Show a database.

```
miosa db get <database>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa db list`

List databases.

```
miosa db list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--all-orgs` - List every organization your sign-in covers, with an ORG column.
- `--workspace string` - Only this workspace (slug, name or id).

#### `miosa db logs`

Show the database log (the last run of one that failed).

```
miosa db logs <database> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-f, --follow` - Keep streaming new lines until Ctrl-C.
- `-n, --tail int` - How many lines.

Examples:

```
miosa db logs my-db -n 200
  miosa db logs my-db --follow
```

#### `miosa db public`

Show, turn on or turn off a database's public endpoint.

```
miosa db public <database> [on|off] [flags]
```

Without on/off, prints the state.
Turning it on opens a TCP endpoint on a
port from 30000-39999, one per database, and prints the host and port.

```
  miosa db public app-db
  miosa db public app-db on
  miosa db public app-db on --allow 203.0.113.0/24 --allow 198.51.100.7
  miosa db public app-db off

```
The allowlist REPLACES the stored one; on with no --allow means any source.
TLS
is required (Postgres needs sslmode=require), the certificate is self-signed,
and the allowlist closes disallowed sources before any TLS is attempted.
MySQL,
Postgres, Redis and MongoDB are supported; other engines answer
ENGINE_NOT_SUPPORTED.

Flags:

- `--allow stringArray` - Allowed source: an IP or CIDR (repeatable; empty means any source).

#### `miosa db recover`

Restore a backup into the database, or into a new one.

```
miosa db recover <database> [flags]
```

Restore a backup (also available as "miosa db restore").

```
  miosa db recover app-db --to-new app-db-restored     # safe: a new database, the source is untouched
  miosa db recover app-db --backup 778439c0 --to-new app-db-restored
  miosa db recover app-db --backup 778439c0 --yes      # over app-db itself

```
--backup is a backup id (see 'miosa db backups'); without it the newest completed
backup is used. --to-new creates a database of the same engine, version and size
in the same workspace, waits for it to run, then restores into it.
Restoring over
the source replaces its data, so it asks first (or pass --yes).

A Redis restore is a merge: keys in the backup replace the same-named keys and
other keys stay.
A backup only restores into a database of the same engine.

Flags:

- `--backup string` - Backup id (default: the newest completed backup).
- `--to-new string` - Create a new database with this name and restore into it.
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask before restoring over the source.

#### `miosa db restart`

Restart a database.

```
miosa db restart <database>
```

#### `miosa db rm`

Permanently delete a database.

```
miosa db rm <database> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa db start`

Start a stopped database.

```
miosa db start <database>
```

#### `miosa db stop`

Stop a database.

```
miosa db stop <database> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa db wake`

Wake a database that scaled to zero.

```
miosa db wake <database>
```

### `miosa deploy`

Create and operate deployments.

```
miosa deploy [flags]
```

Deployments are the running, versioned apps published from sandboxes, git or
uploads. <deployment> is a name, slug or id.

```
  miosa deploy                          deploy the current directory
  miosa deploy create --from-sandbox my-box --wait
  miosa deploy create --git https://github.com/me/app
  miosa deploy list
  miosa deploy get my-app
  miosa deploy logs my-app -n 100
  miosa deploy rollback my-app --to <version-id>
  miosa deploy domains my-app
```

Aliases: `deployments`, `deployment`.

Flags:

- `--ai-access string` - Whether agents may read the published site: allow (the default) or block.
- `--branch string` - Git branch to build (default: the current one).
- `--deployment string` - Push a new version to this existing deployment (id, name or hostname).
- `--domain string` - Custom domain to attach (then add the DNS record it shows).
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--force` - Upload even when the files match the latest version.
- `--name string` - Deployment name (default: the argument, the repo or the directory name).
- `--no-promote` - Upload the version without making it live; prints the version to promote later.
- `--no-wait` - Return as soon as the deployment is created instead of waiting until it is live.
- `--wait` - Wait until the deployment is live (the default; kept for scripts).
- `--wait-timeout duration` - How long to wait for the deployment to go live. (default `10m0s`)
- `--yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa deploy builds`

List builds.

```
miosa deploy builds <deployment>
```

Lists the builds of a repository or sandbox deployment.
Uploaded static sites are not built, so they have none.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy builds my-app
```

#### `miosa deploy create`

Create and publish a deployment from a sandbox, a git repo or a directory.

```
miosa deploy create [name] [flags]
```

Create a new deployment and make it live.

Pick exactly one source:

```
  --from-sandbox <sandbox>   Publish a running sandbox's output.
  --git <url>                Connect a git repository and build it.
  --dir <path>               Upload a local directory: built static output, or a
                             server app with --run-command (and --port).

```
With --from-sandbox, --dir is where the app lives inside the sandbox (default
/workspace).
On its own, --dir is a local directory to package and upload.
A
sandbox publish is static by default; give --run-command (or --type dynamic)
to publish a running server, or --type docker_deploy for App Engine.

```
  miosa deploy create --from-sandbox my-box --wait
  miosa deploy create --from-sandbox my-box --port 3000 --run-command "npm start"
  miosa deploy create --git https://github.com/me/app --branch main --wait
  miosa deploy create --dir ./dist --name my-site

```
--env sets environment variables (repeatable, encrypted at rest). --domain
attaches a custom domain; 'miosa deploy domains <deployment>' shows the DNS
record to add. --wait blocks until the deployment is live and prints its URL.
