Aliases: `publish`.

Flags:

- `--ai-access string` - Whether agents may read the published site: allow (the default) or block.
- `--branch string` - Git branch to build (default: the current one).
- `--build-command string` - Build command (git or Forge source, or a --from-sandbox server publish).
- `--deployment string` - Push a new version to this existing deployment (id, name or hostname).
- `--dir string` - With --from-sandbox: the app's path inside the sandbox. On its own: a local directory to upload.
- `--domain string` - Custom domain to attach (then add the DNS record it shows).
- `--entrypoint string` - With --from-sandbox: the entry file for a static publish.
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--force` - Upload even when the files match the latest version.
- `--forge string` - Deploy this Forge repository (id, org/name or name); builds --branch and redeploys on every push.
- `--from-sandbox string` - Publish this sandbox (name, id or 'current').
- `--git string` - Deploy this git repository URL.
- `--image string` - App Engine container image (not exposed by the public API; see --type docker_deploy).
- `--name string` - Deployment name (default: the argument, the repo or the directory name).
- `--no-auto` - With --forge: do not build automatically on pushes to the branch.
- `--no-promote` - Upload the version without making it live; prints the version to promote later.
- `--no-wait` - Return as soon as the deployment is created instead of waiting until it is live.
- `--port int` - The port the app serves on (--from-sandbox, or --dir with --run-command).
- `--project string` - Project to put the deployment in (name, slug or id); with --forge, the project to link the repository through (one per project).
- `--root-directory string` - With --forge: the app's directory inside the repository.
- `--run-command string` - Command the app starts with (makes the publish dynamic).
- `--type string` - Runtime to publish as: static, dynamic or docker_deploy (default: static, or dynamic with --run-command).
- `--wait` - Wait until the deployment is live (the default; kept for scripts).
- `--wait-timeout duration` - How long to wait for the deployment to go live. (default `10m0s`)
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug, name or id (default: $MIOSA_WORKSPACE, then the profile's default workspace).
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa deploy diff`

Compare two versions file by file.

```
miosa deploy diff <version> <version> [flags]
```

Compare what two versions of a deployment ship.

```
  miosa deploy diff 4bacc5df 7093400e                       # in a linked folder
  miosa deploy diff 4bacc5df 7093400e --deployment my-site  # from anywhere

```
Each file the second version adds, changes or drops is listed with its size, so
a version that went out bigger than expected can be traced to the files that
did it.
The deployment comes from --deployment, or from the .miosa/deployment.json
a first deploy wrote in the folder (--dir, default the current directory).

Flags:

- `--deployment string` - The deployment both versions belong to (default: the folder link).
- `--dir string` - Folder whose .miosa/deployment.json names the deployment (default: the current directory).

#### `miosa deploy domain-add`

Attach a custom domain (then add the DNS record it shows).

```
miosa deploy domain-add <deployment> <hostname> [flags]
```

Attaches the hostname and prints the DNS records to add. --dns chooses what
to do with them:

```
  --dns zone        print them as DNS zone-file lines to paste (BIND syntax)
  --dns cloudflare  create them in your Cloudflare zone, using the value of
                    CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN (needs Zone:Read and DNS:Edit). It is
                    used by this command only and is never sent to MIOSA.

```
--redirect-to H makes this host answer with a permanent redirect to H, keeping
the path and query.
H must be this name with or without "www." and must also be
attached to the deployment and verified, or nothing is redirected.

Registrars without a records API (Namecheap's replaces a whole zone and needs an
allow-listed IP) are not automated: use --dns zone and paste the lines.

Flags:

- `--dns string` - What to do with the DNS records: zone | cloudflare (zone|cloudflare).
- `--redirect-to string` - Redirect this host to the other form of the same name (www.example.com -> example.com or the reverse).

Examples:

```
miosa deploy domain-add my-app www.example.com --dns cloudflare
  miosa deploy domain-add my-app www.example.com --redirect-to example.com
```

#### `miosa deploy domain-move`

Move a custom domain to another deployment (no DNS change).

```
miosa deploy domain-move <domain> [target] [flags]
```

Repoints a domain you already verified at a different deployment.
The
verification, TLS and redirect settings move with it, so you change no DNS
record and verify nothing again; the new deployment serves it within seconds.

<domain> is the hostname.
The target deployment (id, slug or name) is the second
argument, or --to; give it one way or the other, not both.

Flags:

- `--to string` - Deployment to move the domain to (id, slug or name).

Examples:

```
miosa deploy domain-move lunivate.com lunivate-site-v2
  miosa deploy domain-move lunivate.com --to lunivate-site-v2
```

#### `miosa deploy domain-rm`

Detach a custom domain.

```
miosa deploy domain-rm <deployment> <domain-id> [flags]
```

Detaches the domain: it stops serving this deployment.
To serve it from another deployment without touching DNS, use 'miosa deploy domain-move' instead. <domain-id> is the id from 'miosa deploy domains'.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy domains my-app
  miosa deploy domain-rm my-app 3f2a91c0
```

#### `miosa deploy domain-update`

Set or clear the redirect on a custom domain that is already attached.

```
miosa deploy domain-update <hostname> [flags]
```

Changes a custom domain in place, without removing and re-adding it (no DNS
change, no re-verification).

--redirect-to H makes this host answer with a permanent redirect to H, keeping
the path and query.
H must be this name with or without "www." and must also be
attached to the same deployment and verified. --no-redirect makes the host serve
the site again.

Flags:

- `--no-redirect` - Stop redirecting: this host serves the site.
- `--redirect-to string` - Redirect this host to the other form of the same name (www.example.com <-> example.com).

Examples:

```
miosa deploy domain-update www.example.com --redirect-to example.com
  miosa deploy domain-update www.example.com --no-redirect
```

#### `miosa deploy domain-verify`

Check a domain's DNS (--wait: until it is live).

```
miosa deploy domain-verify <deployment> <domain-id> [flags]
```

Checks the domain's DNS once.
With --wait it keeps checking every 10 seconds
until DNS and the TLS certificate are right and the site answers, then prints
"live https://...".
On every check that fails it prints the exact record: type,
name, what is expected and what was observed.
On timeout it prints what is still
missing and exits non-zero. <domain-id> may be the hostname when using --wait.

Flags:

- `--timeout string` - With --wait, give up after this long (for example 15m). (default `10m`)
- `--wait` - Wait until the domain is live (DNS + TLS + serving).

Examples:

```
miosa deploy domain-verify my-app www.example.com --wait --timeout 15m
```

#### `miosa deploy domains`

List custom domains.

```
miosa deploy domains <deployment>
```

Lists the custom domains attached to the deployment, with their DNS verification and serving status.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy domains my-app
  miosa deploy domains 7699e13d
  miosa deploy domains example.com
```

#### `miosa deploy env`

List the deployment's environment variable names.

```
miosa deploy env <deployment> [flags]
```

Lists the names of the deployment's environment variables and when each changed.
Values are encrypted at rest and never printed.

Flags:

- `-e, --environment string` - Environment: production (default), preview or development (production|preview|development).

Examples:

```
miosa deploy env my-app
```

#### `miosa deploy env-pull`

Write the deployment's environment variables to a local .env file.

```
miosa deploy env-pull <deployment> [flags]
```

Downloads the deployment's environment variable values into a dotenv file
(default .env.local, written with mode 0600) so the app runs locally with the
same configuration.
Only an owner or admin of the organization can, signed in
as themselves ('miosa login'); machine, CI and sandbox keys are refused, and
the server audits every variable name pulled.

The values go to the file only: the command prints names, never values.
An
existing file is kept unless you pass --force.

Flags:

- `--file string` - File to write. (default `.env.local`)
- `--force` - Overwrite the file if it exists.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy env-pull my-app
  miosa deploy env-pull my-app --file .env --force
```

#### `miosa deploy env-rm`

Remove environment variables (comma-separated names).

```
miosa deploy env-rm <deployment> <name> [flags]
```

Removes the named variables.
Like env-set, a production change cuts a new release on the server and prints it; pass --no-release to remove them without cutting one.
A name that is not set is reported, not an error.

Aliases: `env-unset`.

Flags:

- `-e, --environment string` - Environment: production (default), preview or development (production|preview|development).
- `--no-release` - Store the change without cutting a release; it applies at the next release.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy env-rm my-app DEBUG
  miosa deploy env-rm my-app OLD_TOKEN,LEGACY_URL
```

#### `miosa deploy env-set`

Set the deployment's environment variables (values are encrypted at rest).

```
miosa deploy env-set <deployment> [flags]
```

Sets one or more environment variables.
A production change cuts a new release on the server and prints it (id and state); the variables are live once that release is.
Pass --no-release to store them without cutting one: they apply at the next release ('miosa deploy redeploy' or a new push).

Flags:

- `-e, --environment string` - Environment: production (default), preview or development (production|preview|development).
- `--no-release` - Store the change without cutting a release; it applies at the next release.
- `--set stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).

Examples:

```
miosa deploy env-set my-app --set DATABASE_URL=postgres://... --set FEATURE=on
  miosa deploy env-set my-app --set FEATURE=off --no-release
```

#### `miosa deploy get`

Show a deployment.

```
miosa deploy get <deployment>
```

Show a deployment and what it is running.

```
  miosa deploy get my-app

```
<deployment> is a name, slug, hostname or id.
Kind is the active version's
kind: static, or dynamic for a deployment that runs a server.
A dynamic or
App Engine deployment also shows the image, command and port it runs.

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa deploy list`

List deployments.

```
miosa deploy list [flags]
```

Lists your deployments with their state and URL.
Any of the name, short id, hostname or id shown here works as <deployment> in the other commands.

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--all-orgs` - List every organization your sign-in covers, with an ORG column.
- `-n, --limit int` - How many.
- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy list
  miosa deploy ls --workspace-id <id> -n 20
```

#### `miosa deploy logs`

Show the running app's log.

```
miosa deploy logs <deployment> [flags]
```

Prints the most recent lines the deployment's process wrote.
Static sites (uploaded files) run no process, so they have no runtime log.

--follow then streams new lines until Ctrl-C: a build in progress is followed until it finishes, a running app's stdout and stderr until you stop.

Flags:

- `-f, --follow` - Keep streaming new lines until Ctrl-C.
- `-n, --lines int` - Number of lines.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy logs my-app
  miosa deploy logs my-app -n 200
  miosa deploy logs my-app --follow
```

#### `miosa deploy observe`

Invocations, errors and latency.

```
miosa deploy observe <deployment>
```

Summarizes the deployment's traffic: invocations, errors, error rate, p50/p95 latency and cold starts.
Latency is not measured for static sites and shows n/a.

Aliases: `metrics`.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy observe my-app
  miosa deploy observe my-app --json
```

#### `miosa deploy promote`

Make a version the live one.

```
miosa deploy promote <version> [flags]
```

Put a version on the air.

```
  miosa deploy promote 4bacc5df                        # in a linked folder
  miosa deploy promote 4bacc5df --deployment my-site  # from anywhere
  miosa deploy promote my-site <release-id>           # promote a release (full id, or a unique prefix of 6+ characters)

```
A version uploaded with 'miosa deploy push --no-promote' is not serving yet.
This makes it live; the deployment keeps its id, URL and domains, and the
previous version stays available to roll back to.

The deployment comes from --deployment, or from the .miosa/deployment.json a
first deploy wrote in the folder (--dir, default the current directory).

Flags:

- `--deployment string` - The deployment the version belongs to (default: the folder link).
- `--dir string` - Folder whose .miosa/deployment.json names the deployment (default: the current directory).

#### `miosa deploy prove`

Check end to end that a deployment is live and serving.

```
miosa deploy prove <deployment>
```

Asks the platform to prove the deployment: it exists, is running, has a public URL, and (for App Engine apps) its host and runtime answer.
The CLI then requests the public URL itself and checks its status and TLS certificate.
Exits 1 when any check fails, so a script or CI step can gate on it.

Aliases: `proof`.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy prove my-app
  miosa deploy prove my-app --json
```

#### `miosa deploy purge-cache`

Drop the edge cache of a static deployment.

```
miosa deploy purge-cache <deployment>
```

Drops every cached file of the deployment's versions, here and on every edge node.
Promotes and rollbacks already serve new bytes (files are cached by content), so this is for forcing a refresh.

Aliases: `cache-purge`.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy purge-cache my-site
```

#### `miosa deploy push`

Upload a new version to an existing deployment.

```
miosa deploy push <deployment> [flags]
```

Push a new version of a local directory to an existing deployment.

```
  miosa deploy push my-site --dir ./dist
  miosa deploy push lunivate.com            # a hostname works too

```
The deployment keeps its id, URL and domains; only a new version is released.
This is what 'miosa deploy' does automatically once a folder is linked: every
deploy and push writes or refreshes .miosa/deployment.json in the folder (and in
the project folder when --dir points inside it).
A push whose files match the
latest version uploads nothing; --force uploads anyway.

Flags:

- `--dir string` - Local directory to upload (default: the current directory).
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--force` - Upload even when the files match the latest version.
- `--no-promote` - Upload the version without making it live; prints the version to promote later.
- `--no-wait` - Return as soon as the version is uploaded instead of waiting until it is live.
- `--wait` - Wait until the new version is live (the default; kept for scripts).
- `--wait-timeout duration` - How long to wait for the version to go live. (default `10m0s`)

#### `miosa deploy redeploy`

Build and release again from the same source.

```
miosa deploy redeploy <deployment> [flags]
```

Rebuilds the deployment from its configured source and releases the result.

The command prints the build it starts.
A failed build marks the deployment failed and records the
reason: 'miosa deploy get <deployment>' reports it and 'miosa deploy builds <deployment>' lists the
run.
A deployment published from a sandbox has no repository to rebuild, so it re-runs the release it
already has.
An uploaded static site has nothing to rebuild: push a new version with
'miosa deploy push'.

Flags:

- `--commit string` - Forge deployments: build this commit instead of the branch head.
- `--wait` - Wait until the redeployed version is live (10 minutes).

Examples:

```
miosa deploy redeploy my-app
  miosa deploy redeploy my-app --commit 1a2b3c4d
```

#### `miosa deploy releases`

List releases.

```
miosa deploy releases <deployment>
```

Lists the deployment's releases: each time a version was released to serve. 'miosa deploy versions' lists the uploaded versions and marks the live one.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy releases my-app
  miosa deploy versions my-app
```

#### `miosa deploy requests`

Show recent HTTP requests.

```
miosa deploy requests <deployment>
```

Lists recent HTTP requests the deployment served: time, method, path, status and duration.
Requests to static sites are not logged yet; this says so instead of showing an empty table.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy requests my-app
  miosa deploy requests my-app --json
```

#### `miosa deploy rm`

Delete a deployment.

```
miosa deploy rm <deployment> [flags]
```

Deletes the deployment, its versions and its URLs.
Custom domains attached
to it stop serving, so rm lists them and refuses while any are attached: move
them first with 'miosa deploy domain-move <domain> <other-deployment>', or pass
--with-domains to delete the deployment and detach them anyway.

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `--with-domains` - Delete even though custom domains are attached (they go offline).
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy rm old-site
  miosa deploy domain-move example.com new-site && miosa deploy rm old-site
```

#### `miosa deploy rollback`

Make an earlier version live again.

```
miosa deploy rollback <deployment> [flags]
```

Switches the live version back to the one you name with --to (a version
number, short id or full id from 'miosa deploy versions').
Before asking for
confirmation it prints the version live now and the exact version it will
switch to.
Without --to it lists the versions and changes nothing: it never
guesses which one you meant.

Flags:

- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--to string` - Version to make live: number, short id or id (see 'miosa deploy versions').
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy versions my-app
  miosa deploy rollback my-app --to 67cf439d
  miosa deploy rollback my-app --to 3 --yes
```

#### `miosa deploy start`

Start a stopped deployment from its last release.

```
miosa deploy start <deployment>
```

Brings a stopped deployment back from the release it was serving, at its current size.
Nothing is built or uploaded, so it also works for an uploaded deployment, which has no source to redeploy from.
A static site is live again at once; a server reads 'building' until its instance is healthy.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy start my-site
```

#### `miosa deploy stop`

Stop a deployment.

```
miosa deploy stop <deployment> [flags]
```

Stops serving traffic.
The deployment, its versions and domains are kept; 'miosa deploy redeploy' or a new push brings it back.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy stop my-app
  miosa deploy stop my-app --yes
```

#### `miosa deploy update`

Change a deployment's source, branch, build or agent access.

```
miosa deploy update <deployment> [flags]
```

Changes only what you pass.

--forge attaches a Forge repository to an uploaded deployment (or moves the
deployment to another repository). --auto builds and releases a new version on
every push or merge to the branch; --no-auto stops that (use 'miosa deploy
redeploy' to build by hand).

--size small|medium sets the runtime size of the deployment's services (small is
2 vCPU / 4 GiB, medium is 4 vCPU / 8 GiB).
A running instance keeps the size it
started with; the next release or restart uses the new one.

--ai-access allow|block says whether agents may read the published site.
allow (the default) serves a generated robots.txt and llms.txt unless your files
have their own. block asks agents to stay away (robots.txt, X-Robots-Tag) and
answers 403 to a request whose User-Agent names one; that check is UA-based, so
it is a strong request, not a guarantee.

Aliases: `settings`.

Flags:

- `--ai-access string` - Whether agents may read the published site: allow or block.
- `--auto` - Build and release on every push/merge to the branch.
- `--branch string` - Branch to build.
- `--build-command string` - Build command.
- `--forge string` - Forge repository to build from (id, org/name or name).
- `--no-auto` - Stop building on pushes.
- `--root-directory string` - The app's directory inside the repository.
- `--size string` - Runtime size: small or medium.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy update my-site --forge my-repo --branch main --auto
  miosa deploy update my-site --no-auto
  miosa deploy update my-site --build-command 'npm run build' --root-directory web
  miosa deploy update my-app --size small
  miosa deploy settings my-site --ai-access block
```

#### `miosa deploy versions`

List versions, with what each one ships and which is live.

```
miosa deploy versions <deployment>
```

List a deployment's versions.

```
  miosa deploy versions my-app

```
Each row carries the version number, its kind, how many files it ships and how
big they are, the artifact sha256, and "live" on the version that is serving.

Compare two of them with:

```
  miosa deploy diff <version> <version>
```

##### `miosa deploy versions rm`

Archive a version that is not live (also: versions archive).

```
miosa deploy versions rm <version> [flags]
```

Archives one version (number, short id or id) of the deployment named by
--deployment, or of the deployment this folder is linked to.
Archiving keeps the
row, its logs and its artifact for audit, takes it off the version list and out of
static storage, and it can no longer be promoted.
A version id on its own finds
its deployment.
The live version is refused, and so is any version the server
still needs, such as the rollback target.

Aliases: `archive`.

Flags:

- `--deployment string` - Deployment the version belongs to (default: the folder's linked deployment).
- `--dir string` - Folder whose .miosa/deployment.json names the deployment.
- `-y, --yes` - Delete without asking.

Examples:

```
miosa deploy versions rm 90a9dee1
  miosa deploy versions rm 3 --deployment my-site --yes
```

### `miosa desktop`

Start and open a sandbox's remote desktop.

```
miosa desktop
```

A sandbox built from a desktop template can serve a graphical desktop. 'desktop
start' launches it and prints the stream URL; 'desktop url' prints it again.
For
full computers see 'miosa computer urls'.

```
  miosa desktop start my-box
  miosa desktop url my-box
```

#### `miosa desktop start`

Start the desktop and print its URL.

```
miosa desktop start [sandbox]
```

#### `miosa desktop url`

Print the running desktop's URL.

```
miosa desktop url [sandbox]
```

Aliases: `status`, `get`.

### `miosa destroy`

Permanently destroy sandboxes.

```
miosa destroy [name|id...] [flags]
```

Permanently destroy one or more sandboxes.
This cannot be undone.

On a terminal it asks first.
In scripts pass --yes (--json never prompts).
With no name the current sandbox (set with 'miosa use') is destroyed.

```
  miosa destroy my-box
  miosa destroy a b c --yes
  miosa destroy          # the current sandbox
  miosa list --ids --state paused | xargs miosa destroy --yes
```

Aliases: `delete`, `rm`.

Flags:

- `-f, --force` - Same as --yes.
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

### `miosa device`

Every machine you can run work on, across runtime kinds.

```
miosa device
```

A device is any runtime MIOSA can run a command on: a sandbox, a computer or
another kind. <device> is an id.

```
  miosa device list
  miosa device list --status running
  miosa device capabilities <device>
  miosa device exec <device> -- uname -a
```

Aliases: `devices`.

#### `miosa device browser`

Show the URL of a device's browser (computers).

```
miosa device browser <device>
```

#### `miosa device capabilities`

What this device can do.

```
miosa device capabilities <device>
```

Aliases: `caps`.

#### `miosa device exec`

Run a command on a device.

```
miosa device exec <device> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-c, --command string` - Shell command to run.
- `--timeout-ms int` - Time limit in milliseconds.

#### `miosa device expose`

Publish a port of a device.

```
miosa device expose <device> <port>
```

#### `miosa device extend`

Replace a device's time to live.

```
miosa device extend <device> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--timeout string` - New time to live, for example 30m or 2h.

#### `miosa device files`

List a directory on a device.

```
miosa device files <device> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--path string` - Directory (default /workspace).

#### `miosa device get`

Show one device.

```
miosa device get <device>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa device list`

List devices.

```
miosa device list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--errored` - Include machines in the error state.
- `-n, --limit int` - How many.
- `--name string` - Only devices whose name contains this.
- `--status string` - Only this state.

#### `miosa device pause`

Pause a device.

```
miosa device pause <device>
```

#### `miosa device read`

Read a file (base64) from a device.

```
miosa device read <device> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--path string` - Path to read.

#### `miosa device resume`

Resume a paused device.

```
miosa device resume <device>
```

#### `miosa device rm`

Permanently destroy a device.

```
miosa device rm <device> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`, `destroy`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa device stop`

Stop a device, keeping its disk.

```
miosa device stop <device>
```

#### `miosa device write`

Write a file on a device.

```
miosa device write <device> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--content string` - File contents (or pass a JSON body with --file).
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--path string` - Path to write.

### `miosa doctor`

Diagnose configuration, connectivity and authentication.

```
miosa doctor
```

Run quick checks and say what to fix: CLI version, config file, the API endpoint,
your key, and clock skew (a skewed clock breaks signed webhooks and tokens).
Exit status is non-zero when a check fails.

### `miosa domain`

Custom domains of the linked service.

```
miosa domain
```

Add, check, verify and remove custom domains on the linked service.

```
  miosa link cliniciq --service API
  miosa domain add app.example.com
  miosa domain list
  miosa domain verify app.example.com --wait
  miosa domain remove app.example.com

```
A domain cannot end in miosa.ai.
The DNS records to create are printed on add
and whenever a verify is still pending.

#### `miosa domain add`

Add a custom domain to the linked service.

```
miosa domain add <fqdn> [flags]
```

Add a custom domain and print the DNS records to create.
With --wait it then
polls verify until the domain is active or failed.

```
  miosa domain add app.example.com
  miosa domain add app.example.com --redirect to-primary --wait
```

Flags:

- `--no-wait` - Add and return without checking DNS.
- `--redirect string` - Redirect policy for this domain.

#### `miosa domain list`

List the linked service's custom domains.

```
miosa domain list
```

#### `miosa domain remove`

Remove a custom domain from the linked service.

```
miosa domain remove <fqdn|id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--yes` - Do not ask.

#### `miosa domain verify`

Check a domain's DNS now.

```
miosa domain verify <fqdn|id> [flags]
```

Ask the API to verify the domain.
Active is immediate when DNS is right; a
pending answer prints the records that are still missing. --wait polls until the
domain is active or failed.

Flags:

- `--no-wait` - Check once and return.

### `miosa drains`

Send a Deploy target's logs to your own endpoint.

```
miosa drains
```

A drain forwards runtime and build logs to an endpoint you control: HTTPS as
one JSON object per line, or syslog over TLS.

```
  miosa drains list
  miosa drains add --name ops --url https://logs.example.test/ingest
  miosa drains add --name archive --kind syslog --url syslog+tls://logs.example.org:6514
  miosa drains test ops
  miosa drains pause ops
  miosa drains rotate-secret ops
  miosa drains rm ops

```
An HTTPS drain gets a signing secret once, on add and on rotate: copy it then,
it is not shown again.
A syslog drain has no secret, because TLS is the
authenticity.

Aliases: `drain`.

Flags:


#### `miosa drains add`

Create a drain and show its secret once.

```
miosa drains add --name <name> --url <url> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--kind string` - Kind: http (default) or syslog. (default `http`)
- `--name string` - Drain name: 1-63 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens.
- `--source stringArray` - Which logs: runtime, build (repeatable; default both).
- `--url string` - Endpoint: https://... or, with --kind syslog, syslog+tls://host[:port].

#### `miosa drains list`

List the drains of a Deploy target.

```
miosa drains list
```

#### `miosa drains pause`

Stop delivering lines, keeping the drain.

```
miosa drains pause <name>
```

#### `miosa drains resume`

Deliver lines again.

```
miosa drains resume <name>
```

#### `miosa drains rm`

Delete a drain.

```
miosa drains rm <name>
```

Aliases: `delete`, `remove`.

#### `miosa drains rotate-secret`

Replace an HTTPS drain's signing secret.

```
miosa drains rotate-secret <name>
```

#### `miosa drains test`

Send one signed line now and report what the endpoint answered.

```
miosa drains test <name>
```

### `miosa egress`

Outbound network policy for sandboxes.

```
miosa egress
```

Egress policies decide which hosts sandboxes may call. 'audit' shows what was
contacted; a policy in audit_only mode records without blocking.

Aliases: `network`.

#### `miosa egress allowlist`

List allowed hosts of a policy.

```
miosa egress allowlist [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--policy string` - Policy id.

#### `miosa egress audit`

Show recent outbound connections.

```
miosa egress audit [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many.

#### `miosa egress policies`

List egress policies.

```
miosa egress policies
```

#### `miosa egress suggestions`

Suggest allowlist entries from observed traffic.

```
miosa egress suggestions
```

### `miosa email`

Send email from your apps through MIOSA.

```
miosa email
```

Add a sending domain, publish the DNS records it returns (SPF, DKIM, DMARC),
verify it, then send from any address on it.
Bounces and complaints add the
recipient to the organization's suppression list and are sent to your webhooks
in the email category (email.delivered, email.bounced, email.complained, ...).

```
  miosa email domains add mail.example.com
  miosa email domains verify mail.example.com
  miosa email send --from hello@mail.example.com --to you@example.org --subject Hi --text "Hello"
  miosa email messages

```
A key that may only send is minted with the email:send scope.

Aliases: `mail`.

#### `miosa email domains`

Sending domains and their DNS records.

```
miosa email domains
```

'add' prints the SPF, DKIM and DMARC records to publish.
After they resolve, 'verify'
checks them; mail can be sent only from a verified domain. <domain> is a domain name or an id.

##### `miosa email domains add`

Add a sending domain and print the DNS records to publish.

```
miosa email domains add <domain>
```

##### `miosa email domains list`

List sending domains.

```
miosa email domains list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa email domains records`

Show a domain's DNS records and their state.

```
miosa email domains records <domain>
```

##### `miosa email domains rm`

Remove a sending domain.

```
miosa email domains rm <domain> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa email domains verify`

Check the published DNS records.

```
miosa email domains verify <domain>
```

#### `miosa email limits`

Daily cap, sent today and whether sending is paused.

```
miosa email limits
```

#### `miosa email message`

Show one message with its delivery events.

```
miosa email message <id>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa email messages`

List sent messages and their delivery state.

```
miosa email messages [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--limit int` - How many to return (default 50, at most 200).
- `--status string` - Only messages in this state: queued, sent, delivered, delayed, bounced, complained or failed.

#### `miosa email send`

Send an email from a verified domain.

```
miosa email send [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--attach stringArray` - Attach a file (repeat; 10 MB in total).
- `--bcc stringArray` - Bcc recipient.
- `--cc stringArray` - Cc recipient.
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--from string` - Sender address on a verified domain, 'Name <addr>' allowed.
- `--html string` - HTML body.
- `--html-file string` - Read the HTML body from a file.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--reply-to stringArray` - Reply-To address.
- `--subject string` - Subject line.
- `--tag stringArray` - Tag as NAME=VALUE (repeat).
- `--text string` - Plain-text body.
- `--text-file string` - Read the plain-text body from a file.
- `--to stringArray` - Recipient (repeat or comma-separate).

Examples:

```
miosa email send --from hello@mail.example.com --to you@example.org --subject "Hi" --text "Hello"
  miosa email send --from hello@mail.example.com --to a@example.org,b@example.org --subject "Report" --html-file report.html --attach report.pdf
  miosa email send --file message.json --idempotency-key order-1042
```

#### `miosa email suppress`

Never send to an address.

```
miosa email suppress <email>
```

#### `miosa email suppressions`

List addresses mail is not sent to.

```
miosa email suppressions
```

Aliases: `suppressed`.

#### `miosa email unsuppress`

Remove an address from the suppression list.

```
miosa email unsuppress <id>
```

### `miosa engine`

Operate an Optimal Engine: managed Engines on MIOSA, and two-Engine sync.

```
miosa engine
```

Managed Engines run on MIOSA as managed databases with engine "optimal":
create one, read it, list them.
They are the same objects 'miosa db' shows.

The sync commands talk to Engine endpoints directly.
They take the two Engine
URLs and an admin key per Engine.
The admin key is used to mint a
workspace-scoped replica key on each side, so the peer key each Engine stores
can read and sync one workspace and nothing else.

#### `miosa engine attach`

Attach a workspace to an Optimal Engine.

```
miosa engine attach <workspace> --to <org|engine> [flags]
```

Attaches a workspace to an Optimal Engine and gives every sandbox, computer,
deployment and agent run in that workspace the Engine URL and a workspace-scoped
key, through the platform's secret injection.
There is nothing to copy into the
workspace.

--to org uses the organization's Engine.
When no Engine is the organization's
yet and exactly one managed Engine exists, that Engine becomes the
organization's, which is the default for a new organization.

A workspace is served by one Engine at a time.
Attaching it to a different one
is refused with the Engine it is already on; pass --replace to move it, which
only an organization owner or admin may do.

```
  miosa engine attach kii --to org
  miosa engine attach kii --to qa-brain
  miosa engine attach kii --to qa-brain --replace
  miosa engine attach kii --to org --json | jq '{scope, engine_workspace_slug}'
```

Flags:

- `--replace` - Move a workspace already attached to another Engine (owners and admins only).
- `--to string` - Engine name or id, or 'org' for the organization's Engine (required).

#### `miosa engine backup`

Take a backup of a managed Optimal Engine now.

```
miosa engine backup <engine>
```

Starts a backup of the Engine's data volume and prints its id and state.

```
  miosa engine backup qa-brain
  miosa engine backups qa-brain          # what exists already
```

#### `miosa engine backups`

List a managed Optimal Engine's backups.

```
miosa engine backups <engine>
```

Lists the Engine's backups, newest first, with the state each one reached.

```
  miosa engine backups qa-brain
  miosa engine backups qa-brain --json | jq '.data[] | {id, state, created_at}'
```

Aliases: `backup-list`.

#### `miosa engine connect`

Write an optimal CLI profile for an Engine workspace.

```
miosa engine connect <engine> [flags]
```

Mints a read/write key for one workspace of an Engine and writes an 'optimal'
CLI profile (url, key, workspace) so 'optimal' and 'oe' reach the Engine through
MIOSA.
The key expires (default 24h, at most 30 days); run connect again to
renew it, or revoke it from the console.

The profile goes to ~/.config/optimal/profile (mode 0600), or
~/.config/optimal/profiles/<name> with --profile <name>. --print writes nothing
and prints shell exports instead.
The workspace is the Engine's only attached
workspace, or the one named with --workspace.

```
  miosa engine connect brain
  miosa engine connect brain --workspace kii --profile kii --ttl 72h
  eval "$(miosa engine connect brain --print)"
```

Flags:

- `--print` - Print shell exports instead of writing a profile.
- `--profile string` - Write ~/.config/optimal/profiles/<name> instead of the default profile.
- `--ttl duration` - Key lifetime (at most 30 days). (default `24h0m0s`)
- `--workspace string` - Workspace to connect (default: the Engine's only attached workspace).

#### `miosa engine create`

Create a managed Optimal Engine (or return the existing one).

```
miosa engine create <name> [flags]
```

Creates a managed Optimal Engine: an Engine in the workspace network with a
durable data volume, an API key and a private URL.

Re-running it with the same name returns the existing Engine, so a script can
call it unconditionally. --wait follows provisioning until the Engine is
running (the target is under 120 s) and prints each state change.

```
  miosa engine create qa-brain --workspace qa --size small --wait
  miosa engine create qa-brain --for workspace --wait
  miosa engine create qa-brain --wait --json | jq '{id, state, engine_version}'

```
--for picks a starting point for how the Engine is used: agent (tiny, 2 GiB),
workspace (xs, 10 GiB) or business (small, 25 GiB). --size and --disk override
either half. --disk is in GiB; --storage-mb stays for exact megabytes and the
two cannot be given together.

Flags:

- `--cpu int` - CPUs (overrides --size).
- `--disk int` - Disk in GiB (overrides --size and --storage-mb).
- `--for string` - How the Engine is used: agent, workspace or business (sets --size and --disk).
- `--memory-mb int` - Memory in MB (overrides --size).
- `--size string` - Size: tiny, xs, small, medium or large.
- `--storage-mb int` - Disk in MB (overrides --size).
- `--timeout int` - Seconds to wait for the Engine to run. (default `120`)
- `--version string` - Engine version (default: the engine's current one).
- `--wait` - Wait until the Engine is running.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug (default: the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa engine detach`

Detach a workspace and revoke its Engine key.

```
miosa engine detach <workspace> [flags]
```

Detaches a workspace from the Engine that serves it and revokes the workspace
key on that Engine.
Resources that are already running keep the variables they
were started with until they are recreated.

A revocation that cannot reach the Engine fails the detach, because the key
would otherwise stay valid. --force marks the attachment detached anyway and
records that the key is still to be revoked; use it only when the Engine or the
workspace is being destroyed.

```
  miosa engine detach kii
  miosa engine detach kii --force --json
```

Flags:

- `--force` - Detach even when the key cannot be revoked on the Engine.

#### `miosa engine get`

Show a managed Optimal Engine, with its usage gauges.

```
miosa engine get <engine>
```

Shows one managed Engine by name or id.
The record is the managed database:
its version, state, private address and workspace, followed by what the Engine
reports it is using against its size's limits (vectors, memories, contexts,
disk, memory, write rate).

A gauge the Engine did not report is left out rather than shown as zero; an
Engine that cannot answer leaves the gauges out and the record prints anyway.

```
  miosa engine get qa-brain
  miosa engine get qa-brain --json | jq '{version: .engine_version, usage}'
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa engine list`

List managed Optimal Engines.

```
miosa engine list [flags]
```

Lists the managed Engines in the organizations your key covers.
Other managed
databases (Postgres, MySQL, Redis, Qdrant) are left out; use 'miosa db list' for
every database.

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--all-orgs` - List every organization your sign-in covers, with an ORG column.

#### `miosa engine recover`

Restore a backup into the Engine, or into a new one.

```
miosa engine recover <engine> [flags]
```

Restores a backup, either over the Engine it came from or into a NEW Engine
(--to-new), which never touches the source.

```
  miosa engine recover qa-brain --to-new qa-brain-restored
  miosa engine recover qa-brain --backup 778439c0 --yes

```
--backup is a backup id (see 'miosa engine backups'); without it the newest
completed backup is used.
Restoring over the Engine replaces its data, so it
asks first (or pass --yes).

Flags:

- `--backup string` - Backup id (default: the newest completed backup).
- `--to-new string` - Create a new Engine with this name and restore into it.
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask before restoring over the source.

#### `miosa engine rm`

Delete a managed Optimal Engine.

```
miosa engine rm <engine> [flags]
```

Deletes the Engine and its data volume.
This cannot be undone; take a backup
first if the data matters ('miosa engine backup').
It asks unless --yes is
given, and a non-interactive run without --yes refuses instead of deleting.

```
  miosa engine rm qa-brain
  miosa engine rm qa-brain --yes
```

Aliases: `delete`, `destroy`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Delete without asking.

#### `miosa engine rollback`

Return a managed Optimal Engine to the version before its last upgrade.

```
miosa engine rollback <engine> [flags]
```

Returns the Engine to the version recorded by its last upgrade.
The previous
version is never guessed: it is the one the upgrade recorded, and it must still
be a staged release.
Data on /data is not touched; a backup restore is a
separate action.

```
  miosa engine rollback qa-brain
  miosa engine rollback qa-brain --json | jq '{from, to, state}'
```

Flags:

- `--timeout int` - Seconds to wait for the rollback call to answer. (default `600`)

#### `miosa engine shell`

Run a shell (or a command) with access to an Engine workspace.

```
miosa engine shell <engine> [-- command [args...]] [flags]
```

Mints a short-lived read/write key for one workspace of an Engine, runs your
shell (or the command after --) with OPTIMAL_ENGINE_URL, OPTIMAL_ENGINE_API_KEY
and OPTIMAL_WORKSPACE set, and revokes the key when it exits.
Nothing is written
to disk.

```
  miosa engine shell brain
  miosa engine shell brain --workspace kii -- optimal boot
```

Flags:

- `--ttl duration` - Key lifetime (at most 30 days). (default `1h0m0s`)
- `--workspace string` - Workspace to connect (default: the Engine's only attached workspace).

#### `miosa engine status`

Show which Engine serves a workspace, or every Engine and its workspaces.

```
miosa engine status [workspace]
```

With a workspace, shows the Engine that serves it, its scope, the Engine
workspace it maps to and the key the workspace's resources use (the key id, not
the key).
A workspace that is not attached prints 'not attached' and exits 0.

Without an argument it lists every managed Engine with the workspaces attached
to it.

```
  miosa engine status kii
  miosa engine status
  miosa engine status --json | jq '.data[] | {engine: .engine.name, workspaces: [.attachments[].workspace_id]}'
```

#### `miosa engine sync`

Keep one workspace in sync between a local and a cloud Engine.

```
miosa engine sync <local> [cloud] [flags]
```

Direct mode wires two reachable Engines for one workspace:

```
  1. mint a replica key on each Engine (scopes sync and read, one workspace)
  2. enable the sync ledger for the workspace on both
  3. register each Engine as the other's peer with policy both
  4. run one cycle unless --no-run, then print both statuses

```
Relay mode (--relay and --engine-id) moves the same changes through MIOSA
instead, for a managed Engine that cannot dial another Engine.
The local Engine
is the initiator: it pulls the cloud's changes through the relay, applies them
locally, then pushes its own ledger back.
Each side carries the sender's engine
envelope (migration, replica_id, version), which the receiving Engine checks
before it applies anything.

The admin keys come from --local-key/--cloud-key or MIOSA_ENGINE_LOCAL_KEY and
MIOSA_ENGINE_CLOUD_KEY.
The relay takes a MIOSA API key with engine:sync, from
--api-key or MIOSA_API_KEY.
With --grant it mints a short-lived engine:sync key
for this run instead: the grant is audited, the key lives in memory only, and
it expires on its own.
No key is ever printed.

Flags:

- `--api-key string` - MIOSA API key with engine:sync (env: MIOSA_API_KEY).
- `--cloud string` - Cloud Engine URL (defaults to the second positional argument).
- `--cloud-key string` - Admin key for the cloud Engine (env: MIOSA_ENGINE_CLOUD_KEY).
- `--engine-id string` - Managed Engine database id on the relay.
- `--grant` - Mint a short-lived engine:sync key for this run instead of using --api-key (audited, never stored).
- `--local string` - Local Engine URL (defaults to the first positional argument).
- `--local-key string` - Admin key for the local Engine (env: MIOSA_ENGINE_LOCAL_KEY).
- `--no-run` - Wire the peers but do not run a sync cycle now.
- `--policy string` - Peer policy: push, pull, both, or none. (default `both`)
- `--relay string` - MIOSA relay base URL (for example https://api.miosa.ai/api/v1); enables relay mode.
- `--since int` - Ledger sequence to pull from on both sides.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace id to sync (required).

##### `miosa engine sync status`

Show sync status and open conflicts on both Engines.

```
miosa engine sync status <local> [cloud] [flags]
```

Reads the sync status of both sides.
In relay mode it reads the cloud
through MIOSA and reports the migration and version the cloud expects, so a
schema mismatch is visible before a push fails. --grant mints a short-lived
engine:sync key for this run instead of taking one from --api-key.

Flags:

- `--api-key string` - MIOSA API key with engine:sync (env: MIOSA_API_KEY).
- `--cloud string` - Cloud Engine URL (defaults to the second positional argument).
- `--cloud-key string` - Admin key for the cloud Engine (env: MIOSA_ENGINE_CLOUD_KEY).
- `--engine-id string` - Managed Engine database id on the relay.
- `--grant` - Mint a short-lived engine:sync key for this run instead of using --api-key (audited, never stored).
- `--local string` - Local Engine URL (defaults to the first positional argument).
- `--local-key string` - Admin key for the local Engine (env: MIOSA_ENGINE_LOCAL_KEY).
- `--relay string` - MIOSA relay base URL; enables relay mode.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace id (required).

#### `miosa engine upgrade`

Upgrade a managed Optimal Engine to a staged release.

```
miosa engine upgrade <engine> --to <version> [flags]
```

Swaps a managed Engine to a staged release.
The control plane checks the
target manifest against the artifact on disk, requires a completed backup,
requires the release's rootfs image on the host, stops the Engine, boots the
new release and verifies the guest's own /api/version.
If the guest does not
report the target version, the control plane rolls back and reports what it
restored.
Data on /data is not touched.

```
  miosa engine upgrade qa-brain --to 0.3.2 --dry-run
  miosa engine upgrade qa-brain --to 0.3.2

```
--force overrides the refusal to upgrade from a version that has no staged
release to roll back to.
Use it only when that version is known to be
recoverable another way.

Flags:

- `--dry-run` - Show what the upgrade would do, without doing it.
- `--force` - Upgrade even when the current version has no staged release to roll back to.
- `--timeout int` - Seconds to wait for the upgrade call to answer. (default `600`)
- `--to string` - Target Engine version (required).

### `miosa env`

Manage environments (what new machines start with).

```
miosa env
```

Manage environments: the template a new sandbox or computer inherits when it
starts.
An environment decides which repositories are cloned, which variables
and secret files are injected, and which of your connections the machine may use.

Every save mints a new immutable version.
A machine keeps the version it
started with until you run 'miosa env upgrade'.

Environments belong to a workspace, or to the organization when no workspace
applies.
Pass --workspace <slug> to work in one workspace; without it the API
uses the workspace of a workspace-bound key, else the organization level.

Commands that edit one environment take --env <name>.
Without it they edit
the default environment of that scope.

```
  miosa env list
  miosa env new staging
  miosa env set --env staging STRIPE_KEY=sk_live_123
  miosa env set --env staging --from-file .env
  miosa env file put --env staging backend/.env ./backend.env
  miosa env repo add --env staging octocat/hello-world --branch develop
  miosa env toggle --env staging github off
  miosa create my-box --env staging
```

Flags:


#### `miosa env default`

Make an environment the default.

```
miosa env default <name>
```

Make an environment the one new machines use when none is named.

#### `miosa env file`

Add and remove secret files.

```
miosa env file
```

Secret files are written into the machine when it starts.
They are stored encrypted and never shown again.

##### `miosa env file put`

Add or replace a secret file.

```
miosa env file put <relpath> <local-file> [flags]
```

Write a secret file into an environment. <relpath> is relative to the
machine's home directory; include the repository folder to land a file inside
a clone. <local-file> is read from disk, or from stdin when it is -.

Files are written mode 0600.
At most 256KB each.

Example:
```
  miosa env add-file --env staging hello-world/backend/.env ./backend.env
```

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).

##### `miosa env file rm`

Remove a secret file.

```
miosa env file rm <relpath> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).

#### `miosa env info`

Show one environment in full.

```
miosa env info <name> [flags]
```

Show an environment: toggles, variables, secret files, repositories.

Variable values are masked.
Pass --reveal to fetch and print them; each reveal
is recorded in the audit log.
Secret file contents are never printed, only
their paths and sizes.

Aliases: `get`.

Flags:

- `--reveal` - Print variable values instead of masking them.

#### `miosa env list`

List environments.

```
miosa env list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa env new`

Create an environment.

```
miosa env new <name> [flags]
```

Create an environment at version 1.
It starts with everything injected
(GitHub, secrets, agent connections) unless it is protected.

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--safe-for-third-parties` - Pass nothing of yours to machines using this environment.

#### `miosa env protect`

Pass nothing of yours to machines using this environment.

```
miosa env protect <on|off> [flags]
```

The master switch.
On: machines other people drive receive no GitHub access,
no secrets, no machine key and no agent connections, whatever the section toggles say.
Off: the four toggles apply.

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).

#### `miosa env rename`

Rename an environment.

```
miosa env rename <name> <new-name>
```

Rename an environment.
Machines stay pinned to their versions.

#### `miosa env repo`

Manage repositories cloned into new machines.

```
miosa env repo
```

##### `miosa env repo add`

Clone a repository into new machines.

```
miosa env repo add <owner/repo> [flags]
```

Attach a repository to an environment.
It is cloned at its base branch into
the machine's home directory, in a folder named after the repository.
MIOSA
never creates a branch.

--setup-file attaches a script (UTF-8, up to 64KB) that runs once in the
repository folder when the machine starts.
With --blocking the machine is not
ready until it finishes; --non-blocking (the default) runs it alongside
everything else.

Adding a repository that is already attached replaces its settings.

Example:
```
  miosa env repo add --env staging octocat/hello-world --branch develop \
    --setup-file ./setup.sh --blocking
```

Flags:

- `--blocking` - Machine is not ready until the setup script finishes.
- `--branch string` - Base branch to clone (default: the repository's default branch).
- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).
- `--non-blocking` - Setup script runs alongside everything else (default).
- `--setup-file string` - Script to run in the repository folder at machine start (max 64KB).
- `--source string` - Repository source: github or forge. (default `github`)

##### `miosa env repo rm`

Stop cloning a repository into new machines.

```
miosa env repo rm <owner/repo> [flags]
```

Aliases: `remove`.

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).

#### `miosa env rm`

Delete an environment.

```
miosa env rm <name> [flags]
```

Soft-delete an environment.
Machines pinned to its versions keep running;
new machines can no longer use it.
The default environment cannot be deleted:
make another one the default first.
You are asked to confirm unless --force
is given.

Aliases: `delete`, `remove`.

Flags:

- `-f, --force` - Skip the confirmation prompt.
- `-y, --yes` - Same as --force.

#### `miosa env set`

Set environment variables.

```
miosa env set [NAME] [flags]
```

Set one or more variables on an environment.
Each call changes only the
variables you name; the rest are untouched.
The whole call mints one version
per variable.

A value is never an argument, because arguments land in shell history and
process listings.
Give the NAME and enter the value when asked (input hidden),
pipe the value on standard input, or load KEY=VALUE lines with --from-file.

Examples:
```
  miosa env set-var STRIPE_KEY
  echo "$VALUE" | miosa env set-var --env staging STRIPE_KEY
  miosa env set-var --env staging --from-file .env
  cat .env | miosa env set-var --env staging --from-file -
```

Aliases: `set-var`.

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).
- `--from-file string` - Read KEY=VALUE lines from a .env file (- for stdin).

#### `miosa env toggle`

Choose what a machine receives from your account.

```
miosa env toggle <github|secrets|machine-key|agent-connections> <on|off> [flags]
```

Switch one of the four sections an environment can pass to a machine:

```
  github              repositories cloned in, plus the GitHub token
  secrets             variables and secret files
  machine-key         a key scoped to the machine's own lifecycle
  agent-connections   your Claude, Codex and OSA connections

```
Turning a section off keeps what you typed; it applies again when switched on.
Toggles are ignored while the environment is protected.

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).

#### `miosa env unset`

Remove environment variables.

```
miosa env unset <KEY>... [flags]
```

Aliases: `unset-var`.

Flags:

- `--env string` - Environment name (default: the default environment).

#### `miosa env upgrade`

Move machines onto their environment's latest version.

```
miosa env upgrade [name|id]... [flags]
```

Upgrade machines to the latest version of the environment they are pinned to.
Running machines get the new configuration now; stopped ones pick it up when
they resume.
Nothing upgrades on its own.

This is not reversible on the machine's disk: a secret the new version
withholds is deleted from the machine, not hidden.
You are asked to confirm
unless --force is given.

With no machine it upgrades the current sandbox (see 'miosa use').

```
  miosa env upgrade my-box            one machine
  miosa env upgrade                   the current sandbox
  miosa env upgrade --all staging     every machine behind on "staging"
```

Flags:

- `--all string` - Upgrade every machine that is behind on this environment.
- `-f, --force` - Skip the confirmation prompt.

#### `miosa env versions`

List an environment's versions and how many machines sit on each.

```
miosa env versions <name>
```

### `miosa environment`

List, create, fork and remove a project's Deploy targets.

```
miosa environment
```

Deploy targets are what Railway calls environments: production, staging, one
per preview.
The linked project is used unless a project is named.

```
  miosa environment list
  miosa environment new staging
  miosa environment fork production --name staging
  miosa environment rm staging
```

Aliases: `environments`.

#### `miosa environment fork`

Copy a Deploy target's settings into a new one.

```
miosa environment fork <from> [flags]
```

Copy a Deploy target: its services, variables and settings, without its data.

```
  miosa environment fork production --name staging
```

Flags:

- `--name string` - Name for the new Deploy target.

#### `miosa environment list`

List a project's Deploy targets.

```
miosa environment list [project]
```

#### `miosa environment new`

Create a Deploy target.

```
miosa environment new <name> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--slug string` - URL-safe name (default: from the name).

#### `miosa environment rm`

Remove a Deploy target and everything in it.

```
miosa environment rm <name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `remove`, `delete`.

Flags:

- `--yes` - Do not ask.

### `miosa events`

Stream a sandbox's lifecycle events, or its agent's chat events.

```
miosa events [sandbox] [flags]
```

Stream events (state changes, readiness, previews) as they happen.
Ctrl-C stops.
With --json each event is one JSON object per line.

--agent shows the agent's events instead (prompts, tool calls, answers) of the
newest run in the sandbox; --convo <chat-id> those of one chat. -f keeps
following until the run ends.

```
  miosa events my-box
  miosa events my-box --json | jq -c 'select(.event == "state")'
  miosa events my-box --agent -f
  miosa events --convo 3f0c...
```

Flags:

- `--agent` - Show the agent's chat events instead of the sandbox's lifecycle.
- `--convo string` - Show one chat's agent events (a chat id).
- `-f, --follow` - With --agent or --convo: keep following until the run ends.

### `miosa exec`

Execute a command in a sandbox, in the foreground or the background.

```
miosa exec [name|id] -- <cmd> [args...] [flags]
```

Run a command inside a sandbox and print its output. miosa exits with the
remote command's exit code, so it drops straight into scripts and CI.

The -- separator is required before the command and its arguments.

--cwd sets the working directory, relative to the sandbox home directory (or
absolute).
The global --timeout bounds the command in seconds: up to 300 the
command runs as one request (default 30); 0 (no limit, up to a day) or more than
300 streams the output live as the command writes it, and so does --stream.
A command that runs out of time exits 124.
Ctrl-C stops following.

--detach starts the command in the background and prints its id; it keeps
running after the CLI exits and its output is kept.
Then:
```
  --attach <id>   print its output as it grows, exit with its code when it ends
  --status <id>   show whether it is running and its exit code
  --kill <id>     stop it

```
A sandbox that is still starting refuses commands; exec retries with backoff
until it is ready (up to 90 seconds).

Examples:
```
  miosa exec my-box -- echo hello
  miosa exec -- ls -la /home      # uses current sandbox
  miosa exec my-box --cwd my-repo --timeout 120 -- npm run build
  miosa exec my-box --timeout 0 -- ./long-test-suite.sh
  miosa exec my-box --detach -- npm run train
  miosa exec my-box --attach <id>
  miosa exec my-box -- bash -c 'for i in 1 2 3; do echo $i; done'
```

Flags:

- `--attach string` - Follow a background command's output and exit with its code.
- `--cwd string` - Working directory, relative to the sandbox home directory.
- `--detach` - Run in the background and print the command's id.
- `--kill string` - Stop a background command.
- `--status string` - Show a background command's state.
- `--stream` - Print the output live as the command writes it.

### `miosa export`

Export everything an organization (or one workspace) owns.

```
miosa export [flags]
```

Writes an organization's records into a bucket of the same organization and
prints the manifest, which lists every file with its SHA-256 and byte size.

```
  miosa export --to bucket:exports
  miosa export --workspace prod --to bucket:backups --prefix exports/2026-10-19/

```
Buckets can be named by id or by name.
What is never exported: secret values,
token bytes, deploy variable values and object bodies (the bucket objects are
listed by key and size, and stay where they are).

Flags:

- `--prefix string` - Key prefix inside the bucket (default exports/<timestamp>/).
- `--to string` - Bucket to write into: bucket:<id-or-name>.
- `--workspace string` - Narrow the export to one workspace (id or slug).

### `miosa extend`

Replace a sandbox's time to live.

```
miosa extend [sandbox] [flags]
```

Set how long the sandbox may keep running from now.
The new value replaces the old
one, it does not add to it.
See the time left with 'miosa usage'.

```
  miosa extend my-box --for 2h
  miosa extend --hours 6
  miosa extend my-box --no-auto-stop
```

Flags:

- `--for string` - New time to live: seconds, 90m, 2h or 2d.
- `--hours int` - New time to live in hours (same as --for <n>h).
- `--no-auto-stop` - Keep it running: no time limit or idle pause (always on).

### `miosa feedback`

Send feedback to the MIOSA team.

```
miosa feedback <message> [flags]
```

Send a message straight to the MIOSA team: a bug, a missing feature, or anything
that got in your way.
Every message is emailed to the team and read by a person,
and replies come to your account's email address.

The CLI version and your operating system are attached so the team can
reproduce what you saw.
Pass - to read the message from standard input.

```
  miosa feedback "miosa deploy hangs after the build finishes"
  miosa feedback --type feature "let me pick the region on create"
  miosa logs my-box | miosa feedback --type bug -
```

Flags:

- `-t, --type string` - Kind of feedback: bug, feature, improvement or general. (default `general`)

### `miosa files`

Copy, list, read, move and delete files in a sandbox.

```
miosa files
```

Work with files inside a sandbox.
A remote path is written <sandbox>:<path>;
the sandbox may be a name or id, and can be omitted for the current sandbox.
Relative remote paths start in /home/user.

```
  miosa files ls my-box:/workspace
  miosa files cp ./app.py my-box:/workspace/app.py
  miosa files cp -r ./src my-box:/workspace/
  miosa files cp my-box:/workspace/out.json ./out.json
  miosa files cat my-box:/workspace/out.json
  echo hi | miosa files write my-box:/tmp/hi.txt
```

#### `miosa files cat`

Print a file from a sandbox.

```
miosa files cat [<sandbox>:]<path>
```

#### `miosa files chmod`

Change a file's permissions (octal mode, for example 755).

```
miosa files chmod <mode> [<sandbox>:]<path>
```

#### `miosa files cp`

Copy files to, from or between sandboxes.

```
miosa files cp <src> <dst> [flags]
```

Copy files between your machine and a sandbox, or between sandboxes.
Remote paths are <sandbox>:<path>.
A directory needs -r.

```
  Local to remote:   miosa files cp ./local.txt my-box:/workspace/local.txt
  Remote to local:   miosa files cp my-box:/workspace/out.txt ./out.txt
  Remote to remote:  miosa files cp a:/src.txt b:/dst.txt
  A directory:       miosa files cp -r ./site my-box:/workspace/

```
A destination ending in / (or an existing directory) receives the source's name.
File contents go through the JSON API as base64; the server's upload limit applies.

Flags:

- `-r, --recursive` - Copy directories.

#### `miosa files ls`

List a sandbox directory.

```
miosa files ls [<sandbox>:]<path> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-l, --long` - Show modification times.

#### `miosa files mkdir`

Create a directory (and its parents) in a sandbox.

```
miosa files mkdir [<sandbox>:]<path>
```

#### `miosa files mv`

Move or rename a file inside a sandbox.

```
miosa files mv [<sandbox>:]<from> <to>
```

Aliases: `rename`.

#### `miosa files rm`

Delete files or directories in a sandbox.

```
miosa files rm [<sandbox>:]<path>... [flags]
```

Delete one or more paths.
A directory needs -r.
A path that does not exist is an
error unless -f is given.

```
  miosa files rm my-box:/tmp/old.log
  miosa files rm -r my-box:/workspace/build
```

Flags:

- `-f, --force` - Ignore paths that do not exist.
- `-r, --recursive` - Delete directories and their contents.

#### `miosa files stat`

Show a file's type, size, mode and modification time.

```
miosa files stat [<sandbox>:]<path>
```

#### `miosa files write`

Write standard input to a file in a sandbox.

```
miosa files write [<sandbox>:]<path>
```

Read standard input and store it as a file in the sandbox, replacing any
existing file.
Parent directories are created.

```
  cat config.yaml | miosa files write my-box:/workspace/config.yaml
```

### `miosa forge`

Work with MIOSA Forge repositories (beta).

```
miosa forge
```

Work with repositories owned by the organization associated with the current MIOSA credential.

Flags:


#### `miosa forge accept`

Steer a Work Order: accept.

```
miosa forge accept WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge accept WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge add`

Add work: split into Work Orders, deduplicated, queued.

```
miosa forge add "text" [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
- `--milestone string` - The phase it goes in.
- `--mission string` - The Mission it goes in.
- `--new` - You checked: it is not a duplicate.
- `--owner string` - The Crew role that owns it.
- `--priority string` - P0 to P3.

Examples:

```
miosa forge add "Retry failed webhooks" --milestone phase-1 --owner backend --priority P1
```

#### `miosa forge answer`

Steer a Work Order: answer.

```
miosa forge answer WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge answer WO-12 "Keep it one more release"
```

#### `miosa forge approve`

Steer a Work Order: approve.

```
miosa forge approve WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge approve WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge approve-spec`

Approve draft specs (agents can't start without one).

```
miosa forge approve-spec WO-12 | --milestone id [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--milestone string` - Approve every draft spec in this phase.

Examples:

```
miosa forge approve-spec WO-12
  miosa forge approve-spec --milestone phase-1
```

#### `miosa forge ask`

Ask the person, going ahead with your own call unless they steer.

```
miosa forge ask WO-12 "question" [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--default string` - What you'll do if nobody steers.
- `--minutes string` - How long to wait (default 60).
- `--option string` - An answer to click, "Label :: what it means" (repeat it).
- `--recommend string` - The option you'd pick (1 is the first).

Examples:

```
miosa forge ask WO-12 "Drop the old endpoint?" --default "Keep it one release" --option "Drop it :: remove now" --option "Keep it :: one more release" --recommend 2
```

#### `miosa forge assign`

Move an agent to another Mission's crew.

```
miosa forge assign <runner> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is moving it.
- `--mission string` - The Mission whose crew it joins.
- `--why string` - Why it moves.

Examples:

```
miosa forge assign backend-2 --mission client-issues --why "P0 from a customer"
```

#### `miosa forge assist`

Work one step of a Work Order another agent holds.

```
miosa forge assist WO-12 [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Your Crew role.
- `--runner string` - The running agent (default FORGE_RUNNER).
- `--step string` - The step you work.

Examples:

```
miosa forge assist WO-12 --step 3
```

#### `miosa forge auth`

Use your MIOSA API key for Forge git (clone, push and pull over HTTPS).

```
miosa forge auth
```

##### `miosa forge auth setup-git`

Make git use your MIOSA login for Forge repositories.

```
miosa forge auth setup-git [flags]
```

Configures git (globally, for the Forge host only) to ask this CLI for
your API key when it talks to Forge.
After it, plain git works against Forge:

```
  git clone https://forge.miosa.ai/<org>/<repo>.git
  git push

```
The password git receives is the API key this CLI is signed in with
('miosa login').
A CLI signed in with a browser session token instead gets a
Forge-only key (forge:read, forge:write) minted once with that session and
kept in this profile; 'miosa logout' forgets it.
Nothing is stored by git.

Flags:

- `--host string` - Forge git host. (default `forge.miosa.ai`)

Examples:

```
miosa forge auth setup-git
  miosa forge repo clone my-repo
```

#### `miosa forge autonomy`

How long agents wait for you before going with their own call.

```
miosa forge autonomy <minutes>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge autonomy 60
```

#### `miosa forge backlog`

Steer a Work Order: backlog.

```
miosa forge backlog WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge backlog WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge brief`

The Mission's brief, or a phase's.

```
miosa forge brief [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
- `--mission string` - A Mission other than the active one.
- `--phase string` - A phase's brief instead of the Mission's.
- `--set string` - Replace it from a file, or - for stdin.

Examples:

```
miosa forge brief
  miosa forge brief --phase phase-1
  miosa forge brief --set brief.md
```

#### `miosa forge cancel`

Steer a Work Order: cancel.

```
miosa forge cancel WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge cancel WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge charter`

The Charter above every Mission: how MIOSA ships, what comes first.

```
miosa forge charter [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
- `--set string` - Replace it from a file, or - for stdin.

Examples:

```
miosa forge charter
  miosa forge charter --set charter.md
```

#### `miosa forge check`

Commit checks: results reported against a commit.

```
miosa forge check
```

A check is a named result (unit-tests passed, lint failed) recorded against a
commit, with the evidence behind it.
Branch policies can require named checks
before a pull request merges. 'miosa forge pr check' reports one against a pull
request's head; these commands work on any commit.

```
  miosa forge check list platform
  miosa forge check suite platform <commit>
  miosa forge check report platform --commit <sha> --name unit-tests --conclusion passed --file evidence.json
```

Aliases: `checks`.

##### `miosa forge check evidence`

Show the evidence behind a check.

```
miosa forge check evidence <repo> <check>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge check evidence platform <check-id>
```

##### `miosa forge check list`

List recent check suites, newest first.

```
miosa forge check list <repo> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--commit string` - Only this commit.
- `--limit int` - How many suites.

Examples:

```
miosa forge check list platform
  miosa forge check list platform --commit <sha>
```

##### `miosa forge check report`

Report a check result against a commit.

```
miosa forge check report <repo> [flags]
```

A completed check needs evidence: put {"evidence": {...}} in --file (up to 64 KB).
Repeating an --idempotency-key returns the first result.

Flags:

- `--commit string` - Full commit SHA the result is for.
- `--conclusion string` - Passed or failed, for a completed check.
- `--details-url string` - Link to the full report.
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--name string` - Check name, for example unit-tests.
- `--status string` - Queued, in_progress or completed (default completed).
- `--summary string` - One line on what the check found.

Examples:

```
miosa forge check report platform --commit <sha> --name unit-tests --conclusion passed --file evidence.json
```

##### `miosa forge check run`

Run a real Check on a Work Order and record the result.

```
miosa forge check run WO-12 -- <command…>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge check run WO-12 -- mix test
  miosa forge check run WO-12 -- pnpm test
```

##### `miosa forge check show`

Show one check.

```
miosa forge check show <repo> <check>
```

Aliases: `get`.

Examples:

```
miosa forge check show platform <check-id>
```

##### `miosa forge check suite`

Show every check on one commit.

```
miosa forge check suite <repo> <commit>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge check suite platform <commit>
```

#### `miosa forge context`

What to read before working: Mission, phase, Work Order [--full].

```
miosa forge context WO-12 [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--full` - Include the Mission's whole brief.

Examples:

```
miosa forge context WO-12
  miosa forge context WO-12 --full
```

#### `miosa forge decide`

Choose an option of a decision (0 is the first).

```
miosa forge decide <decision> <option>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge decide versioning 0
```

#### `miosa forge discover`

File something you found outside your Work Order.

```
miosa forge discover "what" [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who found it.
- `--from string` - The Work Order you found it on.

Examples:

```
miosa forge discover "Billing retries are not idempotent" --from WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge discoveries`

Orchestrator: discoveries waiting to be placed.

```
miosa forge discoveries [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--wait` - Block until there is one (up to 9 minutes).

Examples:

```
miosa forge discoveries
  miosa forge discoveries --wait
```

#### `miosa forge dismiss`

Steer a Work Order: dismiss.

```
miosa forge dismiss WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge dismiss WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge done`

Hand over with a completion report.

```
miosa forge done WO-12 [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--report string` - The completion report as JSON: a file, or - for stdin.

Examples:

```
miosa forge done WO-12 --report report.json
  cat report.json | miosa forge done WO-12 --report -
```

#### `miosa forge edit`

Edit a Work Order.

```
miosa forge edit WO-12 [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
- `--detail string` - New detail.
- `--done-when string` - New Done when.
- `--milestone string` - Move it to this phase.
- `--owner string` - Give it to this role.
- `--title string` - New title.

Examples:

```
miosa forge edit WO-12 --title "Retry webhooks twice" --owner backend
```

#### `miosa forge end`

Steer a Work Order: end.

```
miosa forge end WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge end WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge fork`

Fork a repository into your organization.

```
miosa forge fork <repo> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--name string` - Name for the fork.
- `--slug string` - URL slug for the fork.
- `--visibility string` - Public, private or internal.

Examples:

```
miosa forge fork acme/platform --name platform-fork
```

#### `miosa forge grant`

Repository roles for people.

```
miosa forge grant
```

Roles are read, write, maintain and admin.

##### `miosa forge grant set`

Give a person a role on a repository.

```
miosa forge grant set <repo> <user-id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--role string` - Read, write, maintain or admin.

Examples:

```
miosa forge grant set platform <user-id> --role write
```

#### `miosa forge hooks`

In an agent's folder: Claude Code reports its tools and sub-agents to Forge live.

```
miosa forge hooks install
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge hooks install
```

#### `miosa forge inbox`

What needs you: decisions, questions, approvals, promotions.

```
miosa forge inbox
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge inbox
```

#### `miosa forge intake`

Notes left in Forge waiting to be planned.

```
miosa forge intake [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--save string` - Folder for the attached files.

Examples:

```
miosa forge intake
  miosa forge intake --save ./notes
```

#### `miosa forge join`

Join a Mission's crew as a running agent.

```
miosa forge join [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Your Crew role.
- `--harness string` - The harness you run in: claude-code, codex, osa….
- `--machine string` - The machine you run on.
- `--mission string` - The Mission whose crew you join.
- `--on string` - Where you run: local, sandbox:<id>, computer:<id> or machine:<name>.
- `--sandbox string` - The sandbox you run in.
- `--specialty string` - Your specialty, for example backend.elixir.
- `--worktree string` - Your worktree (default: this git repo).

Examples:

```
miosa forge join --as backend --mission release-pipeline-v1 --on local --harness claude-code
```

#### `miosa forge leave`

Stop this running agent; what it held goes back to the queue.

```
miosa forge leave [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--runner string` - The running agent (default FORGE_RUNNER).

Examples:

```
miosa forge leave
```

#### `miosa forge log`

A line in its activity.

```
miosa forge log WO-12 "text" [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is writing it.

Examples:

```
miosa forge log WO-12 "Found the cause: the retry ignored 429"
```

#### `miosa forge ls`

Work Orders by status.

```
miosa forge ls [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--agent string` - Only this role's.
- `--all` - Include shipped Work Orders.
- `--backlog` - The backlog instead of the Mission.
- `--mine` - Only your role's (FORGE_AGENT).

Examples:

```
miosa forge ls
  miosa forge ls --agent backend
  miosa forge ls --backlog
```

#### `miosa forge merged`

Record a real merge.

```
miosa forge merged WO-12 <sha|PR> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
- `--into string` - The branch it merged into.

Examples:

```
miosa forge merged WO-12 https://github.com/acme/app/pull/88
```

#### `miosa forge milestone`

Phases: add one, edit it, give it a branch, record that branch's merge.

```
miosa forge milestone add|edit|branch|merged [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--after string` - The phase it follows.
- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
