- `--done-when string` - Its Done when.
- `--into string` - The branch it merges into.
- `--title string` - New title.

Examples:

```
miosa forge milestone add "Staging" --done-when "Every release runs on staging first" --after phase-2
  miosa forge milestone branch phase-3 release/staging --into release/v1
```

#### `miosa forge mission`

Create, start, close or edit a Mission.

```
miosa forge mission new|start|close|edit [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
- `--done-when string` - Its Done when, lines separated by |.
- `--goal string` - Why the Mission exists.
- `--organization` - Spans every workspace.
- `--owners string` - Roles of a standing Mission, comma-separated.
- `--standing` - Always open, for ongoing work.
- `--title string` - New title.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace it lives in.
- `--workspace-name string` - That workspace's name.

Examples:

```
miosa forge mission new "Release pipeline v2" --goal "Ship without restarts" --done-when "No restarts|One deploy path"
  miosa forge mission new "Client issues" --standing --owners backend,frontend
  miosa forge mission start release-pipeline-v2
  miosa forge mission close release-pipeline-v2
```

#### `miosa forge next`

Steer a Work Order: next.

```
miosa forge next WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge next WO-12
```

Steer a Work Order: note.

```
miosa forge note WO-12 [text]
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge note WO-12 "Customer confirmed the fix"
```

#### `miosa forge p0`

Set the priority to P0.

```
miosa forge p0 WO-12
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge p0 WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge p1`

Set the priority to P1.

```
miosa forge p1 WO-12
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge p1 WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge p2`

Set the priority to P2.

```
miosa forge p2 WO-12
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge p2 WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge p3`

Set the priority to P3.

```
miosa forge p3 WO-12
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge p3 WO-12
```

#### `miosa forge pause`

Pause a Crew role's agents.

```
miosa forge pause <agent>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge pause backend
```

#### `miosa forge place`

Place a discovery in a Mission and phase.

```
miosa forge place WO-51 [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is placing it.
- `--milestone string` - The phase.
- `--mission string` - The Mission.
- `--owner string` - The Crew role.
- `--priority string` - P0 to P3.
- `--why string` - Why it goes there.

Examples:

```
miosa forge place WO-51 --milestone phase-1 --owner backend --priority P1 --why "Blocks the deploy path"
```

#### `miosa forge planned`

Link a note to the Work Orders it became (or --reason "why none").

```
miosa forge planned <note> WO-12… [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--as string` - Who is recording this.
- `--reason string` - Why the note needs no work.

Examples:

```
miosa forge planned note-3 WO-12 WO-13
  miosa forge planned note-4 --reason "Already shipped"
```

#### `miosa forge policy`

Branch policy and merge policy of a repository.

```
miosa forge policy
```

A branch policy says what a merge into a branch needs.
Publishing one creates a
new version; the newest applies.

```
  miosa forge policy set platform --branch main --approvals 2 --check unit-tests
  miosa forge policy show platform
  miosa forge policy owners platform
```

##### `miosa forge policy owners`

Show the code owners.

```
miosa forge policy owners <repo>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge policy owners platform
```

##### `miosa forge policy set`

Publish a branch policy.

```
miosa forge policy set <repo> [flags]
```

Needs the maintain role.
Without --approvals one approval is required; the author cannot approve their own pull request unless --independent=false.

Flags:

- `--approvals int` - Approvals required.
- `--branch string` - Branch the policy covers (default: the default branch).
- `--check stringArray` - A check that must pass (repeatable).
- `--code-owners` - Require a code owner's approval.
- `--dismiss-stale` - New commits dismiss earlier approvals. (default `true`)
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--independent` - Require an approver other than the author. (default `true`)

Examples:

```
miosa forge policy set platform --branch main --approvals 2 --check unit-tests
  miosa forge policy set platform --branch main --approvals 0 --independent=false
```

##### `miosa forge policy show`

Show the merge policy.

```
miosa forge policy show <repo> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--branch string` - Branch (default: the default branch).

Examples:

```
miosa forge policy show platform
  miosa forge policy show platform --branch release
```

#### `miosa forge pr`

Pull requests, reviews, checks and merging.

```
miosa forge pr
```

<repo> is a repository id, slug or name; <number> is the pull request number.
Reviewing and merging need the write role on the repository, and the branch
policy decides what a merge needs: approvals, passing checks, and a reviewer other
than the author.

```
  miosa forge pr new platform --head fix-redirect --title "Fix redirect loop"
  miosa forge pr list platform
  miosa forge pr show platform 12
  miosa forge pr review platform 12 --decision approved
  miosa forge pr diff platform 12
  miosa forge pr checkout platform 12
  miosa forge pr merge platform 12 --squash
```

Aliases: `pulls`, `pull-request`, `pull-requests`.

##### `miosa forge pr check`

Report a check result on a pull request's head commit.

```
miosa forge pr check <repo> <number> [flags]
```

Reports a check against the pull request's current head commit.
The check needs
evidence: put {"evidence": {...}} in --file.
The reporter must not be the author,
and --head-sha must equal the pull request's head, so a stale result is refused.

Flags:

- `--conclusion string` - Passed or failed, for a completed check.
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--head-sha string` - The pull request's current head commit.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--name string` - Check name, for example unit-tests.
- `--status string` - Queued, in_progress or completed (default completed).

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr check platform 12 --head-sha <sha> --name unit-tests --conclusion passed --file evidence.json
```

##### `miosa forge pr checkout`

Check out a pull request's branch in this git repository.

```
miosa forge pr checkout <repo> <number> [flags]
```

Fetches the pull request's head branch (from its fork, when it has one) and
checks it out as a local branch with the same name, or --branch.
An existing
local branch is moved forward only; local commits are never thrown away.

Flags:

- `--branch string` - Local branch name (default: the pull request's head branch).

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr checkout platform 12
```

##### `miosa forge pr close`

Close a pull request without merging.

```
miosa forge pr close <repo> <number>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr close platform 12
```

##### `miosa forge pr comment`

Add a comment to a pull request.

```
miosa forge pr comment <repo> <number> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--body string` - Comment text.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr comment platform 12 --body "Looks good once CI passes"
```

##### `miosa forge pr comments`

List the comments on a pull request.

```
miosa forge pr comments <repo> <number>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr comments platform 12
```

##### `miosa forge pr diff`

Show the changes a pull request makes.

```
miosa forge pr diff <repo> <number> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--name-only` - Print only the names of the changed files.

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr diff platform 12
  miosa forge pr diff platform 12 --name-only
```

##### `miosa forge pr list`

List pull requests.

```
miosa forge pr list <repo> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--author string` - Only pull requests by this person (name, email or user id).
- `--base string` - Only pull requests into this branch.
- `--state string` - Only pull requests in this state (open|closed|merged|all). (default `open`)

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr list platform
  miosa forge pr list platform --state all --author dana --base main
```

##### `miosa forge pr merge`

Merge a pull request.

```
miosa forge pr merge <repo> <number> [flags]
```

Merges when the branch policy is met: enough approvals, required checks passing, and the base branch not moved.
A merge into a branch that deploys on merge starts that deployment.

Flags:

- `--auto` - Merge by itself once the branch policy is met.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--merge` - Merge with a merge commit (the default).
- `--rebase` - Replay the commits onto the base branch.
- `--squash` - Squash the commits into one on the base branch.

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr merge platform 12
  miosa forge pr merge platform 12 --squash
  miosa forge pr merge platform 12 --rebase --auto
```

##### `miosa forge pr new`

Open a pull request.

```
miosa forge pr new <repo> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`, `open`.

Flags:

- `--base string` - Branch to merge into (default: the repository's default branch).
- `--body string` - Description.
- `--draft` - Open it as a draft.
- `--head string` - Branch with the changes.
- `--head-repo string` - Repository id the head branch lives in, for a pull request from a fork.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--title string` - Title.

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr new platform --head fix-redirect --title "Fix redirect loop"
  miosa forge pr new platform --head docs --base release --title "Docs" --draft
```

##### `miosa forge pr previews`

List the preview deployments of a pull request.

```
miosa forge pr previews <repo> <number>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr previews platform 12
```

##### `miosa forge pr reopen`

Reopen a closed pull request.

```
miosa forge pr reopen <repo> <number>
```

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr reopen platform 12
```

##### `miosa forge pr review`

Approve, request changes on, or comment on a pull request.

```
miosa forge pr review <repo> <number> [flags]
```

Records a review of the pull request at its current head.
You cannot approve your own pull request.

Flags:

- `--body string` - Review comment.
- `--decision string` - What the review says (approved|changes_requested|commented).
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr review platform 12 --decision approved
  miosa forge pr review platform 12 --decision changes_requested --body "Needs a test"
```

##### `miosa forge pr show`

Show a pull request with its reviews, checks and merge readiness.

```
miosa forge pr show <repo> <number>
```

Aliases: `get`, `status`.

Examples:

```
miosa forge pr show platform 12
  miosa forge pr show platform 12 --json
```


<tag> is the release's tag name.

```
  miosa forge release list platform
  miosa forge release new platform --tag v1.2.0 --name "1.2.0" --body "Fixes the redirect"
  miosa forge release upload platform v1.2.0 dist/app.tar.gz
```

Create a release.

```
miosa forge release new <repo> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--body string` - Release notes.
- `--commit string` - Commit or branch to tag (default: the default branch).
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating the same key returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--name string` - Release title.
- `--prerelease` - Mark it as a prerelease.
- `--tag string` - Tag name, created if it does not exist.

Examples:

```
miosa forge release new platform --tag v1.2.0 --name "1.2.0" --body "Fixes the redirect"
```

Create a tag.

```

```

- `--message string` - Annotation for the tag.
- `--name string` - Tag name.
- `--target string` - Commit or branch to tag (default: the default branch).

Examples:

```
miosa forge release tag platform --name v1.2.0 --target main --message "1.2.0"
```

##### `miosa forge release upload`

Attach files to a release.

```
miosa forge release upload <repo> <tag> [file...] [flags]
```

Uploads each file as a release asset (up to 25 MB each). --source-archive
attaches a .tar.gz of the tagged commit instead.
Repeating an upload with the
same --idempotency-key returns the first result.

Flags:

- `--content-type string` - Content type (default: from the file extension).
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: repeating it returns the first result (default: generated).
- `--name string` - Asset name (default: the file name).
- `--source-archive` - Attach a .tar.gz of the tagged commit.

Examples:

```
miosa forge release upload platform v1.2.0 dist/app.tar.gz dist/checksums.txt
  miosa forge release upload platform v1.2.0 --source-archive
```

### `miosa fork`

Fork a running sandbox into a copy.

```
miosa fork [sandbox] [flags]
```

Fork a running sandbox: the copy starts from its disk as it is now. --wait blocks
until the fork accepts commands.

-e KEY=VALUE sets variables in the fork only (the source keeps its own), and
--cpu / --memory resize the fork; both wait for it to be ready and apply before
the command returns.
A size the fork cannot take live is reported (exit 5) and
the fork stays at its source's size.

```
  miosa fork my-box --name my-box-try --ttl 2h --wait
  miosa fork my-box -e STAGE=try -e DEBUG=1 --cpu 4 --memory 8192
```

Flags:

- `--cpu int` - Number of vCPUs of the fork.
- `--fail-fast` - Start only if a machine is ready now; otherwise fail at once and start, bill and count nothing.
- `--memory int` - Memory of the fork in MB.
- `--name string` - Name of the fork (default: derived from the source's).
- `--template string` - Template for the fork.
- `--ttl string` - Lifetime of the fork: seconds, 90m, 2h or 2d.
- `-e, --var stringArray` - Variable KEY=VALUE for the fork only (repeatable).
- `--wait` - Wait until the fork is ready for commands.

### `miosa function`

Edge functions.

```
miosa function
```

<function> is a name or id.

Aliases: `functions`, `fn`.

#### `miosa function build-log`

Show the dependency install of an uploaded project.

```
miosa function build-log <function>
```

Prints the last lines of the newest install of a function made from --dir: what the package
manager printed on the build cell. 'miosa function get' shows its state and, for a failure, the reason.

```
  miosa function build-log hook
```

#### `miosa function create`

Create a function from a source file.

```
miosa function create <name> [flags]
```

Create a function. --kind http runs it on the host function pool: a Node module whose
default export is a web handler (Request in, Response out) or a (req, res) function.
Without
--kind the server creates the original stdout-envelope script function.

```
  miosa function create hook --kind http --code-file index.js
  miosa function create hook --kind http --code-file index.js --env MODE=prod --memory-mb 512
  miosa function create api --kind http --dir ./api --runtime node

```
An http function answers 'serving: false' with a reason (no_fn_hosts, not_published) until a host
that has its runtime accepts it; invoke answers FUNCTION_NOT_PUBLISHED meanwhile.

--dir uploads a project directory instead of one file.
Its dependencies are installed on a build
cell (npm ci with a package-lock.json, pip install with a requirements.txt), so the function reads
'serving_reason: building' until that finishes; 'miosa function get' shows the build and
'miosa function build-log' its output.

Flags:

- `--code-file string` - File holding the function source.
- `--dir string` - Project directory to upload; its dependencies (package-lock.json, requirements.txt) are installed on a build cell. http functions only.
- `--entrypoint string` - Module file name inside the bundle (default index.js; for python module:attr, default main:app).
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--kind string` - Kind: script (default) or http (host function pool) (script|http).
- `--memory-mb int` - Memory in MB; an http function runs only at the sizes a host serves (default 512).
- `--project string` - Project id to attach the function to.
- `--runtime string` - Runtime (an http function supports node and python).
- `--timeout-sec int` - Per-request time limit in seconds, 1-300.
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace id.

#### `miosa function get`

Show a function.

```
miosa function get <function>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa function invoke`

Call a function.

```
miosa function invoke <function> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-d, --data string` - Payload as JSON.

#### `miosa function list`

List functions.

```
miosa function list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa function logs`

Show an http function's recent output.

```
miosa function logs <function> [flags]
```

Prints what the function's handler wrote to stdout and stderr, merged across the hosts that
serve it and oldest first.
This is a recent window kept by the running instances, not a log
store: lines from instances that were replaced or stopped are gone.

```
  miosa function logs hook
  miosa function logs hook --since 15m -n 200
```

Flags:

- `-n, --lines int` - Most lines to show (default 500, at most 2000).
- `--since string` - Only lines newer than this, such as 90s, 15m or 2h.

#### `miosa function rm`

Delete a function.

```
miosa function rm <function> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa function update`

Change a function's code or settings.

```
miosa function update <function> [flags]
```

Change a function.
Only the flags you pass are sent.
A new --code-file or a changed setting
publishes a new version of an http function to its hosts.

```
  miosa function update hook --code-file index.js
  miosa function update hook --dir ./api
  miosa function update hook --state disabled

```
--dir builds the project first; the function keeps serving its current version until the build is
ready, and the other flags of that command are applied together with it.

Flags:

- `--code-file string` - File holding the function source.
- `--dir string` - Project directory to upload; its dependencies (package-lock.json, requirements.txt) are installed on a build cell. http functions only.
- `--entrypoint string` - Module file name inside the bundle (default index.js; for python module:attr, default main:app).
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--memory-mb int` - Memory in MB; an http function runs only at the sizes a host serves (default 512).
- `--state string` - State: active or disabled (active|disabled).
- `--timeout-sec int` - Per-request time limit in seconds, 1-300.

### `miosa gateway`

The AI gateway: model routing policies, budget, limits and request traces.

```
miosa gateway
```

The AI gateway routes the model requests your applications send to MIOSA.
A
policy says which model serves which requests and what to fall back to; the
budget and limits cap spend and rate.

Reading needs the intelligence:gateway:read scope on a key, changing needs
intelligence:gateway:write.
Creating gateway keys and calling models stay in the
console and on the gateway's own address.

```
  miosa gateway policy list
  miosa gateway policy new fast --primary-model claude-haiku --alias fast
  miosa gateway budget show
  miosa gateway budget set --file budget.json
  miosa gateway requests --limit 20
```

Aliases: `ai-gateway`.

#### `miosa gateway budget`

What the gateway may spend.

```
miosa gateway budget
```

The budget is one document per organization; 'set' replaces the fields you give.

##### `miosa gateway budget set`

Set the budget from a JSON file.

```
miosa gateway budget set [flags]
```

Reads the budget document from --file (or - for standard input) and stores it.
Run 'miosa gateway budget show' for the field names.

Flags:

- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.

##### `miosa gateway budget show`

Show the budget and what is spent.

```
miosa gateway budget show
```

#### `miosa gateway environments`

List gateway environments and the keys in each.

```
miosa gateway environments
```

#### `miosa gateway health`

Show whether the gateway is serving.

```
miosa gateway health
```

#### `miosa gateway limit`

Rate and spend limits per key or environment.

```
miosa gateway limit
```

A limit applies to one scope: a gateway key or an environment.

Aliases: `limits`.

##### `miosa gateway limit list`

List limits.

```
miosa gateway limit list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa gateway limit rm`

Remove the limits of one scope.

```
miosa gateway limit rm <key|environment> <id-or-name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa gateway limit set`

Set the limits of one scope from a JSON file.

```
miosa gateway limit set <key|environment> <id-or-name> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.

#### `miosa gateway policy`

Routing policies: which model serves which requests.

```
miosa gateway policy
```

<policy> is an id.

Aliases: `policies`.

##### `miosa gateway policy get`

Show one policy.

```
miosa gateway policy get <policy>
```

##### `miosa gateway policy list`

List policies.

```
miosa gateway policy list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa gateway policy new`

Create a policy.

```
miosa gateway policy new <name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--alias string` - Model name callers use to reach this policy.
- `--enabled` - Whether the policy applies (--enabled=false turns it off).
- `--fallback-model stringArray` - Model to try when the primary fails (repeatable).
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--primary-model string` - Model that serves matching requests first.
- `--priority int` - Lower runs first when several policies match.
- `--strategy string` - How models are chosen (priority|weighted).

##### `miosa gateway policy rm`

Delete a policy.

```
miosa gateway policy rm <policy> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa gateway policy update`

Change a policy.

```
miosa gateway policy update <policy> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--alias string` - Model name callers use to reach this policy.
- `--enabled` - Whether the policy applies (--enabled=false turns it off).
- `--fallback-model stringArray` - Model to try when the primary fails (repeatable).
- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--name string` - Policy name.
- `--primary-model string` - Model that serves matching requests first.
- `--priority int` - Lower runs first when several policies match.
- `--strategy string` - How models are chosen (priority|weighted).

#### `miosa gateway provider-health`

Show the health of each model provider.

```
miosa gateway provider-health
```

#### `miosa gateway request`

Show one gateway request trace.

```
miosa gateway request <id>
```

#### `miosa gateway requests`

List recent gateway requests.

```
miosa gateway requests [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--limit int` - How many to show.
- `--model string` - Only requests for this model.
- `--status string` - Only requests with this status.

#### `miosa gateway settings`

Gateway-wide settings.

```
miosa gateway settings
```

##### `miosa gateway settings set`

Change gateway settings from a JSON file.

```
miosa gateway settings set [flags]
```

Reads the settings from --file (or - for standard input).
Turning on audio transcription needs "acknowledge_no_baa": true, because audio can contain health information.

Flags:

- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.

##### `miosa gateway settings show`

Show gateway settings.

```
miosa gateway settings show
```

### `miosa history`

List the commands run in a sandbox, or print one's stored output.

```
miosa history [sandbox] [command-id] [flags]
```

Lists the commands run in a sandbox through exec (and run), newest first: when,
how (exec or stream), the exit code and how long it took.
Give a
command id to print its stored output: standard output, or with --stderr standard
error.
The last 64 KiB of each is kept for 30 days, so this works while the
sandbox is paused or stopped. -f keeps listing new commands as they finish.

```
  miosa history my-box
  miosa history my-box --mode stream --limit 5
  miosa history my-box 3f0c...           # its output
  miosa history my-box 3f0c... --stderr
  miosa history my-box -f
```

Flags:

- `-f, --follow` - Keep listing new commands until Ctrl-C.
- `-n, --limit int` - How many commands to list (1-200). (default `20`)
- `--mode string` - Only commands that ran this way: exec or stream.
- `--stderr` - With a command id: print its standard error instead.

### `miosa host`

Your own machines enrolled in your organization (bring your own host).

```
miosa host
```

A host is a machine you own, with the MIOSA host agent installed, that your
organization can run work on: commands, containers, apps, tunnels and agents.
'host register' prints the host key once; give it to the agent on the machine.
<host> is an id.

```
  miosa host register studio --platform macos
  miosa host list
  miosa host apps catalog
  miosa host container list <host>
  miosa host tunnel list <host>
```

Aliases: `hosts`, `opencomputers`.

#### `miosa host agent`

Agents running on a host.

```
miosa host agent
```

##### `miosa host agent cancel`

Cancel an agent run on a host.

```
miosa host agent cancel <host> <run>
```

##### `miosa host agent run`

Show one agent run on a host.

```
miosa host agent run <host> <run>
```

##### `miosa host agent runs`

List agent runs on a host.

```
miosa host agent runs <host>
```

#### `miosa host app`

Apps you can install on a host.

```
miosa host app
```

The catalog lists what MIOSA can install; install puts one on a host.

Aliases: `apps`.

##### `miosa host app catalog`

List installable apps.

```
miosa host app catalog
```

##### `miosa host app health`

Check that an installed app is healthy.

```
miosa host app health <host> <app>
```

##### `miosa host app install`

Install an app on a host.

```
miosa host app install <host> <app> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.

##### `miosa host app list`

List the apps installed on a host.

```
miosa host app list <host>
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa host app start`

Start an installed app.

```
miosa host app start <host> <app>
```

##### `miosa host app uninstall`

Uninstall an app.

```
miosa host app uninstall <host> <app> [flags]
```

Aliases: `rm`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa host backup`

Scheduled backups of directories on a host.

```
miosa host backup
```

A backup configuration names a directory on a host, a schedule and how many
snapshots to keep.
A snapshot can be forked onto a host.

```
  miosa host backup list <host>
  miosa host backup new <host> --name nightly --path /data --schedule "0 3 * * *"
  miosa host backup trigger <host> nightly
  miosa host backup snapshot list <host> nightly
```

Aliases: `backups`.

##### `miosa host backup list`

List backup configurations.

```
miosa host backup list <host>
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa host backup new`

Create a backup configuration.

```
miosa host backup new <host> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--compression int` - Compression level 0-9.
- `--exclude stringArray` - Glob to exclude (repeatable).
- `--keep int` - How many snapshots to keep.
- `--keep-days int` - How many days to keep snapshots.
- `--name string` - A name for the configuration.
- `--path string` - Directory on the host to back up.
- `--schedule string` - Schedule expression, for example "0 3 * * *".
- `--timezone string` - Timezone the schedule is read in.

##### `miosa host backup pause`

Pause a backup's schedule.

```
miosa host backup pause <host> <config>
```

##### `miosa host backup resume`

Resume a backup's schedule.

```
miosa host backup resume <host> <config>
```

##### `miosa host backup rm`

Delete a backup configuration.

```
miosa host backup rm <host> <config> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa host backup snapshot`

Snapshots taken by a backup configuration.

```
miosa host backup snapshot
```

Aliases: `snapshots`.

###### `miosa host backup snapshot fork`

Write a snapshot's files onto a host.

```
miosa host backup snapshot fork <snapshot> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--file string` - Read the request body from a JSON file (- for standard input); flags override its fields.
- `--overwrite` - Overwrite files that already exist.
- `--to-host string` - Host to write onto (default: the original).
- `--to-path string` - Path to write into.
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

###### `miosa host backup snapshot get`

Show one snapshot.

```
miosa host backup snapshot get <snapshot>
```

Aliases: `show`.

###### `miosa host backup snapshot list`

List a configuration's snapshots.

```
miosa host backup snapshot list <host> <config> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--before string` - Only snapshots started before this time.
- `--limit int` - How many.

###### `miosa host backup snapshot rm`

Delete a snapshot.

```
miosa host backup snapshot rm <snapshot> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa host backup trigger`

Take a snapshot now.

```
miosa host backup trigger <host> <config>
```

##### `miosa host backup update`

Change a backup configuration.

```
miosa host backup update <host> <config> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--compression int` - Compression level 0-9.
- `--exclude stringArray` - Glob to exclude (repeatable).
- `--keep int` - How many snapshots to keep.
- `--keep-days int` - How many days to keep snapshots.
- `--name string` - A name for the configuration.
- `--path string` - Directory on the host to back up.
- `--schedule string` - Schedule expression.
- `--timezone string` - Timezone the schedule is read in.


A runner is a GitHub Actions runner the host keeps registered for one

```
  miosa host gha-runner list <host>
  miosa host gha-runner new <host> --repo https://github.com/acme/app --token <token>
  miosa host gha-runner events <host> <runner>
```

Stream a runner's activity events.

```

```

```

```

Aliases: `create`, `add`.

Flags:

- `--ephemeral` - Deregister the runner after one job.
- `--label stringArray` - Runner label (repeatable).
- `--name string` - Runner name (default: generated).
- `--repo string` - Repository URL, for example https://github.com/acme/app.
- `--token string` - GitHub registration token (sent to the host, not stored).
- `--token-secret string` - Name of a host secret holding the token.

Reinstall and restart a runner.


### `miosa import`

Load a CSV into a managed database, or a Notion export into an Engine.

```
miosa import <file> | --from bucket:<bucket>/<key> --to <target> [flags]
```

Load a CSV into a table of a managed Postgres database, or a Notion export zip
into a workspace's Optimal Engine.

```
  miosa import ./people.csv --to db:app-db/people
  miosa import ./notion.zip --to engine:research --watch

```
A local file is uploaded to the organization's hidden 'imports' bucket under a
content-addressed key and imported from there, so the same file lands on the
same object twice.
A file already in storage can be named instead:

```
  miosa import --from bucket:exports/people.csv --to db:app-db/people

```
Types are inferred from the values (bigint, numeric, boolean, timestamptz,
text).
A column that needs another type can be pinned:

```
  miosa import ./people.csv --to db:app-db/people --override zip=text

```
An Engine import makes every page one context at optimal://<workspace>/notion/<page>,
with parent and link edges, and is searchable when the job reports done.
It is a
durable job: '--watch' follows it, and 'miosa import --job <id> --to engine:<workspace>'
reprints it later.

Every import prints its job id and its counts; refused rows come with their line
numbers.

Flags:

- `--from string` - Source: bucket:<bucket>/<key>.
- `--idempotency-key string` - Engine import key; a random one is used when empty, and repeating it returns the same job.
- `--job string` - Reprint one Engine import job by id.
- `--override stringArray` - Pin a column's type: column=text (repeatable).
- `--to string` - Destination: db:<database>/<table> or engine:<workspace>.
- `--watch` - Follow an Engine import until it finishes.

#### `miosa import railway`

Import a Railway project (dry run unless --apply).

```
miosa import railway [flags]
```

Read one Railway project through Railway's public API with your own token and
write a plan: the MIOSA project, Deploy targets, services, databases and variable
names it would create, and everything it cannot carry (build commands, health
checks, volumes, Railway domains, custom domains).

```
  miosa import railway --project acme-api                      # review the plan
  miosa import railway --project acme-api --out plan.json      # keep it
  miosa import railway --apply --plan plan.json                # create it

```
The token is read from RAILWAY_TOKEN, --token-file, or a prompt.
It is never
stored.
Variable values are not copied unless you pass --copy-variables, which
prints a warning: Railway disclosed on 2026-10-21 that its secrets were exposed, so rotate them.

With --from-file, a railway.json or railway.toml is read locally and no token
or network call is needed.

RAILWAY_API_URL overrides the Railway endpoint (for a proxy or recorded
fixtures).

Guide: https://miosa.ai/docs/migrate/railway

Flags:

- `--allow-missing-values` - Create variables with no value as empty strings.
- `--apply` - Create what the plan says.
- `--cleanup` - Delete exactly the ids recorded in --state.
- `--copy-variables` - Copy variable values, not just names (prints a warning).
- `--environment string` - Railway environment to import. (default `production`)
- `--exclude stringSlice` - Leave these services out (comma separated).
- `--from-file string` - Read railway.json or railway.toml instead of the API.
- `--include-targets` - Also import other Railway environments as Deploy targets.
- `--into string` - Import into an existing MIOSA project.
- `--name string` - MIOSA project name (default: the Railway project name).
- `--only stringSlice` - Import only these services (comma separated).
- `--out string` - Write the plan JSON here.
- `--plan string` - With --apply: the plan to apply.
- `--project string` - Railway project id or name.
- `--region string` - With --apply: MIOSA region for services whose Railway region has no equivalent.
- `--resume` - Continue an interrupted apply.
- `--service-name string` - With --from-file: the service name.
- `--state string` - Where created ids are recorded (default .miosa/import-railway-<project>.json).
- `--suffix string` - Append this to every service name and insert it before each custom domain's first dot.
- `--token string` - Railway token (prefer RAILWAY_TOKEN or --token-file).
- `--token-file string` - File holding the Railway token.
- `--via string` - How to read Railway: token (default) or railway-cli (its logged-in session).
- `--workspace string` - MIOSA workspace (slug, name or id).
- `--yes` - With --cleanup: do not ask.

#### `miosa import vercel`

Read a vercel.json and write a reviewable plan.

```
miosa import vercel --from-file vercel.json [flags]
```

Read a Vercel project's vercel.json and write a plan: the MIOSA project,
the production Deploy target, one service, and the environment variable names
it declares.
Review the plan, then --apply it.

What maps: the app, its region (when the file names one MIOSA has), and the
environment variable names.
What does not, and is listed with the reason:
rewrites, redirects, headers, builds, the functions block, cleanUrls,
trailingSlash, buildCommand, installCommand, outputDirectory and crons.

```
  miosa import vercel --from-file vercel.json --name shop --repo https://github.com/acme/shop
  miosa import vercel --from-file vercel.json --out plan.json
  miosa import vercel --apply --plan plan.json
```

Flags:

- `--allow-missing-values` - Create variables with no value as empty strings.
- `--apply` - Create what the plan says.
- `--from-file string` - The vercel.json to read.
- `--into string` - Import into an existing MIOSA project.
- `--name string` - MIOSA project name (default: vercel-app).
- `--out string` - Write the plan JSON here.
- `--plan string` - With --apply: the plan to apply.
- `--region string` - Region: us-east, us-west or us-mia (overrides the file).
- `--repo string` - Git URL for the service's source.
- `--resume` - Continue an interrupted apply.
- `--service string` - Service name inside the project. (default `web`)
- `--state string` - Where created ids are recorded (default .miosa/import-vercel-<project>.json).
- `--workspace string` - MIOSA workspace (slug, name or id).

### `miosa info`

Show a machine's state, environment and setup status.

```
miosa info [name|id]
```

Show a sandbox (or computer): state, size, the environment and version it
is pinned to, whether an upgrade is available, and the status and error of its
setup script.

Example:
```
  miosa info my-box
  miosa info            # uses current sandbox
```

Aliases: `status`.

### `miosa interrupt`

Stop the agent running in a sandbox, or one chat's running turn.

```
miosa interrupt [sandbox] [flags]
```

Stops the agent run in a sandbox (default: the current one). --convo <chat-id>
stops the running turn of one chat instead, wherever it runs.

```
  miosa interrupt my-box
  miosa interrupt --convo 3f0c...
```

Flags:

- `--convo string` - Stop this chat's running turn (a chat id).

### `miosa limits`

Limits of the organization a new machine would bill.

```
miosa limits
```

Show what the next create would be charged to and whether it can start: the
organization that pays, its available credits, running-machine limit, sandbox
spend cap, and your own caps in it.
It changes nothing.

Use --bill-to to ask about another organization you can bill.

### `miosa link`

Attach this directory to a project, Deploy target and service.

```
miosa link <project> [flags]
```

Write the project, Deploy target and service to .miosa/link.json so later
commands need no arguments.

```
  miosa link cliniciq
  miosa link cliniciq --environment staging --service API
  miosa status            # the linked project and service
  miosa unlink

```
The link holds names and ids, never a key.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Alias of --environment.
- `--environment string` - Deploy target (default: the project's default).
- `--service string` - Service to link.

### `miosa list`

List sandboxes.

```
miosa list [flags]
```

List sandboxes in your organization, newest first.

Filters run on the server.
One page is shown by default (--limit, max 100);
--all follows every page.
Errored sandboxes are hidden unless you ask for them
with --errored or --state error.

```
  miosa list
  miosa list --state running
  miosa list --tag env=staging --search api
  miosa list --all --json | jq -r '.data[].id'
  miosa list -q                      # ids only, one per line
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--all` - Follow every page.
- `--all-orgs` - List every organization your sign-in covers, with an ORG column.
- `--errored` - Include sandboxes in the error state.
- `--ids` - Print only ids, one per line.
- `--limit int` - Page size, 1-100 (default 50).
- `--page int` - Page number.
- `--search string` - Match on id, name or template.
- `--state string` - Only this state, for example running or paused.
- `--tag stringArray` - Only sandboxes with this tag, KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--template string` - Only this template.
- `--workspace string` - Filter by workspace slug.
- `--workspace-id string` - Filter by workspace id (server side).

### `miosa login`

Sign in to MIOSA.

```
miosa login [flags]
```

Sign in and store an API key for this machine.

With no flags on a terminal, login opens your browser, shows a short code to
confirm there, and stores the key MIOSA issues for this CLI.
On that page you
choose which of your organizations this terminal may work in (all of them, or
some) and which one it starts in; one key covers them all.
Switch with
'miosa org use <org>', change the set with 'miosa org select'.

For scripts and CI, pass an existing key without putting it in the process
arguments:

```
  miosa login --key-stdin <<< "$MIOSA_API_KEY"

```
or skip login and export MIOSA_API_KEY.

```
  miosa login                       # browser sign-in
  miosa login --all-orgs            # every organization pre-selected
  miosa login --org osa --org acme  # these organizations pre-selected
  miosa login --no-browser          # print the URL instead of opening it
  miosa login --with-key            # paste a key (input hidden)
  miosa login --profile work        # store as a separate profile

```
For an agent that cannot keep a command waiting, sign-in is two steps: the
first prints the link and code for a person to approve and returns at once,
the second waits for the approval and stores the key.
Running the first again
shows the same pending link until it expires; --new starts over.

```
  miosa login --no-wait --json      # {"url": ..., "user_code": ..., "expires_at": ...}
  miosa login --wait --json         # after the person approved

```
--github, --google and --email go straight to that way of signing in on the
sign-in page; --signup opens account creation instead, for someone new to MIOSA.

Flags:

- `--all-orgs` - Pre-select every organization on the approval page.
- `--device` - Use the browser flow even when stdin is not a terminal.
- `--email` - Sign in with your email address.
- `--github` - Sign in with GitHub.
- `--google` - Sign in with Google.
- `--key-stdin` - Read the API key from standard input.
- `--new` - With --no-wait: start a new sign-in instead of showing the pending one.
- `--no-browser` - Print the sign-in URL instead of opening a browser.
- `--no-wait` - Start a browser sign-in, print its link and code, and return (finish with --wait).
- `--org stringArray` - Pre-select this organization on the approval page (repeatable).
- `--signup` - Create a MIOSA account instead of signing in to one.
- `--wait` - Finish the sign-in that --no-wait started: wait for approval, then store the key.
- `--with-key` - Paste an API key instead of using the browser.

### `miosa logout`

Sign out: revoke the stored key on the server and remove it.

```
miosa logout [flags]
```

Sign out of the current profile.
The key 'miosa login' created is revoked on
the server first, then the profile's API key, current sandbox and the Forge key
it minted for git (if any) are removed.
A browser session is ended the same way.

With --all every profile is signed out and the config file is deleted.

A key you stored yourself (not created by 'miosa login') is removed from the
profile but left active; a key given with --api-key or MIOSA_API_KEY is never
revoked.
If the server cannot be reached, the keys are still removed
locally and the command exits non-zero, naming the key that is still active.

Flags:

- `--all` - Sign out of every profile and delete the config file.

Examples:

```
miosa logout
  miosa logout --profile work
  miosa logout --all
```

### `miosa logs`

Show a sandbox's system log.

```
miosa logs [sandbox] [flags]
```

Print the last lines of the sandbox's boot and system log; --follow keeps streaming.
For a background process use 'miosa process logs'.
With --json each line is an object.

```
  miosa logs my-box --lines 200
  miosa logs my-box -f
```

Flags:

- `--build` - With a link: the service's build log instead of its runtime log.
- `-f, --follow` - Keep streaming new lines.
- `-n, --lines int` - Number of recent lines. (default `100`)

### `miosa mcp`

Connect AI clients to the MIOSA Model Context Protocol server.

```
miosa mcp
```

MIOSA serves a Model Context Protocol server at <api-url>/mcp over
Streamable HTTP, authenticated with the same API key as the rest of the CLI.
It gives an AI client tools to create and manage sandboxes, computers,
deployments, storage, databases and volumes.

```
  miosa mcp tools                       list the tools the server exposes
  miosa mcp install --client claude     add the server to Claude Code's config
  miosa mcp install --client cursor     add the server to Cursor's config
  miosa mcp serve                       run a stdio bridge for clients that need one

```
The 'server' and 'catalog' subcommands manage the tool servers your agents
call, and the pre-built catalogue to add them from.

#### `miosa mcp catalog`

Add a tool server from the pre-built catalogue.

```
miosa mcp catalog
```

The catalogue lists tool servers that are ready to add, each with the method it
takes to authenticate.
Try one before adding it, then install it as a tool server.

```
  miosa mcp catalog list
  miosa mcp catalog test github --value token=ghp_x
  miosa mcp catalog install github --name github --value token=ghp_x
```

Aliases: `catalogue`.

##### `miosa mcp catalog install`

Add a catalogue entry as a tool server.

```
miosa mcp catalog install <slug> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--method string` - The authentication method to use.
- `--name string` - Name for the new tool server.
- `--value stringArray` - NAME=VALUE for the method (repeatable).
- `--workspace-id string` - Add it to this workspace.

##### `miosa mcp catalog list`

List the catalogue entries.

```
miosa mcp catalog list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--workspace-id string` - Include what this workspace already added.

##### `miosa mcp catalog test`

Check a catalogue entry before adding it.

```
miosa mcp catalog test <slug> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--method string` - The authentication method to use.
- `--value stringArray` - NAME=VALUE for the method (repeatable).

#### `miosa mcp install`

Add MIOSA MCP to an AI client's config.

```
miosa mcp install [flags]
```

Writes the hosted MCP endpoint (and your API key) into an AI client's config
file, keeping the other servers it already lists. --print shows the change
without writing anything.

```
  miosa mcp install                          Claude Code, user scope
  miosa mcp install --client cursor          Cursor
  miosa mcp install --client gemini          Gemini CLI
  miosa mcp install --client claude --scope project   a shared .mcp.json
```

Flags:

- `--client string` - Client to configure: claude, cursor, gemini or manual. (default `claude`)
- `--force` - Overwrite an existing entry of the same name.
- `--name string` - Name to register MIOSA MCP under. (default `miosa`)
- `--print` - Print the config instead of writing it.
- `--scope string` - Config scope for claude: user or project. (default `user`)
- `--url string` - MCP endpoint (default: the API base URL + /mcp).

#### `miosa mcp serve`

Run a stdio bridge to MIOSA MCP for clients that need one.

```
miosa mcp serve [flags]
```

Reads newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 messages from standard input, forwards
each to MIOSA MCP, and writes the response as one line on standard output.
Notifications (which have no response) produce no output.

```
  {
    "mcpServers": {
      "miosa": {"command": "miosa", "args": ["mcp", "serve"]}
    }
  }
```

Flags:

- `--url string` - MCP endpoint (default: the API base URL + /mcp).

#### `miosa mcp server`

Tool servers agents can call.

```
miosa mcp server
```

A tool server is a tool your agents can call over MCP.
Define it once with
its secret stored encrypted, and every agent allowed to use it inherits the
connection.

```
  list                 the tool servers you defined
  add github           define one, with its transport, url and secrets
  test <server>        reach it and list its tools
  rm <server>          delete it

```
Secrets are given as --secret NAME=VALUE and are never returned.

Aliases: `servers`.

##### `miosa mcp server add`

Define a tool server.

```
miosa mcp server add <name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--args stringArray` - An argument for the command (repeatable).
- `--command string` - The command to run (stdio).
- `--secret stringArray` - NAME=VALUE stored encrypted, never returned (repeatable).
- `--secret-env stringArray` - An environment variable from --secret (repeatable).
- `--secret-header stringArray` - A header whose value comes from --secret (repeatable).
- `--transport string` - Transport to reach it: http, stdio or sse.
- `--url string` - The server URL (http and sse).
- `--workspace-id string` - Bind the server to one workspace.

##### `miosa mcp server get`

Show one tool server.

```
miosa mcp server get <server>
```

Aliases: `show`.

##### `miosa mcp server list`

List the tool servers.

```
miosa mcp server list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--status string` - Only servers in this status.
- `--workspace-id string` - Only the servers of this workspace.

##### `miosa mcp server rm`

Delete a tool server.

```
miosa mcp server rm <server> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa mcp server test`

Reach the server and list its tools.

```
miosa mcp server test <server>
```

#### `miosa mcp tools`

List the tools MIOSA MCP exposes.

```
miosa mcp tools [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--url string` - MCP endpoint (default: the API base URL + /mcp).

### `miosa member`

People in your organization.

```
miosa member
```

Roles: owner, admin, member, viewer.
Only owners and admins can change membership.

Aliases: `members`, `team`.

#### `miosa member add`

Add a member.

```
miosa member add <email> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--role string` - Role (admin|member|viewer). (default `member`)

#### `miosa member list`

List members.

```
miosa member list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa member rm`

Remove a member.

```
miosa member rm <member-id> [flags]
```

Aliases: `remove`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa member role`

Change a member's role.

```
miosa member role <member-id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--role string` - New role (admin|member|viewer).

### `miosa middleware`

Show, set or clear a service's edge middleware.

```
miosa middleware <project> <service> [flags]
```

Edge middleware runs in front of a server service: ordered path rules
(redirect, rewrite, request and response headers) and, optionally, a published
function that can answer, continue or rewrite.

```
  miosa middleware shop API --from middleware.json
  miosa middleware shop API            # what is configured now
  miosa middleware shop API --off

```
The file is the rule set exactly as the API takes it:

```
  {
    "rules": [
      {"path": "/old/*", "redirect": {"to": "/new", "status": 308}},
      {"path": "/api/*", "request_headers": {"set": {"x-plan": "pro"}}}
    ],
    "function": {"slug": "gate", "paths": ["/app/*"], "timeout_ms": 250, "on_error": "closed"}
  }

```
Rules and the function are validated by the API; its error names the path
(settings.edge_middleware.<field>).
Changes apply within about five seconds.
Protected header names (host, content-length, x-miosa-*, x-forwarded-* and
the rest) are refused.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--from string` - JSON file holding the rule set.
- `--off` - Remove every rule and the function.

### `miosa module`

Apply a manifest of resources to a workspace.

```
miosa module
```

A module is a named set of buckets, volumes, databases, schedules, agents and interfaces
declared in one YAML file.

```
  module: wo28
  resources:
    - {kind: bucket, name: wo28-assets, spec: {visibility: private}}
    - {kind: schedule, name: wo28-nightly, spec: {schedule: "0 2 * * *", command: "true"}}
    - {kind: agent, name: wo28-reviewer, spec: {configuration: {instructions: "Review the queue."}}}
    - {kind: interface, name: wo28-web, spec: {project_id: <project>, kind: static}}

```
An interface is a node of a project's canvas, so it names its project in spec.project_id.
plan and diff change nothing. apply creates what is missing and updates what drifted; run twice it
changes nothing the second time. remove deletes exactly the manifest's resources.
A literal secret in
a spec is refused: reference it as ${NAME}, secret://<name> or vault:<name>.

#### `miosa module apply`

Create and update the manifest's resources.

```
miosa module apply <manifest.yaml> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (required).

#### `miosa module diff`

Show where the workspace differs from the manifest.

```
miosa module diff <manifest.yaml> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (required).

#### `miosa module plan`

Show what apply would create or change.

```
miosa module plan <manifest.yaml> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (required).

#### `miosa module remove`

Delete the manifest's resources.

```
miosa module remove <manifest.yaml> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--dry-run` - List what would be removed and remove nothing.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (required).
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

### `miosa move`

Move a resource to another workspace.

```
miosa move <resource> [flags]
```

Move a resource to another workspace of the same organization.

```
  miosa move app-db --to-workspace staging
  miosa move assets --type bucket --to-workspace staging

```
<resource> is a name or id.
Without --type the resource is looked up among
sandbox, computer, deployment, database, bucket, volume, schedule, agent, webhook, project; a name that matches more than one kind
needs --type.
A move changes ownership and scope only: names, hostnames,
connection strings and object URLs keep working, and the data is untouched.
A
running database, deployment or machine must be stopped first.
Moving needs
admin rights in both workspaces.

Cross-organization transfer (deployments today) takes the target organization
and the resource's name, and bills the target organization from the transfer:

```
  miosa move my-app --to-org acme-eu --to-workspace production --confirm my-app
```

Flags:

- `--accept-env-changes` - Move a deployment even though the target's workspace variables differ.
- `--confirm string` - The resource's name, required for a cross-organization transfer.
- `--detach-from-project` - Leave its project behind (deployments, databases, buckets).
- `--dry-run` - Run every check and show the plan without moving anything (deployments, databases, buckets).
- `--project string` - Project of the target workspace to put it in (deployments, databases, buckets).
- `--to-org string` - Transfer to this organization (id or slug); needs --to-workspace and --confirm.
- `--to-workspace string` - The workspace to move it to (slug, name or id).
- `--type string` - Kind of resource: sandbox, computer, deployment, database, bucket, volume, schedule, agent, webhook, project.

### `miosa notifications`

Your notifications.

```
miosa notifications
```

Aliases: `notification`, `inbox`.

#### `miosa notifications list`

List notifications.

```
miosa notifications list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa notifications read`

Mark one read.

```
miosa notifications read <id>
```

#### `miosa notifications read-all`

Mark all read.

```
miosa notifications read-all
```

#### `miosa notifications unread`

Count unread notifications.

```
miosa notifications unread
```

### `miosa onboard`

Get set up: sign in, then only what the account still lacks.

```
miosa onboard [flags]
```

Walks a new machine or a new account to a working setup, and is safe to run
again: each step that is already done is only reported.

```
  1. Sign in, when this CLI is not signed in (the browser flow of 'miosa login').
  2. Show the organization and the workspace new sandboxes land in.
  3. Show the plan. On Free, offer the paid plans; picking one opens its
     checkout and waits for the payment (Ctrl-C stops waiting, not the payment).

  miosa onboard                       # interactive
  miosa onboard --github              # sign in with GitHub
  miosa onboard --signup              # create an account first
  miosa onboard --plan developer      # go straight to that plan's checkout

```
For an agent, onboarding is two steps like 'login': the first prints the link
and code for a person to approve and returns, the second waits for the approval
and finishes the rest.

```
  miosa onboard --json                # {"status":"pending","url":...,"next":"miosa onboard --wait"}
  miosa onboard --wait --json         # after the person approved
```

Flags:

- `--email` - Sign in with your email address.
- `--github` - Sign in with GitHub.
- `--google` - Sign in with Google.
- `--no-open` - Print links instead of opening a browser.
- `--no-wait` - Return once the checkout exists, without waiting for the payment.
- `--plan string` - Plan to subscribe to (e.g. developer); opens its checkout.
- `--signup` - Create a MIOSA account instead of signing in to one.
- `--wait` - Finish the sign-in a first 'onboard' started, then the rest.

### `miosa open`

Open the web console (or print its URL).

```
miosa open [kind] [id] [flags]
```

Open the MIOSA console at a resource.
With no arguments it opens the dashboard.
Kinds: agent, api-keys, billing, computer, database, deployment, docs, logs, members, run, sandbox, settings, storage, template, usage, webhooks, workflow.
A sandbox may be given by name.

```
  miosa open sandbox my-box
  miosa open run 4f2c...
  miosa open billing
  miosa open --print sandbox my-box   # just print the URL
```

Flags:

- `--print` - Print the URL instead of opening it.

### `miosa optimal`

Chat with Optimal and manage its chats from the terminal.

```
miosa optimal
```

Optimal is MIOSA's operator: it reads your workspaces and acts on them with
your approval.
These commands use your API key (osa:write).
Optimal gets exactly your own
access for a few minutes at a time, never more.

A chat lives in one workspace (--workspace), or reads every workspace you can
open (--org-wide).
An org-wide chat never guesses where to write: it asks which
workspace, and every change it makes becomes an approval in that workspace.

```
  miosa optimal chat "how many sandboxes are in each workspace?" --org-wide
  miosa optimal chat "create a sandbox named web in staging" --org-wide --json
  miosa optimal chat "and its size?" --workspace staging --chat <id>
  miosa optimal chats --org-wide
  miosa optimal runs --workspace staging
  miosa optimal cancel <run>
  miosa optimal approvals --workspace staging
  miosa optimal approve <approval> --workspace staging

```
Deciding an approval needs a key with the osa:approve scope.

#### `miosa optimal approvals`

List the approvals Optimal is waiting on.

```
miosa optimal approvals [flags]
```

Every change Optimal makes waits for an approval in the workspace it changes.
In a workspace (--workspace) this lists that workspace's pending approvals.
Org-wide
(--org-wide) it lists the approvals of your running org-wide chats, each in its own
workspace; --chat narrows to one chat.

```
  miosa optimal approvals --workspace staging
  miosa optimal approve <approval> --workspace staging
```

Flags:

- `--chat string` - Only this chat's approvals.
- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

#### `miosa optimal approve`

Approve a change Optimal is waiting on.

```
miosa optimal approve <approval> [flags]
```

Decides one approval from 'miosa optimal approvals'.
Use the workspace the change is
in (--workspace), or --org-wide --chat <chat> for an org-wide chat's approval.
The API key needs the osa:approve scope; a key without it can chat but not decide.

Flags:

- `--chat string` - The org-wide chat the approval belongs to.
- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `--reason string` - Why (kept with the decision).
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

#### `miosa optimal cancel`

Stop a run (or the running turn of a chat).

```
miosa optimal cancel <run|chat> [flags]
```

Stops a run.
Pass a run id (run_...) from 'miosa optimal runs', or a chat id to stop
whatever that chat is running.
A run waiting on an approval stops too: its pending
approvals are declined.

Flags:

- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

#### `miosa optimal chat`

Send Optimal a message and stream its answer.

```
miosa optimal chat <message...> [flags]
```

Send a message and stream what Optimal does until it answers, asks you something
(which workspace, or an approval), or fails.
Without --chat a new chat starts and
its id is printed; pass it to --chat to continue.

An org-wide chat asks which workspace before it changes anything ("needs_workspace").
Answer with --act-in <workspace> on the same chat; the change then waits for your
approval in that workspace.

With --json each event is one line: {"type", "chat_id", "scope", ...}.
The answer
is the line with "type":"message"; "scope" is "org-wide" or "workspace".

Flags:

- `--act-in string` - Org-wide chat: the workspace (name or id) its changes apply to.
- `--chat string` - Continue this chat (its id) instead of starting one.
- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `--timeout duration` - Stop following after this long (the chat keeps working). (default `10m0s`)
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

#### `miosa optimal chats`

List Optimal chats, newest first.

```
miosa optimal chats [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many chats. (default `20`)
- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

#### `miosa optimal reject`

Reject a change Optimal is waiting on.

```
miosa optimal reject <approval> [flags]
```

Decides one approval from 'miosa optimal approvals'.
Use the workspace the change is
in (--workspace), or --org-wide --chat <chat> for an org-wide chat's approval.
The API key needs the osa:approve scope; a key without it can chat but not decide.

Flags:

- `--chat string` - The org-wide chat the approval belongs to.
- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `--reason string` - Why (kept with the decision).
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

#### `miosa optimal run`

Show one run: its state, chat, workspace and credits.

```
miosa optimal run <run> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

#### `miosa optimal runs`

List Optimal's runs, newest first (running ones by default).

```
miosa optimal runs [flags]
```

A run is one turn of a chat: what Optimal did for one message, and how it ended
(running, succeeded, failed or canceled).
By default only
running runs are listed; --all lists every recent run, --state one state.

```
  miosa optimal runs --org-wide
  miosa optimal runs --workspace staging --all --json
  miosa optimal run <run>
  miosa optimal cancel <run>
```

Flags:

- `--all` - Every recent run, not only running ones.
- `-n, --limit int` - How many runs. (default `50`)
- `--org-wide` - Read every workspace you can open (an organization chat).
- `--state string` - Only runs in this state: running, succeeded, failed, canceled.
- `-w, --workspace string` - The workspace (name or id) the chat lives in.

### `miosa org`

The organization you work in, who pays for new machines, members and limits.

```
miosa org
```

Show and choose the organization that pays for new sandboxes and computers.

```
  miosa org                       who pays right now
  miosa org list                  organizations you belong to
  miosa org bill acme             new machines bill acme, in the console, the CLI and the API
  miosa org bill --clear          bill the organization your key belongs to again
  miosa --bill-to acme create my-box  bill one create to acme without changing the setting
  miosa --org acme deploy list        work in acme; refused (exit 3) if this key does not act in acme
  miosa org use acme              work in acme from now on (a key that covers several orgs)
  miosa org select                change which organizations this terminal may work in
  miosa org members               members of the organization your key belongs to
  miosa org invite dana@example.com
  miosa org caps                  per-member spend and concurrency caps
  miosa org caps set dana --spend 25 --concurrent 3
  miosa org transfer dana@example.com   make dana the owner (the owner only)
  miosa org create "Acme Labs"     create an organization you own
  miosa org default-visibility set private   new sandboxes start private

```
A machine keeps billing the organization it was created under, even if you
change this later.
API keys issued in an organization other than your personal
one are locked to that organization and cannot bill another.

#### `miosa org apply`

Create the workspaces and projects a topology file lists.

```
miosa org apply <topology.yaml> [flags]
```

Reads the company topology: every workspace under entities, and its projects, are created if
missing.
It never deletes.
Workspaces in the organization that the file does not list, other than the
keep_workspaces ones, are reported as extras and left alone.
A second run creates nothing.

```
  miosa --org osa org apply topology/miosa-company.yaml --dry-run

```
The entity, access and bridge attributes are not stored yet (they need workspace metadata); members
and invites are never created.

Flags:

- `--dry-run` - Show what would be created and create nothing.

#### `miosa org bill`

Choose the organization that pays for new machines.

```
miosa org bill [org] [flags]
```

Pin the organization (id, slug or name) that pays for new sandboxes and
computers, for your whole account.
With --clear, go back to billing the
organization your key belongs to.

Flags:

- `--clear` - Stop pinning an organization.

#### `miosa org caps`

Per-member spend and concurrency caps (owners and admins).

```
miosa org caps
```

Everyone in an organization shares its balance.
Caps limit what one member's
sandboxes can spend in a billing period and how many they can run at once.
A member over a cap cannot start or resume that organization's machines, and
their running machines there are snapshot-stopped within about a minute.
Other
members, and that member's machines in other organizations, are not affected.

##### `miosa org caps clear`

Remove both caps from a member.

```
miosa org caps clear <member>
```

##### `miosa org caps list`

List members with their caps and usage.

```
miosa org caps list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa org caps set`

Set a member's caps.

```
miosa org caps set <member> [flags]
```

Set a member's spend cap (dollars per billing period) and/or the most machines
they can run at once. <member> is an email, name or user id.
A flag you leave
out keeps its current value.

Flags:

- `--concurrent string` - Most machines the member can run at once.
- `--no-concurrency-cap` - Remove the running-machines limit.
- `--no-spend-cap` - Remove the spend cap.
- `--spend string` - Spend cap in dollars per billing period, for example 25 or 25.50.

Examples:

```
miosa org caps set dana@example.com --spend 25 --concurrent 3
  miosa org caps set dana@example.com --no-spend-cap
```

#### `miosa org create`

Create an organization you own.

```
miosa org create <name> [flags]
```

Create a new organization with you as its owner.
Its URL slug is made from the
name.
You can own one company organization (and have one personal organization).

The key you signed in with stays in its own organization.
To work in the new one,
run 'miosa login' and pick it.

```
  miosa org create "Acme Labs"
```

Flags:

- `--personal` - Create your personal organization instead of a company one.

#### `miosa org default-visibility`

Whether new sandboxes start shared or private.

```
miosa org default-visibility
```

New sandboxes start shared: every member of the organization may use them.
An
organization owner or admin can make them start private instead.
Existing sandboxes keep
theirs; change one with 'miosa share' or 'miosa unshare', or pick at create time with
'miosa create --visibility private'.

```
  miosa org default-visibility show
  miosa org default-visibility set private
```

##### `miosa org default-visibility set`

Set the visibility new sandboxes start with (owners and admins).

```
miosa org default-visibility set <shared|private>
```

##### `miosa org default-visibility show`

Show the visibility new sandboxes start with.

```
miosa org default-visibility show
```

#### `miosa org delete`

Organizations cannot be deleted from the CLI.

```
miosa org delete [org]
```

Deleting an organization removes every machine, database and key in it, so it is
never done with an API key.
Its owner can ask MIOSA support to delete it
('miosa feedback' or the dashboard's Help menu).

#### `miosa org diff`

Compare the organization with a topology file: undeclared, missing, untagged.

```
miosa org diff --file <topology.yaml> [flags]
```

Compare the live organization with a desired-state topology file.

```
  miosa org diff --file topology/org.yaml
  miosa org diff --file topology.yaml --json
  miosa org diff --file topology.yaml --no-tags      # skip the resource tag scan

```
Reported, in both directions:

```
  undeclared   exists in the organization, not in the file
  missing      in the file, not in the organization
  untagged     sandboxes in a declared workspace with no purpose tag

```
keep_workspaces are never reported as undeclared.
Exits 1 when anything is
reported, so a deploy can gate on it.

Flags:

- `--file string` - Path to the topology file.
- `--tags` - Scan declared workspaces for resources with no purpose tag. (default `true`)

#### `miosa org invite`

Invite someone to the organization your key belongs to.

```
miosa org invite <email> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--role string` - Role to give: owner, admin or member. (default `member`)

#### `miosa org limits`

Same as 'miosa limits': who pays and whether a create can start.

```
miosa org limits
```

#### `miosa org list`

List the organizations you can bill.

```
miosa org list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa org members`

List the members of the organization your key belongs to.

```
miosa org members
```

#### `miosa org select`

Change which organizations this terminal may work in (re-approve in the browser).

```
miosa org select [flags]
```

Opens the sign-in page with the organizations this key covers already ticked,
so you can add or remove some and approve again.
The new key replaces the old
one; nothing else changes.

Flags:

- `--all` - Pre-select every organization.

Examples:

```
miosa org select
  miosa org select --all
```

#### `miosa org transfer`

Make another member the owner of the organization.

```
miosa org transfer <member> [flags]
```

Hand the organization's ownership to another member, named by email or user
id.
Only the current owner can do this, and only the new owner can hand it
back.
It asks you to type the organization's slug unless --yes.

```
  miosa org transfer dana@example.com
  miosa --org acme org transfer dana@example.com --yes
```

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa org use`

Work in this organization from now on (this profile).

```
miosa org use <org>
```

Sets the organization commands work in, like 'gh auth switch'.
The key must
cover it ('miosa org list' shows which do); --org and MIOSA_ORG still override
it for one command.
A key that covers one organization only cannot switch:
sign in with 'miosa login --all-orgs' or add organizations with 'miosa org select'.

Examples:

```
miosa org use osa
  miosa org use commas && miosa deploy list
```

### `miosa pause`

Pause a sandbox (it keeps its disk and memory state).

```
miosa pause [sandbox]
```

Pause a running sandbox.
Compute billing stops; storage keeps billing at the paused rate.
Resume it with 'miosa resume'.
Memory is checkpointed, so processes continue where they were.

### `miosa policy`

Show or change a computer's network policy.

```
miosa policy
```

Show, apply or reset the egress network policy of a computer (a name or id).
Sandboxes use organization-wide egress policies instead; see 'miosa egress'.

#### `miosa policy reset`

Remove the policy and return to the default.

```
miosa policy reset <computer>
```

#### `miosa policy set`

Apply a network policy from a YAML or JSON file.

```
miosa policy set <computer> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-f, --file string` - Path to the policy YAML or JSON file (required).

#### `miosa policy show`

Show the current network policy.

```
miosa policy show <computer>
```

### `miosa ports`

List the ports listening inside a sandbox.

```
miosa ports [sandbox]
```

### `miosa preview`

Publish a sandbox port at a public URL.

```
miosa preview
```

A preview exposes a port that a process in the sandbox is listening on.
Private
previews need your key or a share link; 'preview share' makes one reachable by
anyone with the link for a limited time.
The port must already be listening
(see 'miosa ports').

```
  miosa process start my-box -- python -m http.server 8080
  miosa preview create my-box 8080 --name web
  miosa preview share my-box <id> --ttl 1h
```

Aliases: `previews`.

#### `miosa preview create`

Expose a port.

```
miosa preview create [sandbox] <port> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--name string` - A label for the preview.
- `--ttl string` - Remove it after this long: seconds, 30m, 2h.
- `--visibility string` - Who can open it (private|public). (default `private`)

#### `miosa preview domain-add`

Serve the sandbox's preview at your own domain (then add the DNS record).

```
miosa preview domain-add [sandbox] <hostname>
```

Attaches a custom domain to the sandbox: it serves the sandbox's newest active preview, with
that preview's visibility.
Point a CNAME at proxy.miosa.ai; the domain verifies on its own once
DNS is right (or run 'miosa deploy domain-verify <hostname>').

Examples:

```
miosa preview domain-add my-box app.example.com
```

#### `miosa preview get`

Show a preview.

```
miosa preview get [sandbox] <id>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa preview list`

List previews.

```
miosa preview list [sandbox]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa preview rm`

Remove a preview.

```
miosa preview rm [sandbox] <id>
```

Aliases: `delete`.

#### `miosa preview share`

Make a preview reachable by anyone with its link.

```
miosa preview share [sandbox] <id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--ttl string` - Link lifetime: seconds, 30m, 2h.

#### `miosa preview status`

Check that a preview answers (process, TLS, route).

```
miosa preview status [sandbox] <id>
```

#### `miosa preview unshare`

Revoke a preview's public link.

```
miosa preview unshare [sandbox] <id>
```

### `miosa process`

Run and manage background processes in a sandbox.

```
miosa process
```

A process keeps running after the CLI disconnects and its output is kept.
Use it for
servers and long jobs; use 'exec' for a command whose result you wait for.

```
  miosa process start my-box --name api -- node server.js
  miosa process list
  miosa process logs <id>
  miosa process kill <id>
```

Aliases: `proc`.

#### `miosa process get`

Show a process.

```
miosa process get [sandbox] <id>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa process kill`

Stop a process.

```
miosa process kill [sandbox] <id>
```

Aliases: `stop`.

#### `miosa process list`

List processes.

```
miosa process list [sandbox]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa process logs`

Show a process's output.

```
miosa process logs [sandbox] <id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-n, --tail int` - Number of recent lines.

#### `miosa process start`

Start a background process.

```
miosa process start [sandbox] -- <command> [args...] [flags]
```

Start a process and return immediately with its id.
The command goes after --.
A single word is a shell line; several words are quoted as an argv, like 'ssh'.

```
  miosa process start -- python -m http.server 8080
  miosa process start my-box --name worker --cwd /workspace -- ./run.sh
```

Flags:

- `--cwd string` - Working directory.
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--name string` - A name for the process.
- `--sudo` - Run as root.

### `miosa profile`

Manage config profiles (several accounts or environments).

```
miosa profile
```

A profile is a named sign-in and set of defaults in ~/.miosa/config.toml.
The profile in effect is --profile, then MIOSA_PROFILE, then the one set with
'profile use', then "default".

```
  miosa login --profile work
  miosa profile list
  miosa profile use work
  miosa --profile default list
```

Aliases: `profiles`.

#### `miosa profile add`

Create an empty profile (then run 'miosa login --profile <name>').

```
miosa profile add <name> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--api-url string` - API base URL for this profile.
- `--workspace string` - Default workspace slug.

#### `miosa profile list`

List profiles.

```
miosa profile list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa profile rm`

Delete a profile and its stored key.

```
miosa profile rm <name>
```

Aliases: `remove`, `delete`.

#### `miosa profile show`

Show a profile's settings (the key is masked).

```
miosa profile show [name]
```

#### `miosa profile use`

Make a profile the default for new commands.

```
miosa profile use <name>
```

### `miosa project`

Projects group resources inside a workspace.

```
miosa project
```

<project> is a name, slug or id.

Aliases: `projects`.

#### `miosa project changes`

Staged changes of a Deploy target: list, apply or discard them.

```
miosa project changes
```

Changes staged in the console (services created or edited, variables set or
removed) wait in a set until they are applied together.
Apply writes all of them or
none, and by default deploys every service the set touched, once each.

```
  miosa project changes list shop
  miosa project changes apply shop
  miosa project changes apply shop --no-deploy
  miosa project changes rm shop <change-id>
  miosa project changes discard shop --yes
```

##### `miosa project changes apply`

Apply the staged changes (all or none), then deploy what they touched.

```
miosa project changes apply <project> [flags]
```

Apply every staged change at once: a change that fails stops the whole set and
nothing is written.
Then each service the set touched is deployed once, unless
--no-deploy.
A set staged before another apply answers that it is stale; list it,
review, and apply again.

Flags:
