- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--no-deploy` - Apply without deploying the services the changes touched.

##### `miosa project changes discard`

Drop every staged change.

```
miosa project changes discard <project> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

##### `miosa project changes list`

List the staged changes.

```
miosa project changes list <project> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa project changes rm`

Drop one staged change.

```
miosa project changes rm <project> <change-id> [flags]
```

Aliases: `unstage`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa project create`

Create a project.

```
miosa project create <name> [flags]
```

Create a project in a workspace.
The API requires one, so without --workspace
it goes where the console would put it: the profile's default workspace
('miosa workspace use'), else the workspace the organization marks default, else
the one whose slug is "default", else its only workspace.

Flags:

- `--description string` - What it is for.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace: slug, name or id (default: the default workspace).
- `--workspace-id string` - Workspace id (same as --workspace).

Examples:

```
miosa project create shop
  miosa project create shop --workspace staging
```

#### `miosa project get`

Show a project.

```
miosa project get <project>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa project list`

List projects.

```
miosa project list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa project rm`

Delete a project and everything in it.

```
miosa project rm <project> [flags]
```

Delete a project.
A workspace's default project cannot be deleted, and a
project that still owns databases, deployments or sandboxes answers 409: move or
delete those first.
Its services are stopped and deleted with it; while one is
still stopping (just after 'service delete') the delete is accepted and
finishes on its own as soon as it has stopped.

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa project target`

Deploy targets of a project (production, staging, ...).

```
miosa project target
```

Every project has a default Deploy target; the first read creates 'production'.
Services, variables and releases live inside one Deploy target.

```
  miosa project target list shop
  miosa project target create shop staging
  miosa project target duplicate shop production --name staging
  miosa project target rm shop staging --yes
```

Aliases: `targets`.

##### `miosa project target create`

Create a Deploy target.

```
miosa project target create <project> <name> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--kind string` - Environment kind.
- `--slug string` - URL slug (default: from the name).

##### `miosa project target duplicate`

Copy an environment's services and variables.

```
miosa project target duplicate <project> <environment> [flags]
```

Copy a Deploy target's services and variables into a new one.
Secrets are
generated again and no data or managed database is copied.

```
  miosa project target duplicate shop production --name staging
```

Flags:

- `--name string` - Name for the new environment.

##### `miosa project target list`

List a project's Deploy targets.

```
miosa project target list <project>
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa project target rm`

Delete an environment.

```
miosa project target rm <project> <environment> [flags]
```

Delete a non-default Deploy target.
Its services stop.

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `--yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa project vars`

Shared variables of a Deploy target.

```
miosa project vars
```

Shared variables are visible to every service in an environment.
A service reads
one as ${{shared.NAME}}.

```
  miosa project vars list cliniciq
  miosa project vars set cliniciq API_BASE=https://api.acme.dev
  miosa project vars unset cliniciq API_BASE
```

##### `miosa project vars list`

List shared variables (values masked).

```
miosa project vars list <project> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa project vars set`

Set shared variables.

```
miosa project vars set <project> KEY=VALUE [KEY=VALUE...] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa project vars unset`

Delete shared variables.

```
miosa project vars unset <project> <NAME>[,NAME...] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

### `miosa prompt`

Run an agent on a sandbox or computer and follow it.

```
miosa prompt [text...] [flags]
```

Send an instruction to a coding agent running in a sandbox or computer, stream
what it does, and print its answer.
Harnesses: Claude Code, Codex, OSA, OpenCode,
pi, Prime Agent, Kimi Code, Mistral Vibe and Hermes Agent (the list is read from
the platform, so a new one works without upgrading the CLI).

The run uses YOUR model connection: add one with 'miosa connections add models'.
One connected plan or key powers every harness that accepts it: see which with
'miosa connections harnesses', and choose which connection a harness uses with
'miosa connections use'.
MIOSA never falls back to its own keys; without a
connection the run is refused.

The target is --sandbox/--computer, the current sandbox ('miosa use'), or a new
machine MIOSA provisions for the run with --new-machine.
The prompt is the
arguments, "-" for standard input, or --file.

Follow-ups: --chat ID names a chat and continues it (the agent keeps its memory
on the machine); --continue resumes the machine's most recent run; --new-chat
starts a chat and prints its id.

```
  miosa prompt "add a health check to the api" --sandbox my-box
  miosa prompt --harness codex --model gpt-6.1-sol "fix the failing test"
  miosa prompt --harness opencode "add a health check"   # on your ChatGPT plan or any key it accepts
  miosa prompt --new-machine --workspace acme "write a README for this repo"
  miosa prompt --chat 3f0c... "now add tests"
  git diff | miosa prompt --sandbox my-box -     # the diff is the prompt
  miosa prompt --agent reviewer --no-follow "review PR 42"   # prints the run id
```

Flags:

- `--after-run string` - What happens to a new machine when the run ends: destroy, pause or keep.
- `--agent string` - Run a saved agent (name or id) instead of an ad hoc one.
- `--chat string` - Chat id; continues the chat if it has runs.
- `--computer string` - Computer to run in.
- `--continue` - Continue the most recent run on the target.
- `--external-user string` - Run as one of your end users (your own id for them): their connections are used.
- `--file string` - Read the prompt from a file.
- `--harness string` - Harness: claude-code, codex, osa, opencode, pi, prime, kimi, mistral, hermes (default: the agent's, or claude-code).
- `--idempotency-key string` - Replay-safe key: the same key returns the same run.
- `--machine string` - Kind of new machine: sandbox or computer.
- `--max-time int` - Stop the run after this many seconds.
- `--model string` - Model for the harness (default: the harness default; see 'miosa connections harnesses HARNESS').
- `--new` - Same as --new-chat.
- `--new-chat` - Start a chat (a new chat id is generated and printed).
- `--new-machine` - Provision a machine for this run.
- `--no-follow` - Print the run id and return without waiting.
- `--notify-email` - Email you when the run finishes.
- `--notify-webhook string` - Webhook id to notify when the run finishes.
- `--reasoning-effort string` - Reasoning effort for this run, as the model takes it (for example low, medium, high); the API names what a model accepts.
- `--resume string` - Same as --chat: continue this chat.
- `--reuse string` - New machine lifetime: run (one per run) or chat (one per --chat, files persist).
- `-s, --sandbox string` - Sandbox to run in (default: the current sandbox).
- `--size string` - Size of a new machine: micro, tiny, xs, small, quad, medium, large, xl.
- `--source string` - Label recorded on the run (max 40 characters).
- `--template string` - Template of a new machine.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace (slug or id) for a new machine.

### `miosa protection`

Put a password in front of a service.

```
miosa protection <project> <service> [flags]
```

Without flags, shows whether the service is protected.

```
  miosa protection shop API --password 'open-sesame'
  miosa protection shop API --off
  miosa protection shop API --share-link --expires 3600
  miosa protection shop API --revoke <link-id>

```
The password is never printed back.
A share link is a way past the password for
one person: the token is shown once, and expires after --expires seconds (the
API's default when omitted).

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--expires int` - Share link lifetime in seconds (7 days when omitted, at most 90).
- `--off` - Remove protection.
- `--password string` - Password visitors must enter (never printed back).
- `--revoke string` - Revoke this share link id.
- `--share-link` - Mint a share link that skips the password (token shown once).

### `miosa proxy`

Forward local ports to sandbox ports.

```
miosa proxy [name|id] <local>:<remote> [<local>:<remote> ...] [flags]
```

Forward one or more local TCP ports to ports inside a sandbox, over the control
plane's WebSocket tunnel.
Any TCP service works (a database, a dev server, a
debugger), not only HTTP; use 'miosa preview' to publish an HTTP port at a URL
instead.

Each mapping is <local-port>:<remote-port>.
The command blocks until
interrupted with Ctrl-C.
It listens on 127.0.0.1 unless --bind says otherwise.

A key needs the sandboxes:exec scope, because a tunnel reaches every service in
the sandbox.
The sandbox must be running; a paused one is not woken.

--reverse turns it around: a port inside the sandbox reaches a port on your
machine (an ssh -R over the sandbox's SSH tunnel; needs the OpenSSH client).
The mapping is still <local>:<remote>: the sandbox's localhost:<remote>
connects to <local> on --bind (default 127.0.0.1) here.

A connection that cannot reach the sandbox (it is moving between hosts, or the
network blinked) is retried up to 5 times before it is dropped.

```
  miosa proxy my-box 8080:80
  miosa proxy my-box 5432:5432 6379:6379
  miosa proxy 8080:80          # the current sandbox
  miosa forward my-box --reverse 3000:8080   # the sandbox's :8080 reaches your :3000
```

Aliases: `forward`.

Flags:

- `--bind string` - Local address to listen on (with --reverse: to connect to). (default `127.0.0.1`)
- `--reverse` - Forward sandbox ports to ports on this machine instead.

### `miosa ps`

List background processes (same as 'process list'); 'top' lists every process.

```
miosa ps [sandbox]
```

### `miosa push`

Copy a directory into a sandbox, leaving out what .gitignore names.

```
miosa push [sandbox] <dir> [flags]
```

Copy a local directory into a sandbox, by default to /workspace/<dir name>.
In a git repository only the files git tracks or does not ignore are copied
(git ls-files: every .gitignore, .git/info/exclude and the global excludes apply).
Outside one, the directory's .gitignore is read.
Either way .git and what
.miosaignore names are left out; --no-ignore copies everything.
Patterns follow
.gitignore: '#' comments, '!' to put a path back, a trailing '/' for directories,
a leading '/' to anchor at the root, '**/' for any depth.

Without a sandbox the current one is used ('miosa use').

```
  miosa push ./app
  miosa push my-box ./app --to /workspace/app
  miosa push my-box . --no-ignore
```

Flags:

- `--no-ignore` - Copy everything, ignoring .gitignore, .miosaignore and .git.
- `--to string` - Where in the sandbox (default: /workspace/<dir name>).

### `miosa recover`

Recover a sandbox from its disk into a NEW sandbox.

```
miosa recover [sandbox]
```

Recover a failed or errored sandbox: MIOSA provisions a new sandbox from the preserved
disk and returns its id.
The original keeps existing until you destroy it.

### `miosa redeploy`

Build and release the linked service again.

```
miosa redeploy [flags]
```

Queue a release from the linked service's own source and wait for it.

```
  miosa link cliniciq --service API
  miosa redeploy
```

Flags:

- `--no-wait` - Queue the release and return.
- `--timeout duration` - How long to wait for the release. (default `10m0s`)

### `miosa regions`

List regions and whether they have capacity.

```
miosa regions
```

### `miosa rename`

Rename a sandbox.

```
miosa rename [sandbox] <name>
```

Give a sandbox a new name.
Its id does not change.

```
  miosa rename my-box api-box
```

### `miosa restart`

Restart a sandbox in place, keeping its disk.

```
miosa restart [sandbox] [flags]
```

Power-cycle a running sandbox under the same id: it stops keeping its disk, then
boots that disk again.
Files, packages and enabled services survive; running
processes do not.
Use it when a sandbox stops answering. --wait blocks, with
progress, until it accepts commands.

```
  miosa restart my-box --wait
```

Flags:

- `--wait` - Wait until it is ready for commands.

### `miosa restore`

Restore a sandbox from a checkpoint.

```
miosa restore [name|id] <checkpoint-id>
```

Create a new sandbox from a checkpoint snapshot.

The original sandbox is not modified; a new computer is provisioned
branched from the checkpoint state.

Example:
```
  miosa restore my-box cp_abc123
  miosa restore cp_abc123        # uses current sandbox
```

### `miosa resume`

Resume a paused or stopped sandbox.

```
miosa resume [sandbox] [flags]
```

Resume a sandbox from where it paused or stopped. --wait blocks, with progress,
until it accepts commands.

```
  miosa resume my-box --wait
```

Flags:

- `--fail-fast` - Start only if a machine is ready now; otherwise fail at once and start, bill and count nothing.
- `--wait` - Wait until it is ready for commands.

### `miosa rollback`

Serve the linked service's previous release.

```
miosa rollback [flags]
```

Queue a release that serves an earlier build with its frozen source and settings.
The previous release is the newest one that is not the active one; --to names one
(number, unique id prefix or id).

```
  miosa rollback
  miosa rollback --to 42
```

Flags:

- `--no-wait` - Queue the rollback and return.
- `--timeout duration` - How long to wait for the release. (default `10m0s`)
- `--to string` - Release number, id or unique id prefix (default: the previous one).

### `miosa run`

List, follow and stop agent runs.

```
miosa run [flags]
```

A run is one instruction executed by an agent harness on a machine. 'miosa prompt'
starts one; these commands inspect runs started from anywhere (the console, the
API, triggers, workflows).

```
  miosa run list --status running
  miosa run follow <id>
  miosa run stop <id>
  miosa run usage --from 2026-10-01 --group-by runner

```
With a command after -- it is a one-shot instead: a fresh sandbox runs the command,
its output is printed, and --rm removes the sandbox afterwards. miosa exits with
the command's status.

```
  miosa run --rm -- python -c 'print(6 * 7)'
  miosa run --rm --size small --set TOKEN=abc --cwd /home/user -- ./build.sh
```

Aliases: `runs`.

Flags:

- `--cwd string` - Working directory for the command (default: the pushed directory).
- `--fail-fast` - Start only if a machine is ready now; otherwise fail at once and start, bill and count nothing.
- `--keep` - Keep the sandbox after the command (default: delete it).
- `--meta stringArray` - Metadata KEY=VALUE on the sandbox (repeatable).
- `--name string` - Name for the sandbox.
- `--push string` - Copy this local directory in first, as 'miosa push' does, and run there.
- `--sandbox` - In a linked directory, use a sandbox instead of running here.
- `-e, --set stringArray` - Environment variable KEY=VALUE for the command (repeatable).
- `--size string` - Sandbox size, as for 'miosa create'.
- `--template string` - Sandbox template (default miosa-sandbox).
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug (default: the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa run diagnostics`

Show why a run failed.

```
miosa run diagnostics <run>
```

#### `miosa run download`

Download a file a run produced.

```
miosa run download <run> <file-id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-O, --output-file string` - Write to this file (default: standard output).

#### `miosa run events`

List a run's events.

```
miosa run events <run>
```

#### `miosa run files`

List files the run produced.

```
miosa run files <run>
```

#### `miosa run follow`

Stream a run until it finishes.

```
miosa run follow <run>
```

Stream the run's events and print its answer when it ends.
Ctrl-C detaches without stopping the run.

Aliases: `watch`.

#### `miosa run get`

Show one run.

```
miosa run get <run>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa run interrupt`

Stop the agent run attached to a machine.

```
miosa run interrupt [flags]
```

Stops the newest queued or running agent run on a machine, the same as
'miosa run stop <id>' without having to know the id.
With no agent run attached
this fails with NO_AGENT_RUN.
This is what the miosa-stop-agent helper inside a
machine calls.

```
  miosa run interrupt --sandbox my-box
```

Flags:

- `--computer string` - Computer.
- `-s, --sandbox string` - Sandbox (default: the current one).

#### `miosa run list`

List runs, newest first.

```
miosa run list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--agent-id string` - Only runs of this agent (id).
- `--chat string` - Only runs of this chat.
- `--harness string` - Only runs of this harness: claude-code, codex, osa or custom.
- `-n, --limit int` - How many runs. (default `20`)
- `--source string` - Only runs from this source: console, api, workflow, trigger, test or a client label.
- `--status string` - Only runs in this status: queued, running, succeeded, failed or canceled.
- `--target string` - Only runs on this machine (id).
- `--workspace-id string` - Only runs in this workspace.

#### `miosa run logs`

Print the command output of a run (stdout, then stderr).

```
miosa run logs <run>
```

#### `miosa run messages`

Show the agent's messages.

```
miosa run messages <run>
```

#### `miosa run outputs`

Show a run's result, messages and files.

```
miosa run outputs <run>
```

#### `miosa run steer`

Queue a prompt for the agent already running on a machine.

```
miosa run steer <prompt> [flags]
```

Adds a prompt as the next turn of the agent run attached to a machine.
The
agent finishes what it is doing, then a run that continues the same chat starts
with your prompt.
With no agent run attached on that machine this fails with
NO_AGENT_RUN.
The prompt is the arguments, or "-" for standard input; it can be
up to 64 KiB.

This is what the miosa-queue-prompt helper inside a machine calls.

```
  miosa run steer --sandbox my-box "also add a changelog entry"
  git diff | miosa run steer --sandbox my-box -
```

Flags:

- `--computer string` - Computer to steer.
- `-s, --sandbox string` - Sandbox to steer (default: the current one).

#### `miosa run stop`

Stop a running run.

```
miosa run stop <run>
```

Aliases: `cancel`.

#### `miosa run usage`

Token usage and estimated cost of runs.

```
miosa run usage [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--from string` - Start date, 2026-10-01 or RFC 3339.
- `--group-by string` - Group results by runner, model or agent.
- `--to string` - End date.

### `miosa runtimes`

Show the sandbox runtimes the platform offers.

```
miosa runtimes
```

Show the sandbox runtimes the platform can schedule a sandbox on.

A runtime is the engine a sandbox boots on: Firecracker, the default; SOMA,
the faster start for supported Linux images; or Auto, which lets the platform
pick.
Availability is a live gate: a runtime the platform has turned off is
listed with the reason it is unavailable.

Pass a runtime to 'miosa create' with --runtime <id>.

#### `miosa runtimes list`

List the sandbox runtimes and their availability.

```
miosa runtimes list
```

List every sandbox runtime with its live availability and largest shape.

```
  miosa runtimes list
  miosa runtimes list --json | jq -r '.data[] | select(.available) | .id'
```

Aliases: `ls`.

### `miosa sandbox`

Inspect and resize a sandbox's vCPU and memory.

```
miosa sandbox
```

Show a sandbox's vCPU and memory and what it can change on the running
sandbox right now, and change them.

What can change live depends on the runtime and the host; 'miosa sandbox
resources' shows it for one sandbox.
A running Firecracker sandbox can take
more vCPU up to the count it booted with (on hosts with the cpu controller)
and more memory up to its live ceiling.
SOMA sandboxes cannot resize live.
A change that cannot apply live is not saved: resize says so and exits 5;
--restart recreates the sandbox at the new size instead.

```
  miosa sandbox resources my-box
  miosa sandbox resize my-box --cpu 4
  miosa sandbox resize my-box --memory 8192
  miosa sandbox update my-box --always-on
```

#### `miosa sandbox resize`

Change a sandbox's vCPU or memory.

```
miosa sandbox resize [sandbox] --cpu <n> --memory <mb> [--restart] [flags]
```

Change the vCPU count and/or memory of a sandbox.
At least one of --cpu or
--memory is required.

The change is applied live where the runtime supports it (vCPU on a running
Firecracker sandbox, up to its booted count; memory up to the live ceiling).
A change that cannot apply live is NOT saved: the command says so and exits 5.
Pass --restart to recreate the sandbox from its disk at the new size instead:
the new sandbox gets a new id, running processes stop, files are kept, and the
source stays paused until you destroy it.

Flags:

- `--cpu int` - New vCPU count (leave unset to keep it).
- `--memory int` - New memory in MB (leave unset to keep it).
- `--restart` - Recreate the sandbox at the new size when the change cannot apply live (new id).

Examples:

```
miosa sandbox resize my-box --cpu 1
  miosa sandbox resize my-box --memory 2048 --restart
```

#### `miosa sandbox resources`

Show vCPU, memory, and what can change live.

```
miosa sandbox resources [sandbox]
```

Read the sandbox's current vCPU and memory and the capabilities the host
reports for it: whether vCPU and memory can change on the running sandbox
right now, up to what, and why not when they cannot.

Takes a sandbox name, an id, or nothing for the current sandbox.

#### `miosa sandbox update`

Change whether a sandbox is always-on.

```
miosa sandbox update [sandbox] --always-on[=false] [flags]
```

Mark a sandbox always-on, or clear the mark.

An always-on sandbox is never paused for inactivity, and the platform resumes
it by itself after a host restart. --always-on is required.

Flags:

- `--always-on` - Keep the sandbox running (--always-on=false clears it).

### `miosa schedule`

Scheduled jobs.

```
miosa schedule
```

A schedule runs a command at set times. <job> is a name or id.

```
  miosa schedule create --name ingest --cron "*/10 * * * *" --tz America/Chicago \
      --on my-box --command "sh /workspace/ingest.sh" --overlap skip --timeout 600 \
      --env MODE=prod --secret TOKEN=secret://org/INGEST_TOKEN

```
With --on the command runs on that machine, waking it if it is paused, and never
starts a new one.
Without it each run starts a fresh sandbox, runs the command and
destroys the sandbox.

With --agent each tick is a run of that agent, with the workspace's approval
policies and budgets, and a timeout of up to an hour:

```
  miosa schedule create --name drafts --cron "0 9 * * 1-5" --agent drafter \
      --task "Draft follow-up notes for yesterday's calls" --timeout 1800
  miosa schedule run drafts
  miosa agent runs drafter

```
An agent schedule is one of the agent's triggers; change it with 'miosa agent triggers'.

Aliases: `schedules`, `schedule-jobs`.

#### `miosa schedule create`

Create a schedule.

```
miosa schedule create [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--agent string` - Run this agent (name or id) instead of a command.
- `--command string` - Shell command to run.
- `--cron string` - Five-field cron expression, for example "*/10 * * * *".
- `--env stringArray` - Environment variable NAME=value, stored encrypted (repeatable).
- `--name string` - Name, unique in the organization.
- `--on string` - Run on this existing machine (name or id) instead of a fresh sandbox.
- `--overlap string` - When a run is due while the previous one is still going (skip|queue|allow).
- `--secret stringArray` - Environment variable NAME=secret://org/NAME or secret://<workspace>/NAME (repeatable).
- `--task string` - With --agent: the instruction each run receives.
- `--timeout int` - Seconds a run may take, up to 3600 (default 300; at least 60 with --agent).
- `--tz string` - IANA time zone the expression is evaluated in (default UTC).
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (default: $MIOSA_WORKSPACE, then the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa schedule executions`

List past runs.

```
miosa schedule executions <job>
```

Aliases: `runs`.

#### `miosa schedule get`

Show a job.

```
miosa schedule get <job>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa schedule list`

List jobs.

```
miosa schedule list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa schedule output`

Show a run's exit code and output.

```
miosa schedule output <job> <run>
```

Aliases: `logs`.

Examples:

```
miosa schedule runs nightly
miosa schedule output nightly <run-id>
```

#### `miosa schedule pause`

Pause a job.

```
miosa schedule pause <job>
```

#### `miosa schedule resume`

Resume a job.

```
miosa schedule resume <job>
```

#### `miosa schedule rm`

Delete a job.

```
miosa schedule rm <job> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa schedule run`

Run a job now.

```
miosa schedule run <job> [flags]
```

Aliases: `run-now`.

Flags:

- `--agent string` - The agent, for a schedule created with --agent.

### `miosa scp`

Copy files to or from a sandbox (same as 'files cp'; no SSH key needed).

```
miosa scp <src> <dst> [flags]
```

Copy files between your machine and a sandbox, or between sandboxes.
Remote paths are <sandbox>:<path>.
A directory needs -r.

```
  Local to remote:   miosa files cp ./local.txt my-box:/workspace/local.txt
  Remote to local:   miosa files cp my-box:/workspace/out.txt ./out.txt
  Remote to remote:  miosa files cp a:/src.txt b:/dst.txt
  A directory:       miosa files cp -r ./site my-box:/workspace/

```
A destination ending in / (or an existing directory) receives the source's name.
File contents go through the JSON API as base64; the server's upload limit applies.

Flags:

- `-r, --recursive` - Copy directories.

### `miosa secret`

Organization and workspace secrets.

```
miosa secret
```

Secrets belong to the organization or to one workspace and are shared to
workspaces explicitly.
Lists show names, never values.

```
  miosa secret ls
  miosa secret set GHL_TOKEN --value-from-file ./token
  miosa secret share GHL_TOKEN --workspace staging
  miosa secret reveal GHL_TOKEN
```

Aliases: `secrets`, `vault`.

#### `miosa secret get`

Show a secret's metadata and versions (never its value).

```
miosa secret get <id|name>
```

#### `miosa secret import`

Load many secrets from a dotenv file.

```
miosa secret import --file <dotenv> [flags]
```

Load many names at once.
Values are read from the file and never echoed; the
command prints the names it set.

A line the vault cannot take is reported with its line number and the reason
instead of being skipped silently.
When every line is unusable the command
fails, and --strict fails on the first unusable line before anything is set.

Flags:

- `--file string` - Dotenv file to read.
- `--scope string` - Org or workspace. (default `org`)
- `--strict` - Fail on the first line that cannot be imported.
- `--workspace-id string` - Required with --scope workspace.

#### `miosa secret list`

List secrets (names and metadata, never values).

```
miosa secret list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--scope string` - Only this scope: org or workspace.
- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace.

#### `miosa secret reveal`

Print a secret's value (owner or admin only; audited).

```
miosa secret reveal <id|name>
```

Print the current value.
The server allows this only for an owner or admin
acting as themselves, never for a machine, CI, sandbox or organization key, and
writes an audit row every time.

#### `miosa secret rm`

Delete a secret and all of its versions.

```
miosa secret rm <id|name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa secret set`

Create a secret, or rotate an existing one.

```
miosa secret set <name> [flags]
```

Create or rotate.
A rotation adds a version and keeps the name, the scope,
the shares and (unless --description is passed) the description.

The value can come from --value, --value-from-file, or standard input when it is
piped: printf %s "$SECRET" | miosa secret set NAME

Flags:

- `--description string` - What it is for.
- `--scope string` - Org or workspace. (default `org`)
- `--value string` - The value (prefer --value-from-file or stdin).
- `--value-from-file string` - Read the value from a file.
- `--workspace-id string` - Required with --scope workspace.

#### `miosa secret share`

Share a secret with workspaces.

```
miosa secret share <id|name> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--workspace stringSlice` - Workspace slug or id to share with (repeatable).

#### `miosa secret unshare`

Stop sharing a secret with a workspace.

```
miosa secret unshare <id|name> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id to stop sharing with.

#### `miosa secret versions`

List a secret's versions (numbers and dates, never values).

```
miosa secret versions <id|name>
```

### `miosa service`

Create and operate web services, workers, private services and static sites.

```
miosa service
```

Services are the apps a project runs: a web service, a worker, a private service or
a static site, each with releases, variables and an optional public address.

<project> is a name, slug or id; <service> is a name, slug or id.
The API only
resolves services by id, so the CLI looks the name up first.

```
  miosa service create cliniciq API --type web --repo https://github.com/acme/api
  miosa service deploy cliniciq API --wait
  miosa service logs cliniciq API
  miosa service scale cliniciq API --replicas 4
  miosa service vars set cliniciq API DATABASE_URL='${{Postgres.DATABASE_URL}}'
  miosa service domains cliniciq API

```
'miosa services' and 'miosa svc' are aliases of this group.
A named background
process inside a sandbox is 'miosa daemon', a different product.

Aliases: `services`, `svc`.

#### `miosa service cancel`

Cancel a queued or building release.

```
miosa service cancel <project> <service> <release> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service connection`

Show a database service's connection URLs.

```
miosa service connection <project> <service> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service create`

Create a service.

```
miosa service create <project> <name> [flags]
```

Create a web service, worker, private service, static site, Schedule or database
service.
A web or static service gets a public address unless --no-public is given.

```
  miosa service create cliniciq API --type web --repo https://github.com/acme/api
  miosa service create cliniciq Jobs --type worker --repo https://github.com/acme/api --start-command "node worker.js"
  miosa service create cliniciq Cache --type private --image redis:7
  miosa service create cliniciq Nightly --type schedule --repo https://github.com/acme/api --schedule "0 3 * * *"
  miosa service create cliniciq API --repo https://github.com/acme/api --size dedicated-4
```

Flags:

- `--branch string` - Git branch (default: the repository default).
- `--build-command string` - Static services: build command (Railpack builds only).
- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--dockerfile string` - Dockerfile path (needs Services build cells).
- `--env stringArray` - Variable KEY=VALUE (repeatable); a value may be a ${{...}} reference.
- `--image string` - Container image reference.
- `--no-public` - Do not give a web or static service a public address.
- `--output-dir string` - Static services: directory to serve (default: detected).
- `--port int` - Port the container listens on (--image only).
- `--region string` - Region: us-west, us-east or us-mia.
- `--replicas int` - Replicas to run (1-50).
- `--repo string` - Git repository URL.
- `--root-dir string` - Directory inside the repository to build.
- `--schedule string` - Five field schedule expression (with --type schedule).
- `--size string` - Dedicated vCPU size: dedicated-2, dedicated-4, dedicated-8, dedicated-16.
- `--slug string` - URL label (default: from the name).
- `--start-command string` - Command the service starts with.
- `--type string` - Service type: web, worker, private, static, schedule, database. (default `web`)

#### `miosa service delete`

Delete a service.

```
miosa service delete <project> <service> [flags]
```

Delete a service and stop its replicas.
The service disappears from reads at once.

Aliases: `rm`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa service deploy`

Build and release a service.

```
miosa service deploy <project> <service> [flags]
```

Queue a release.
With no source flag the service's own source is rebuilt.
With
--dir a local directory is uploaded as the source.

```
  miosa service deploy cliniciq API
  miosa service deploy cliniciq API --dir . --message "fix the header" --wait
  miosa service deploy cliniciq API --repo https://github.com/acme/api --branch main
```

Flags:

- `--branch string` - Git branch.
- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--dir string` - Upload this local directory as the source.
- `--image string` - Deploy this container image.
- `--message string` - Release message.
- `--port int` - Port the container listens on (--image only).
- `--repo string` - Build this git repository URL.
- `--root-dir string` - Directory inside the repository to build.
- `--timeout duration` - How long --wait may take. (default `10m0s`)
- `--wait` - Wait until the release is live or fails.

#### `miosa service domains`

Show a service's addresses.

```
miosa service domains <project> <service> [flags]
```

Show the private address every service has and the public address it may have.

```
  miosa service domains cliniciq API
  miosa service domains enable cliniciq API --label api
  miosa service domains disable cliniciq API
```

Aliases: `domain`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa service domains disable`

Remove a service's public address.

```
miosa service domains disable <project> <service> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa service domains enable`

Give a service a public address.

```
miosa service domains enable <project> <service> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--label string` - Host label (default: the service slug).

#### `miosa service get`

Show one service.

```
miosa service get <project> <service> [flags]
```

Aliases: `show`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service list`

List a project's services.

```
miosa service list <project> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service logs`

Show a service's output, a release's events, or a build's log.

```
miosa service logs <project> <service> [flags]
```

Show what the service's process writes (stdout and stderr), the tail of each
replica's log.
With several replicas each line names its replica; --replica reads
one. --follow keeps printing new lines until Ctrl-C.

With --events this shows what happened to a release instead: every state
transition, and the reason a release failed.
The newest release is used unless
--release names one (a release number or id).

With --build this shows the build log: the build the release used, or the build
named by --build-id. --follow keeps reading until the build finishes.
A service
built from an image has no build log.

--tail is the number of lines from the end of the log (runtime 1-1000, build
1-2000).

Flags:

- `--build` - Show the build log instead of the service's output.
- `--build-id string` - Build id to read with --build (default: latest).
- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--events` - Show a release's events instead of the service's output.
- `-f, --follow` - Keep printing new lines (with --build: until the build finishes).
- `--release string` - With --events: release number or id (default: the newest).
- `--replica string` - Read one replica's log (its id).
- `-n, --tail int` - Lines from the end of the log (runtime 1-1000, build 1-2000). (default `200`)

Examples:

```
miosa service logs shop api
  miosa service logs shop api --follow
  miosa service logs shop api --events
  miosa service logs shop api --build --follow
```

#### `miosa service metrics`

Requests, errors and latency for a service.

```
miosa service metrics <project> <service> [flags]
```

The service's own metric series: requests, errors, error rate and response
latency (p50 and p95).
CPU, memory and network are not collected for services,
so those fields are null and the footer says which columns are real.

```
  miosa service metrics cliniciq API
  miosa service metrics cliniciq API --range 24h --json
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--range string` - Window: 15m, 1h, 6h, 24h or 7d. (default `1h`)

#### `miosa service promote`

Serve this release again (the API's rollback).

```
miosa service promote <project> <service> <release> [flags]
```

Queue a release that serves an earlier release's built image with its frozen
source and settings. 'promote' is the same operation; the Services API calls it
rollback.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa service release`

Show one release.

```
miosa service release <project> <service> <release> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service releases`

List a service's releases.

```
miosa service releases <project> <service> [flags]
```

Aliases: `history`.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service restart`

Restart a service.

```
miosa service restart <project> <service> [flags]
```

Queue a release that restarts the service.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service rollback`

Serve this release's built image again.

```
miosa service rollback <project> <service> <release> [flags]
```

Queue a release that serves an earlier release's built image with its frozen
source and settings. 'promote' is the same operation; the Services API calls it
rollback.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa service runs`

Show a Schedule's recent runs.

```
miosa service runs <project> <service> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--output` - Also print each run's output.

#### `miosa service scale`

Change replicas or region.

```
miosa service scale <project> <service> [flags]
```

Change the replica count or the region and queue a release that applies it.

```
  miosa service scale cliniciq API --replicas 4
  miosa service scale cliniciq API --region us-east
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--region string` - Region: us-west, us-east or us-mia.
- `--replicas int` - Replicas to run (1-50).

#### `miosa service select`

Point this directory's link at another service.

```
miosa service select <service>
```

Update .miosa/link.json so later commands use this service.

```
  miosa link cliniciq
  miosa service select API
  miosa redeploy
```

Aliases: `use`.

#### `miosa service settings`

Show what each service setting does today.

```
miosa service settings <project>
```

Every setting is 'enforced' (it takes effect), 'stored' (saved but not applied
yet) or 'unsupported'.
Read this before relying on a setting.

#### `miosa service start`

Start a stopped service.

```
miosa service start <project> <service> [flags]
```

Start a stopped service and queue a release.
A service that is already running is a conflict.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

#### `miosa service stop`

Stop a service now.

```
miosa service stop <project> <service> [flags]
```

Stop every active release now.
Fails if a replica cannot be stopped, so the service is never reported stopped while it still runs.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa service templates`

List project templates (add Postgres, Redis, a web app).

```
miosa service templates
```

##### `miosa service templates apply`

Apply a template to a project.

```
miosa service templates apply <project> <slug> [flags]
```

Create the template's services and variables in a Deploy target.

```
  miosa service templates apply cliniciq nextjs-postgres
  miosa service templates apply cliniciq redis
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--no-deploy` - Create the services without deploying them.

#### `miosa service update`

Change a service's name or settings.

```
miosa service update <project> <service> [flags]
```

Change a service's name or settings.
Settings are merged: only the flags you
pass change.

```
  miosa service update cliniciq API --replicas 3
  miosa service update cliniciq API --name backend --start-command "node dist/server.js"
  miosa service update cliniciq API --strategy rolling --rollout-step 10 --rollout-step 50 --rollout-step 100
  miosa service update cliniciq API --spread single_host
  miosa service update cliniciq API --size dedicated-4
  miosa service update cliniciq site --build-command "npm run build" --output-dir dist

```
--spread is auto (replicas land on different hosts where the network allows) or
single_host. --strategy is replace or rolling; rolling starts the new version
beside the old one, shifts traffic in steps and rolls back automatically if the
new replica is unhealthy. --rollout-step must ascend and end at 100.

Flags:

- `--build-command string` - Static services: build command (Railpack builds only).
- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--name string` - New name.
- `--output-dir string` - Static services: directory to serve (default: detected).
- `--region string` - Region: us-west, us-east or us-mia.
- `--replicas int` - Replicas to run (1-50).
- `--rollout-step intSlice` - Traffic step in percent (repeatable, ascending, ends at 100).
- `--rollout-step-s int` - Seconds to wait between steps (10-3600).
- `--size string` - Dedicated vCPU size: dedicated-2, dedicated-4, dedicated-8, dedicated-16.
- `--spread string` - Replica placement: auto or single_host.
- `--start-command string` - Command the service starts with.
- `--strategy string` - Release strategy: replace or rolling.
- `--terminate-grace int` - Seconds the old version drains during a rolling deploy (>=10).

#### `miosa service vars`

Variables of a service.

```
miosa service vars
```

Variables are sealed at rest and never printed except by 'reveal'.
A value may be a
reference: ${{Postgres.DATABASE_URL}} (another service), ${{shared.API_BASE}} (an
environment shared variable), ${{NAME}} (another variable of this service) or
${{secret(32)}} (a generated secret).

```
  miosa service vars list cliniciq API
  miosa service vars set cliniciq API DATABASE_URL='${{Postgres.DATABASE_URL}}' NODE_ENV=production
  miosa service vars effective cliniciq API
  miosa service vars reveal cliniciq API DATABASE_URL
```

##### `miosa service vars effective`

Show the variables a deploy would receive, references resolved.

```
miosa service vars effective <project> <service> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa service vars list`

List a service's variables (values masked).

```
miosa service vars list <project> <service> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa service vars reveal`

Print one variable's value.

```
miosa service vars reveal <project> <service> <NAME> [flags]
```

Print one variable's plaintext.
Needs the env:reveal scope and a person acting
as themselves; a sealed variable is never revealed.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa service vars set`

Set variables.

```
miosa service vars set <project> <service> KEY=VALUE [KEY=VALUE...] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

##### `miosa service vars unset`

Delete variables.

```
miosa service vars unset <project> <service> <NAME>[,NAME...] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).

### `miosa service-account`

Non-human accounts that own API keys for pipelines and programs.

```
miosa service-account
```

A service account is not a person: it owns keys you scope narrowly, so a
pipeline or a program does not run with someone's own access.
Deleting the
account revokes every key it owns.
Only organization owners and admins manage
service accounts. <account> is an id.

```
  miosa service-account new deploy-pipeline --description "release pipeline"
  miosa service-account key new <account> --preset ci --expires-in 90 --quiet-key
  miosa service-account keys <account>
  miosa service-account rm <account>

```
Keys minted here are rotated, restricted and revoked with 'miosa api-key'.

Aliases: `service-accounts`, `sa`.

#### `miosa service-account key`

Mint a key for a service account.

```
miosa service-account key
```

##### `miosa service-account key new`

Create a key owned by a service account.

```
miosa service-account key new <account> [name] [flags]
```

Create an API key.
With no --scope or --preset the key gets the default set of
scopes.
Presets: read-only (every :read scope), ci (sandboxes, deployments,
previews), agent (run agents on sandboxes), cli (what the miosa CLI uses day to
day, including connections), automation (server-side scripts: sandboxes with
files, exec, logs and services, deployments, agents), full-access (every scope;
organization owners and admins only). 'miosa api-key presets' lists them.
Scope names look like sandboxes:exec
or agents:run; see 'miosa api-key list --json' for what existing keys hold.

The secret is printed once. --quiet-key prints only the secret.

Flags:

- `--allowed-ip stringArray` - Only accept requests from this IP or CIDR (repeatable).
- `--expires-in int` - Expire after this many days (default: never).
- `--preset string` - Permission preset: read-only, ci, agent, cli, automation or full-access.
- `--quiet-key` - Print only the secret.
- `--rate-limit int` - Requests per minute for this key.
- `--scope stringArray` - A scope to grant, for example sandboxes:exec (repeatable).
- `--ttl duration` - Expire after this long, in whole days: 30d, 720h (same as --expires-in).
- `--workspace-id string` - Bind the key to one workspace.

#### `miosa service-account keys`

List the keys a service account owns.

```
miosa service-account keys <account> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--all` - Include revoked keys.

#### `miosa service-account list`

List service accounts.

```
miosa service-account list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa service-account new`

Create a service account.

```
miosa service-account new <name> [flags]
```

Aliases: `create`.

Flags:

- `--description string` - What the account is for.

#### `miosa service-account rm`

Delete a service account and revoke its keys.

```
miosa service-account rm <account> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa service-account update`

Rename a service account or change its description.

```
miosa service-account update <account> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--description string` - New description.
- `--name string` - New name.

### `miosa share`

Let every member of the organization use a sandbox.

```
miosa share [sandbox]
```

Make a private sandbox shared: every member of the organization may run commands in it,
open its terminal, copy files and SSH in again.

Sharing a sandbox that was private withholds its creator's personal logins (their GitHub
token and a MIOSA key in their name) from its next start, because other people can now
use it.
A running sandbox keeps what it has until it is stopped and resumed.
Only its
creator or an organization owner or admin can share it.

```
  miosa share my-box
```

### `miosa snapshot`

Browse, name, fork and delete snapshots of sandboxes and computers.

```
miosa snapshot
```

Snapshots are point-in-time copies of a machine.
Every snapshot of a machine
is its history.
A named snapshot is a pinned copy you keep on purpose:
it stays after the machine is gone and is never part of history.

```
  miosa snapshot list my-box
  miosa snapshot save my-box web-stack
  miosa snapshot named
  miosa snapshot tree <snapshot-id> /home/user
  miosa snapshot pull <snapshot-id> /home/user/app -o ./recovered
  miosa snapshot fork --name web-stack
  miosa snapshot delete <snapshot-id> --yes

```
Nothing is deleted unless you ask for it by id, and a snapshot that something
still depends on is never deleted.

Aliases: `snapshots`.

#### `miosa snapshot delete`

Delete snapshots you name, or a machine's whole history.

```
miosa snapshot delete <snapshot-id>... [flags]
```

Permanently deletes snapshots.
Only what you name is touched.

```
  miosa snapshot delete <id> <id> --yes
  miosa snapshot delete --all my-box --yes

```
A snapshot that a name, a fork in progress, a backup, a paused computer
or another snapshot depends on is skipped and reported, never deleted.
Named
snapshots are not part of history; remove those with 'miosa snapshot rm'.

Flags:

- `--all string` - Delete every snapshot in this machine's history.
- `--computer` - Treat the --all machine as a computer, not a sandbox.
- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa snapshot fork`

Create a new machine from a snapshot.

```
miosa snapshot fork [snapshot-id] [flags]
```

Creates a new, independent sandbox or computer (the same kind as the source)
from a snapshot.
The source machine and the snapshot are untouched.

With --name the copy comes from a named snapshot, which works even after the
machine it came from is gone.
The source's environment variables are not
carried over.

```
  miosa snapshot fork <snapshot-id>
  miosa snapshot fork --name web-stack
```

Flags:

- `--name string` - Fork the named snapshot with this name.

#### `miosa snapshot info`

Show a snapshot and what depends on it.

```
miosa snapshot info <snapshot-id>
```

#### `miosa snapshot latest`

Show the newest snapshot of a machine.

```
miosa snapshot latest <machine> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--computer` - Treat <machine> as a computer, not a sandbox.

#### `miosa snapshot list`

List snapshot history across your machines, or for one machine.

```
miosa snapshot list [machine] [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--computer` - Treat <machine> as a computer, not a sandbox.
- `--limit int` - Maximum rows (default 50, maximum 200).
- `--search string` - Match machine name, machine id or snapshot id.
- `--workspace string` - Only machines in this workspace id.

#### `miosa snapshot named`

List your named snapshots and the free allowance.

```
miosa snapshot named [flags]
```

Aliases: `names`.

Flags:

- `--workspace string` - Only names whose source lived in this workspace id.

#### `miosa snapshot pull`

Download a file or a folder from a snapshot.

```
miosa snapshot pull <snapshot-id> <path> [flags]
```

Downloads one file, or a folder (extracted from a tar) into the output
directory.
Nothing is overwritten: existing files stop the download.

```
  miosa snapshot pull <id> /home/user/.env -o ./recovered
  miosa snapshot pull <id> /home/user/app  -o ./recovered
```

Flags:

- `-d, --output-dir string` - Directory to write into. (default `.`)
- `--to string` - Alias of --output-dir. (default `.`)

#### `miosa snapshot rm`

Remove a named snapshot.

```
miosa snapshot rm <name> [flags]
```

Removes the name.
If the name pinned an existing snapshot, that snapshot goes
back to the machine's history untouched.
If the name owned a snapshot taken for
it, that snapshot is deleted unless something still depends on it.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa snapshot save`

Save a machine, or an existing snapshot, under a name.

```
miosa snapshot save <machine> <name> [flags]
```

Pins a copy under a name that outlives the machine.

```
  miosa snapshot save my-box web-stack          a running machine is captured now;
                                                a stopped one pins its newest snapshot
  miosa snapshot save --snapshot <id> web-stack pin one existing snapshot

```
You keep 10 named snapshots free per organization.
Beyond that each name costs
credit; the save is refused while there is none.

Flags:

- `--computer` - Treat <machine> as a computer, not a sandbox.
- `--snapshot string` - Pin this existing snapshot id instead of a machine.
- `--wait` - Wait until a new capture is ready. (default `true`)

#### `miosa snapshot share`

Make a read-only link to a snapshot's files (the link is shown once).

```
miosa snapshot share <snapshot-id> [flags]
```

Create a link anyone holding it can use to browse and download the snapshot's files,
read-only, until it expires. --path limits it to one directory.

```
  miosa snapshot share <snapshot-id> --hours 24 --path /workspace/app --label review
```

Flags:

- `--hours int` - Hours until the link expires.
- `--label string` - A note for 'snapshot shares'.
- `--path string` - Share only this directory (default /).

#### `miosa snapshot shares`

List a snapshot's share links (tokens are never shown again).

```
miosa snapshot shares <snapshot-id>
```

#### `miosa snapshot tree`

List the files in a snapshot (works while the machine is stopped).

```
miosa snapshot tree <snapshot-id> [path]
```

#### `miosa snapshot unshare`

Revoke a share link.

```
miosa snapshot unshare <snapshot-id> <share-id>
```

### `miosa ssh`

Open an SSH session in a sandbox, or run a command over it.

```
miosa ssh [name|id] [-- <cmd> [args...]] [flags]
```

Connect to a sandbox with the system ssh client. miosa makes a one-time key,
asks the API for a short-lived certificate for it (15 minutes) that only this
sandbox accepts, and runs ssh through MIOSA's authenticated tunnel.
Where the
server has no certificate authority it authorizes the one-time key in the
sandbox instead.
The key, certificate and host file live in a private temporary
directory that is removed when ssh ends.
Nothing needs setting up in ~/.ssh.

With no command and a terminal you get a login shell.
Standard input is piped
through, so a script that is not on the sandbox yet runs without copying it:

```
  miosa ssh my-box -- bash -s < ./setup.sh
  cat ./setup.sh | miosa ssh my-box -- bash -s
  miosa ssh my-box < ./setup.sh          # no command: runs 'bash -s'
  miosa ssh my-box -- bash -lc "cd my-repo && npm test"

```
With several arguments after -- each one is quoted, so 'bash -lc "a && b"'
reaches the sandbox as written. miosa exits with the remote command's exit code.

When ssh or ssh-keygen is not installed, or with --api, the command runs over
the MIOSA API instead of an SSH connection: standard input is then read to the
end and sent with the command, so it is limited to 80KB, and the global
--timeout bounds the command (1-300 seconds, default 30).
Without a command and
with a terminal on stdin that mode opens 'miosa console'.

Flags:

- `--api` - Run over the MIOSA API instead of an SSH connection.
- `--cwd string` - Working directory, relative to the sandbox home directory.

### `miosa steer`

Send the agent running in a sandbox a new message without stopping it.

```
miosa steer [sandbox] <message>
```

Adds a message as the next turn of the agent running in a sandbox (default: the
current one): it finishes what it is doing, then continues the same chat with your
message.
With no agent running this fails with NO_AGENT_RUN. "-" reads the message
from standard input (up to 64 KiB).

```
  miosa steer my-box "also add a changelog entry"
  miosa steer "use the existing helper instead"
  git diff | miosa steer my-box -
```

### `miosa stop`

Stop a sandbox, keeping its disk.

```
miosa stop [sandbox]
```

Stop a sandbox.
The disk persists and the sandbox can be resumed; use 'destroy' to delete it.

### `miosa storage`

Object storage buckets.

```
miosa storage
```

Buckets hold objects behind an S3-compatible endpoint. <bucket> is the bucket name or id.

Aliases: `bucket`, `buckets`.

#### `miosa storage create`

Create a bucket.

```
miosa storage create <name> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--class string` - Data classification: public, internal, confidential or restricted (public|internal|confidential|restricted).
- `--region string` - Region.
- `--visibility string` - Who can read objects (private|public). (default `private`)
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (default: $MIOSA_WORKSPACE, then the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa storage get`

Show a bucket, or download one of its objects.

```
miosa storage get <bucket> [key] [file]
```

Download an object to a file (default: the key's base name).
Use - to write to
standard output.

```
  miosa storage get builds App.dmg
  miosa storage get assets brand/logo.png -

```
With only a bucket, show the bucket.

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa storage import-notion`

Turn a Notion export zip in a bucket into clean Markdown.

```
miosa storage import-notion <bucket> [flags]
```

Reads a Notion export zip (Settings, Export, Markdown and CSV) from a bucket
and writes a clean Markdown tree back to the same bucket: Notion's 32 character
ids are dropped from every name, database CSVs become tables that link to their
row pages, and links between pages point at the new paths. manifest.json lists
every file with its source path and Notion id.

Upload the zip first (miosa storage put).
Nothing is written when the zip is
refused.
Running it again rewrites the same files.

Flags:

- `--prefix string` - Where the tree goes (default notion/).
- `--source string` - Key of the export zip in the bucket.

Examples:

```
miosa storage import-notion docs --source exports/notion.zip
  miosa storage import-notion docs --source exports/notion.zip --prefix wiki/
```

#### `miosa storage keys`

S3 keys for a bucket.

```
miosa storage keys
```

Per-bucket keys for S3 clients (aws s3, SDKs).
The secret is shown once, at creation.

Aliases: `key`.

##### `miosa storage keys create`

Create an S3 key (the secret is shown once).

```
miosa storage keys create <bucket> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--expires-at string` - Expiry as an RFC 3339 time.
- `--name string` - Label for the key. (default `cli`)
- `--prefix string` - Only keys under this prefix.
- `--read-write` - Allow writes and deletes (default: read only).

##### `miosa storage keys list`

List a bucket's keys (no secrets).

```
miosa storage keys list <bucket>
```

Aliases: `ls`.

##### `miosa storage keys revoke`

Revoke a key.

```
miosa storage keys revoke <bucket> <id> [flags]
```

Aliases: `rm`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa storage list`

List buckets.

```
miosa storage list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa storage objects`

List objects.

```
miosa storage objects <bucket> [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls-objects`.

Flags:

- `-n, --limit int` - How many.
- `--prefix string` - Only keys with this prefix.

#### `miosa storage presign`

Make a temporary URL for one object.

```
miosa storage presign <bucket> <key> [flags]
```

Print a signed URL that reads (GET) or writes (PUT) one object without an API
key, for up to 7 days.
A private bucket refuses presigned URLs.

```
  miosa storage presign builds App.dmg --ttl 10m
  miosa storage presign builds incoming.bin --method PUT
```

Flags:

- `--method string` - GET to read, PUT to upload. (default `GET`)
- `--ttl string` - How long the URL works: seconds or a duration (10m, 2h). (default `1h`)

#### `miosa storage put`

Upload a file, or standard input, to a bucket.

```
miosa storage put <bucket> <file|-> [key] [flags]
```

Upload a local file, or standard input with -, as an object.
The key defaults to
the file name; with - it is required.
Files over 256 MiB go up in 64 MiB parts
automatically (the object appears only when every part is in).

An upload has no overall deadline, so a large file is not cut off while it is
still moving; --timeout is the idle limit instead, and 0 disables even that.

```
  miosa storage put assets ./logo.png brand/logo.png
  miosa storage put builds ./App.dmg
  cat backup.sql | miosa storage put backups - db/backup.sql
```

Flags:

- `--content-type string` - Content type (default: from the file extension).

#### `miosa storage rm`

Delete a bucket.

```
miosa storage rm <bucket> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa storage rm-object`

Delete one object.

```
miosa storage rm-object <bucket> <key> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Accepted for scripts; deleting one object does not ask.

### `miosa tag`

Show, add or remove sandbox tags.

```
miosa tag [sandbox] [flags]
```

Tags are key/value labels you can filter on ('miosa list --tag k=v').
Without flags the current tags are printed.
Changes are merged into the existing tags.

```
  miosa tag my-box --set env=staging --set owner=ana
  miosa tag my-box --remove owner
  miosa tag my-box --clear
```

Flags:

- `--clear` - Remove every tag first.
- `--remove stringArray` - Remove a tag by key (repeatable).
- `--set stringArray` - Add or change a tag, KEY=VALUE (repeatable).

### `miosa telemetry`

Export sandbox and account metrics and lifecycle events over OTLP.

```
miosa telemetry
```

Send the organization's telemetry to an OpenTelemetry endpoint (OTLP/HTTP):
every minute the sandbox and account metrics (sandboxes by state, each running
sandbox, the credit balance), and every lifecycle event (create, pause, resume,
destroy, exec) since the last export, as log records.

With no subcommand it shows the export and how its last run went.

```
  miosa telemetry
  miosa telemetry set https://otlp.datadoghq.com -H dd-api-key=$DD_API_KEY
  miosa telemetry set https://api.honeycomb.io -H x-honeycomb-team=$HONEYCOMB_KEY
  miosa telemetry unset

```
The URL is the OTLP base: /v1/metrics and /v1/logs are added to it.
Header
values are stored encrypted and never shown again, only their names.
Setting
an export replaces the current one and sends a test export at once.
Changing
it needs an owner or administrator.

#### `miosa telemetry set`

Replace the export and send a test export now.

```
miosa telemetry set <url> [flags]
```

Replace the export with <url> (https, the OTLP base) and the given request
headers, then send a test export at once.
The export is saved either way; when
the endpoint refuses the test, the command says what it answered and exits 1.

```
  miosa telemetry set https://otlp.datadoghq.com -H dd-api-key=$DD_API_KEY
  miosa telemetry set https://otel.example.com:4318 -H authorization="Bearer $TOKEN"
```

Flags:

- `-H, --header stringArray` - Request header sent with every export, name=value (repeatable).

#### `miosa telemetry unset`

Remove the export.

```
miosa telemetry unset
```

Aliases: `rm`.

### `miosa template`

Sandbox templates.

```
miosa template
```

Templates are the images sandboxes start from. 'list' shows the catalog you can
create from; 'custom' lists the templates your organization built.

```
  miosa template list
  miosa template custom
  miosa template builds my-template
```

Aliases: `templates`.

#### `miosa template build`

Start a build.

```
miosa template build <template>
```

#### `miosa template build-logs`

Show a build's log.

```
miosa template build-logs <build>
```

#### `miosa template builds`

List a template's builds.

```
miosa template builds <template>
```

#### `miosa template custom`

List the templates your organization built.

```
miosa template custom
```

#### `miosa template get`

Show a template.

```
miosa template get <template>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa template list`

List templates you can create sandboxes from.

```
miosa template list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa template promote`

Make the latest build the live one.

```
miosa template promote <template>
```

#### `miosa template rm`

Delete a custom template.

```
miosa template rm <template> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa template rollback`

Return to the previous version.

```
miosa template rollback <template> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa template warm-pool`

Show how many warm sandboxes are kept ready.

```
miosa template warm-pool <template>
```

### `miosa token`

Mint short-lived tokens for your end users.

```
miosa token
```

Tokens here are for products that act for their own customers.
Your backend
authenticates with its msk_* key and mints a token bound to one end user and one
workspace; that token carries a non-empty subset of your key's own scopes and
expires within a day at most (default one hour).
Hand it to the end user's
browser; never log it.

A token minted here cannot mint or manage other tokens.

```
  miosa token create cus_123 ws_456 --scope sandboxes:read --expires-in 3600
  miosa token list
  miosa token revoke <id>
```

Aliases: `tokens`, `delegation`.

#### `miosa token create`

Mint a short-lived token for one end user and workspace.

```
miosa token create <user> <workspace> [flags]
```

Mint a delegation token. <user> is your own id for the end user; <workspace> is the
workspace the token is bound to and must belong to your organization. --scope is
required and may not exceed your own key's scopes.

The token is printed once. --quiet-token prints only the token.

Flags:

- `--expires-in int` - Lifetime in seconds, 1 to 86400 (default 3600).
- `--quiet-token` - Print only the token.
- `--scope stringArray` - A scope to grant, for example sandboxes:read (repeatable).

#### `miosa token list`

List the tokens still valid (newest first).

```
miosa token list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--before string` - Page backwards from this cursor.

#### `miosa token revoke`

Revoke a token (it stops working at once).

```
miosa token revoke <id> [flags]
```

Aliases: `rm`, `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

### `miosa top`

List every process in a sandbox, busiest first, even while it is stopped.

```
miosa top [sandbox]
```

Lists the processes running in a sandbox, the busiest first (at most 200): pid,
CPU and memory use, how long each has run and its command.
A stopped or paused
sandbox is not woken: it shows the list read last, taken at its stop or at the
last 'top', and says when.

```
  miosa top my-box
  miosa top my-box --json
```

### `miosa unlink`

Detach this directory from its project.

```
miosa unlink
```

### `miosa unshare`

Make a sandbox private to you and the organization's admins.

```
miosa unshare [sandbox]
```

Make a sandbox private.
Only its creator and organization owners and admins may use it;
other members can still see it in lists and stop or pause it.
SSH keys other members
added to it are revoked.

Only its creator or an organization owner or admin can unshare it.

```
  miosa unshare my-box
```

### `miosa up`

Deploy this directory to the linked service.

```
miosa up [dir] [flags]
```

Upload the directory (the current one by default) as the linked service's source,
queue a release, and wait for it.
Then print the build's log.

```
  miosa link cliniciq --service API
  miosa up
  miosa up ./dist --message "hotfix" --no-wait

```
Needs a linked directory: `miosa link <project> --service <name>`.

Flags:

- `--logs` - Print the build's log after the release. (default `true`)
- `--message string` - Release message.
- `--no-wait` - Queue the release and return.
- `--timeout duration` - How long to wait for the release. (default `10m0s`)

### `miosa update`

Update the miosa CLI to the latest release.

```
miosa update [flags]
```

Download the latest release for this platform, verify it against its SHA-256
checksum, and replace the running binary.

If miosa was installed with Homebrew or npm, run the package manager instead;
update prints the command.

```
  miosa update            # install the latest release
  miosa update --check    # only report whether a newer release exists
  miosa update --version 1.2.4

```
First install or a CI image: use the install script at https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Miosa-osa/miosa-cli-go/main/install.sh.

Aliases: `upgrade`, `self-update`.

Flags:

- `--check` - Only check; do not install.
- `--force` - Reinstall even when already up to date.
- `--version string` - Install this version instead of the latest.

### `miosa url`

Print the public URL for a sandbox.

```
miosa url [name|id]
```

Print the HTTPS URL for a sandbox's public endpoint.

Example:
```
  miosa url my-box
  miosa url            # uses current sandbox
```

### `miosa usage`

Show runtime, time left and estimated cost.

```
miosa usage [sandbox] [flags]
```

Show a sandbox's runtime, time left and estimated cost. --since (and --until,
default now) adds what it used inside that window: run time and the credits
billed for it.
Each takes a time (2026-10-20T10:00:00Z) or a duration back
from now (24h, 7d).

```
  miosa usage my-box
  miosa usage my-box --since 7d
  miosa usage my-box --since 2026-10-20T00:00:00Z --until 2026-10-21T00:00:00Z
```

Flags:

- `--since string` - Start of the window: a time or a duration back (24h, 7d).
- `--until string` - End of the window (default now): a time or a duration back.

### `miosa use`

Set (or show) the current sandbox.

```
miosa use [name|id] [flags]
```

Set a sandbox as the current default for commands that accept an optional name/id.
The current sandbox is stored in the active profile of ~/.miosa/config.toml and is
what "current" means anywhere a sandbox is expected.
The sandbox must exist.

```
  miosa use my-box
  miosa use              # print the current sandbox
  miosa use --clear
```

Flags:

- `--clear` - Forget the current sandbox.

### `miosa variables`

Read and write variables of the linked project, target or service.

```
miosa variables
```

Read and write variables without repeating the project and service.

```
  miosa variables list                     # the linked service
  miosa variables list --shared            # the Deploy target's shared variables
  miosa variables set API_BASE=https://example.test
  miosa variables set CALLBACK='${{API_BASE}}/cb'
  miosa variables unset OLD_KEY
  miosa variables import .env
  miosa variables export > .env

```
A value may be a reference: ${{Service.NAME}}, ${{shared.NAME}}, ${{NAME}} (this
service) or ${{secret(32)}}.
References are checked before the write.

--skip-deploys is accepted for Railway compatibility: a variable write never
redeploys here; the service reports a pending rebuild or redeploy instead.

Aliases: `vars`.

#### `miosa variables export`

Print the target's variables as .env lines.

```
miosa variables export [flags]
```

Print KEY="VALUE" lines, or `export KEY="VALUE"` with --shell so the output
can be sourced.
Values are printed to stdout only; nothing is written to disk.

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Alias of --environment.
- `--environment string` - Deploy target (default: the linked one).
- `--service string` - Service (default: the linked one).
- `--shared` - The Deploy target's shared variables.
- `--shell` - Prefix each line with export.

#### `miosa variables import`

Set variables from a .env file (or stdin).

```
miosa variables import <file|-> [flags]
```

Read KEY=VALUE lines and set them on the target.
Blank lines, comments and a
leading "export " are ignored; quotes are stripped. "-" reads stdin, so a pasted
block works:

```
  miosa variables import -
  miosa variables import .env
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Alias of --environment.
- `--environment string` - Deploy target (default: the linked one).
- `--service string` - Service (default: the linked one).
- `--shared` - Write the Deploy target's shared variables.

#### `miosa variables list`

List variables of the linked service or the shared set.

```
miosa variables list [project] [service] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Alias of --environment.
- `--environment string` - Deploy target (default: the linked one).
- `--kv` - Print NAME=VALUE lines.
- `--service string` - Service (default: the linked one).
- `--shared` - The Deploy target's shared variables.

#### `miosa variables set`

Set variables on the linked service or the shared set.

```
miosa variables set KEY=VALUE [KEY=VALUE...] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Alias of --environment.
- `--environment string` - Deploy target (default: the linked one).
- `--service string` - Service (default: the linked one).
- `--shared` - Write the Deploy target's shared variables.
- `--skip-deploys` - Railway compatibility: do not redeploy (never does).

#### `miosa variables unset`

Delete variables from the linked service or the shared set.

```
miosa variables unset NAME [NAME...] [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--deploy-target string` - Alias of --environment.
- `--environment string` - Deploy target (default: the linked one).
- `--service string` - Service (default: the linked one).
- `--shared` - Write the Deploy target's shared variables.

### `miosa version`

Print the CLI version.

```
miosa version
```

### `miosa volume`

Persistent volumes shared between machines.

```
miosa volume
```

A volume outlives any one machine.
It attaches to one machine at a time for
writing (several for reading), and keeps its data when detached.
<volume> is a name or an id.

```
  miosa volume create data --size-gb 5
  miosa volume attach data my-box --mount /data
  miosa volume attachments data
  miosa volume detach data my-box
```

Aliases: `volumes`.

#### `miosa volume attach`

Attach a volume to a machine.

```
miosa volume attach <volume> <machine> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--mount string` - Where to mount it in the machine.
- `--read-only` - Mount read-only (several machines may share it).

#### `miosa volume attachments`

Where a volume is attached.

```
miosa volume attachments <volume> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--all` - Include past attachments.

#### `miosa volume create`

Create a volume.

```
miosa volume create <name> [flags]
```

Creates a volume of the given size on local disk (the only backend).
A volume is formatted when it is first attached.

Flags:

- `--size-gb string` - Size in GB (fractions allowed).
- `--workspace string` - Workspace slug or id (default: the profile's default workspace).

#### `miosa volume detach`

Detach a volume from a machine.

```
miosa volume detach <volume> <machine>
```

#### `miosa volume fork`

Create a new volume from a snapshot.

```
miosa volume fork <snapshot> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--to-new string` - Name of the new volume.

#### `miosa volume get`

Show a volume.

```
miosa volume get <volume>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa volume list`

List volumes.

```
miosa volume list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--deleted` - Include deleted volumes.

#### `miosa volume mount`

Mount a volume in a deployed service.

```
miosa volume mount <volume> <service> [flags]
```

Adds the volume to the service's settings so every replica mounts it at --mount
from its next deploy.
A volume has one writer: a service with a writable volume
runs one replica, and a redeploy stops the previous version first.
Use --read-only
to share it with several replicas. <service> is a name, slug or id.

Flags:

- `--mount string` - Absolute path in the service.
- `--project string` - Project name or id (default: the workspace's Default project).
- `--read-only` - Mount read-only.
- `--workspace string` - Workspace whose project to use (default: the profile's, then the organization's Default workspace).

#### `miosa volume rm`

Delete a volume.

```
miosa volume rm <volume> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa volume snapshot`

Take a snapshot of a volume now.

```
miosa volume snapshot <volume>
```

Copies the volume's backing file on the host that holds it.
A volume attached for writing is copied by an atomic clone, so the image is consistent.
A volume that was never attached has nothing to snapshot.

#### `miosa volume snapshot-rm`

Delete a snapshot.

```
miosa volume snapshot-rm <snapshot> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa volume snapshots`

List a volume's snapshots.

```
miosa volume snapshots <volume>
```

#### `miosa volume unmount`

Stop mounting a volume in a deployed service.

```
miosa volume unmount <volume> <service> [flags]
```

Removes the volume from the service's settings; the volume and its data stay.
Takes effect on the next deploy.

Flags:

- `--project string` - Project name or id (default: the workspace's Default project).
- `--workspace string` - Workspace whose project to use (default: the profile's, then the organization's Default workspace).

### `miosa waf`

Show or change the service's web application firewall.

```
miosa waf <project> <service> [flags]
```

Without flags, shows the current rules.

```
  miosa waf shop API --mode enforce --deny 203.0.113.0/24 --rate 120
  miosa waf shop API --allow 198.51.100.0/24 --country-allow US --country-allow CA
  miosa waf shop API --off

```
--mode is off, observe or enforce.
Country rules only apply when something in
front of the service sets the country header; without it they are skipped, which
