the help says rather than pretending they work. --rate is requests per minute
per source; 0 clears the limit.

Flags:

- `--allow stringArray` - Allowed source: IP or CIDR (ip_allow; repeatable).
- `--attack-mode string` - Attack mode: on or off.
- `--country-allow stringArray` - Allowed country, alpha-2 (repeatable).
- `--country-deny stringArray` - Blocked country, alpha-2 (repeatable).
- `--deny stringArray` - Blocked source: IP or CIDR (ip_deny; repeatable).
- `--deploy-target string` - Deploy target name or id (default: the project's default).
- `--mode string` - Mode: off, observe or enforce.
- `--off` - Remove every WAF rule.
- `--rate int` - Requests per minute per source (0 clears the limit).

### `miosa wait`

Wait until a sandbox reaches a state.

```
miosa wait [sandbox] [flags]
```

Block until the sandbox is ready for commands (default), running, paused or destroyed.
Exits 0 when reached, non-zero on timeout or if the sandbox lands in the error state.

```
  miosa create my-box && miosa wait my-box
  miosa wait my-box --for paused --timeout 5m
```

Flags:

- `--for string` - State to wait for: ready, running, paused, destroyed. (default `ready`)
- `--timeout duration` - Give up after this long (seconds, or a duration like 3m). (default `3m0s`)

### `miosa webhook`

Send events to your own HTTPS endpoints.

```
miosa webhook
```

Webhooks call your URL when something happens (a sandbox becomes ready, a
deployment succeeds).
Each delivery is signed: the x-miosa-signature header is
"sha256=" plus the hex HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body under the webhook's secret.
The secret is returned only when the webhook is created.

```
  miosa webhook events
  miosa webhook create https://example.com/hooks/miosa --event sandbox.ready --event sandbox.error
  miosa webhook test <id>
  miosa webhook deliveries <id>
  miosa webhook replay <id> <delivery-id>
```

Aliases: `webhooks`, `hook`.

#### `miosa webhook create`

Register an endpoint.

```
miosa webhook create <url> [flags]
```

Register an endpoint.
The signing secret is printed once.
Omit --event to receive every event type.

Flags:

- `--description string` - What it is for.
- `--event stringArray` - Event type to subscribe to (repeatable; see 'webhook events').
- `--header stringArray` - Extra header sent with each delivery, NAME=VALUE (repeatable).
- `--name string` - A label.
- `--retries int` - Delivery retries, 0-10.
- `--workspace string` - Limit to one workspace (slug or id).

#### `miosa webhook deliveries`

List recent deliveries and their outcomes.

```
miosa webhook deliveries <id>
```

#### `miosa webhook events`

List the event types you can subscribe to.

```
miosa webhook events
```

#### `miosa webhook get`

Show a webhook.

```
miosa webhook get <id>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa webhook list`

List webhooks.

```
miosa webhook list [flags]
```

Aliases: `ls`.

Flags:

- `--workspace-id string` - Only this workspace.

#### `miosa webhook replay`

Send a past delivery again.

```
miosa webhook replay <id> <delivery>
```

#### `miosa webhook rm`

Delete a webhook.

```
miosa webhook rm <id> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa webhook rotate-secret`

Replace the signing secret and print the new one once.

```
miosa webhook rotate-secret <id> [flags]
```

Replace the webhook's signing secret with a new random one and print it once.
Every delivery after this is signed with the new secret, so update your
endpoint's verification first or accept both while you switch.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa webhook test`

Send a test delivery.

```
miosa webhook test <id>
```

#### `miosa webhook update`

Change a webhook.

```
miosa webhook update <id> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--active` - Enable or disable deliveries.
- `--event stringArray` - Replace the subscribed events (repeatable).
- `--name string` - A label.
- `--retries int` - Delivery retries, 0-10.
- `--url string` - New endpoint.

### `miosa whoami`

Show who this CLI is signed in as: user, organization, plan and access.

```
miosa whoami [flags]
```

Asks the API who the key or session is (GET /whoami), which works for an API key as
well as a browser session.
Servers that predate that route fall back to the
organization record, which has no user, workspace or scopes.

Flags:

- `--check` - Only check the sign-in: exit 0 when it works, 3 when it does not.

### `miosa workflow`

Automations made of steps, agents and tools.

```
miosa workflow
```

<workflow> is a name or id.

Aliases: `workflows`.

#### `miosa workflow get`

Show a workflow.

```
miosa workflow get <workflow>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa workflow list`

List workflows.

```
miosa workflow list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa workflow pause`

Pause triggers.

```
miosa workflow pause <workflow>
```

#### `miosa workflow resume`

Resume triggers.

```
miosa workflow resume <workflow>
```

#### `miosa workflow rm`

Delete a workflow.

```
miosa workflow rm <workflow> [flags]
```

Aliases: `delete`.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Do not ask for confirmation.

#### `miosa workflow run`

Start a run.

```
miosa workflow run <workflow> [flags]
```

Flags:

- `--input string` - Input as JSON.

#### `miosa workflow run-cancel`

Cancel a workflow run.

```
miosa workflow run-cancel <run>
```

#### `miosa workflow run-get`

Show a workflow run.

```
miosa workflow run-get <run>
```

#### `miosa workflow runs`

List runs of all workflows.

```
miosa workflow runs
```

### `miosa workspace`

Manage workspaces.

```
miosa workspace
```

Workspaces group machines, deployments and data inside your organization. <workspace> is a slug, name or id.

#### `miosa workspace activity`

Recent activity in a workspace.

```
miosa workspace activity <workspace>
```

#### `miosa workspace create`

Create a new workspace.

```
miosa workspace create <name>
```

#### `miosa workspace delete`

Delete a workspace (asks you to type its name).

```
miosa workspace delete <workspace> [flags]
```

Deletes the workspace.
On a terminal it shows the workspace and asks you to
type its name to confirm.
Off a terminal (scripts, CI) it refuses unless --yes
is given. <workspace> is its name, slug or id.

Flags:

- `-y, --yes` - Delete without the typed confirmation.

Examples:

```
miosa workspace delete acme
  miosa workspace delete acme --yes
```

#### `miosa workspace get`

Show a workspace.

```
miosa workspace get <workspace>
```

Aliases: `show`.

#### `miosa workspace inventory`

Everything a workspace contains.

```
miosa workspace inventory <workspace>
```

#### `miosa workspace list`

List workspaces.

```
miosa workspace list
```

Aliases: `ls`.

#### `miosa workspace members`

List the people in a workspace.

```
miosa workspace members <workspace>
```

#### `miosa workspace rename`

Rename a workspace.

```
miosa workspace rename <workspace> <new-name> [flags]
```

Rename a workspace; --slug also changes its slug.

```
  miosa workspace rename staging "Staging EU"
  miosa workspace rename staging "Staging EU" --slug staging-eu

```
The slug appears in URLs and, where an organization maps workspaces to another
system by slug, is the key of that mapping: changing it can break the mapping,
so the command warns when the slug really changed.

Flags:

- `--slug string` - New slug.

#### `miosa workspace retire`

Empty a workspace, then delete it (asks you to type its name).

```
miosa workspace retire <workspace> [flags]
```

Retire a workspace in the documented order: list what it holds, delete those
resources in dependency order, then delete the workspace itself.

```
  miosa workspace retire acme --dry-run   # show what would go, change nothing
  miosa workspace retire acme             # asks you to type the name
  miosa workspace retire acme --yes       # no prompt (scripts, CI)

```
On a terminal it asks you to type the workspace name before anything is
deleted.
Off a terminal it refuses unless --yes is given. <workspace> is its
name, slug or id.

Flags:

- `--dry-run` - Show what would be deleted, change nothing.
- `--include-production` - Retire even when the workspace holds production resources.
- `-y, --yes` - Retire without the typed confirmation.

Examples:

```
miosa workspace retire acme --dry-run
  miosa workspace retire acme --yes
```

#### `miosa workspace stats`

Counts of machines, deployments and databases.

```
miosa workspace stats <workspace>
```

#### `miosa workspace transfer`

Move machines to another workspace.

```
miosa workspace transfer <workspace> [flags]
```

Moves sandboxes and computers from <workspace> to the workspace whose id you give with --to.
Name each one with --id (repeatable).
Deployments, databases and buckets cannot be
moved yet; the command only moves what the platform accepts and reports how many moved.

Flags:

- `--id stringArray` - Id of a sandbox or computer to move (repeatable).
- `--to string` - Id of the workspace to move them to (miosa workspace list).

Examples:

```
miosa workspace transfer old --to new --id <sandbox-id> --id <computer-id>
```

#### `miosa workspace update`

Rename a workspace or change its description and labels.

```
miosa workspace update <workspace> [flags]
```

Changes the name, slug or description of a workspace, and sets labels on it
(--meta key=value, repeatable): the entity it belongs to, an owner, tags.
The labels you
give replace the ones it has, so pass all of them.
Machines, deployments and data stay
where they are.

Flags:

- `--description string` - What the workspace is for.
- `--meta stringArray` - Label key=value (repeatable).
- `--name string` - New name.
- `--slug string` - New slug.

Examples:

```
miosa workspace update acme --name 'Acme Inc' --description 'Client delivery'
  miosa workspace update acme --meta entity=lunivate --meta owner=roberto
```

#### `miosa workspace usage`

Spend and runtime in a workspace.

```
miosa workspace usage <workspace>
```

#### `miosa workspace use`

Make a workspace the default for this profile.

```
miosa workspace use <workspace>
```

Stores the workspace slug as default_workspace in the active profile.
The workspace must exist.
